my love for you is brewing

by 3V1LH4G

Summary

Rio owns a flower shop, Agatha owns a cafe across the street. Rio has a huge crush on Agatha.

Notes

I haven’t written anything like this since 2016, so please bear with me lmao. Hope u enjoy <3

Chapter 1: Red roses

In one of the busier streets of Westview, two businesses sat across from each other on a charming cobblestone street: a cozy flower shop, owned by Rio, notoriously kind to all of her clients, and a warm, inviting cafe run by the somewhat distant, sharp-tongued Agatha. Despite their proximity, the two hadn’t shared more than polite greetings and casual conversations whenever Rio spent her work break at the cafe. This was more than most people could say, though, because Agatha was not known for her small talk. In fact, people were often surprised when they found out that the cafe owner could be quite closed off and distant, contrary to the stereotypical chatty baristas. Agatha was a hard-working woman, spending all her time either at the well-established cafe or at home, caring for her 6-year-old son, Nicky. Rio’s shop, however, was one of the more recent additions to the lively street. She had finally found the courage to open her own flower shop, despite having majored in law. She was supposed to follow her father’s footsteps, but quickly realised it wasn’t for her. She enjoyed being among the people of her quaint town, where everyone knew and cared about each other. It was a stark contrast to the big corporate world she worked in for the first few years after her graduation, just long enough to save up for her shop and move back to Westview.

On one particularly cold morning, right after the hectic Christmas days, Rio is working on an order for a regular customer. The bell above the door chimed through the little flower shop as a man stepped inside. She recognised him as Ralph, Agatha’s ex-husband. Rio tried not to roll her eyes when she looked him up and down. He was wearing a tailored navy suit, blonde hair tamed neatly with an awful lot of gel. He always walked around like he owned the place, which was exactly what he was doing in her shop right now. That’s why she was annoyed whenever she saw his face. It had nothing to do with the fact that he used to be married to Agatha. Or that he was the one who used to hold her, touch her and-

No, it had everything to do with the fact that he was a snob. And with the fact that he broke Agatha’s heart when he betrayed her. The heart that she wished she could carefully put back together. Of course, she would never dare to admit that to anyone, though.

As he walked further into the shop, his phone was pressed to his ear, in the middle of a conversation, speaking loudly enough for everyone to hear. He made his way to the register, not even bothering to look at any of the flowers available in the store, and lazily gestured to the red roses on his left.

“I need a dozen of these, ASAP,” he said, only lowering the phone for a second to place his order. He flashed the florist a toothy grin, fully convinced that it made him look charming, but it only highlighted his overconfidence even more.

Without waiting for a response, he turned his attention back to the person on the other end of the line. Rio normally would’ve been nosy enough to listen in on the conversation, but honestly, she couldn’t care less about Ralph and his pretentious job and his exaggeratedly important clients.

The brunette stepped around the counter to collect the roses, and as she bent down to pick them up, she felt eyes on her. She shivered at the thought of being ogled by this arrogant man and quickly stood back up to shake off the feeling. It wasn’t new to her, sure, but she hated it regardless. Especially because he had chosen the most stereotypically romantic flower known to man, obviously planning on giving them to someone. She didn’t bother spending energy on wondering who they were for.

“Actually, can you reschedule my 8 AM meeting?” he said, “If all goes well today, I’ll be spending the morning with my wife and son.”

Wife? Who the hell is he talking about? Surely it can’t be about Agatha.

Rio’s mind started racing and she felt a frown forming as she wondered what Ralph was planning. She knew that he and Agatha had been divorced for a long time, only staying in contact for the rare occasions he made time to see Nicky. She bit the inside of her cheek while she laid the roses on her messy workstation.

Ralph finally ended the call and pocketed his phone, turning his attention to Rio. She quickly tried to hide her confusion and straightened her back, seemingly unfazed by his attitude and earlier conversation. The florist, back to being unimpressed by him, stood behind the counter, her eyebrow quirked and her mouth forming a sarcastic retort that slipped out before she could stop herself.

“A dozen red roses? That’s what you expect will do the trick?”, she deadpanned. “Nothing screams ‘I want to try again, I’ll actually put in the effort this time, please forgive me’ like red roses. Truly, you’ll woo any woman within a 10-mile radius.”

Ralph blinked, momentarily thrown off by the brunette’s bold comment. Not willing to show that his ego took a small hit, he cleared his throat. “Excuse me?”

Rio didn’t miss a beat and continued, twirling a red rose between her fingers. “Oh, nothing. Just saying that red roses are so… classic.” She tilted her head, a smirk tugging at her lips. “Really says, ‘I’m putting in the bare minimum, but somehow I’m confident it’ll work.’”

“Well, classics are classics for a reason. Women love that stuff, so why wouldn’t it work?” he said, regaining his footing. Then, he added with a smirk of his own, “Besides, anything would work as long as it comes from me.” 

The florist had to try her best not to snort a laugh at that. Typical, she thought to herself. Of course he would say that and actually believe it.

“Sure, sure,” Rio said, playing along. If he wasn’t bothered to ask for advice, she wouldn’t be bothered suggesting an alternative. “And nothing says ‘I’m not over you’ quite like throwing  money at flowers and hoping they do the talking for you.”

She shifted her focus back toward the bouquet, her movements deliberate as she snipped at the stems a little too aggressively. Ralph either didn’t notice or chose to ignore it, fiddling with the phone in his hand after impatiently checking the time.

Although Rio was reluctant to help this man any more than necessary, she was still passionate about her craft and made sure that every bouquet that left this store was something that she was proud of. She tied the final red ribbon around the bouquet, adding a carefully designed sticker with her shop’s name to the paper wrapping. She slid the packaged bundle of flowers across the counter with an overly sweet smile. “There you go. A dozen red roses, ready to win over whoever you’re looking to impress.”

The man gave her a tight smile, clearly unsure if she was joking or insulting him more. “Thanks,” he muttered, grabbing the bouquet and leaving a few dollar bills on the counter. “Keep the change,” he said with a wink, and the florist could swear she felt a bit of vomit travelling up her throat as she swallowed heavily. Disgusting.

Upon seeing the man turn back around to leave, Rio let out a breath she didn’t know she was holding. She felt her shoulders relax when the door swung shut behind him. The whole interaction couldn’t have been longer than 15 minutes, but it had surely been an excruciatingly long 15 minutes.

She turned her attention back to her work, but she couldn’t help but wonder how Agatha would react when she received those roses. She was mostly convinced that the cafe owner would laugh in his face, half amused and half exasperated for thinking she would be won over with such a cheesy gift. However, a small, tiny part in the back of her mind, deep in the parts of her brain that had a tendency to whisper anxiety-inducing sentences, she was worried that it would work. After all, it would be so much easier for the blue-eyed woman to have her son’s father back in their life, their endless back-and-forth surely taking its toll on Nicky.

As an attempt to distract herself from such thoughts, Rio started cleaning her workstation, throwing away the leftover stems from the roses. She hissed as one of the thorns prickled her index finger, a small drop of blood rapidly forming on the surface of her skin. She quickly walked over to the sink in the back room to rinse it off and searched through the cupboards for a small bandaid. When she walked back to the front of the shop, she glanced at the clock. It was only 11.30 am, but she decided she deserved to take her break a bit earlier than usual.

Given that she mostly worked alone, the florist preferred to spend her lunch break in the company of other people. The cosy cafe across the street was the perfect place for a quick change of scenery while remaining close to her store in case a customer came. Rio didn’t mind not being able to go far, because the cafe’s coffee was really good. And the owner was really hot. But, she mainly went for the coffee, of course.

With a green marker, the florist scribbled on a piece of paper, ‘At cafe across the street. Back soon,’ adding a little heart at the end. Most of the people in Westview knew that they could find her at the cafe whenever she wasn’t in the store during her opening hours, but she preferred to leave a note on the door regardless.

She grabbed her long, black coat and wrapped a thick fluffy scarf around her neck. Even though the cafe was just across the street, it was incredibly cold and she did not want to risk getting sick. Rio firmly shut the door behind her and made her way to her favourite cafe.

The soft hum of conversation mixed with a faint hiss of the espresso machine as the brunette pushed the door open. The smell of fresh coffee and baked goods hit her like a warm hug, but she barely noticed. Her eyes instantly landed on Agatha behind the counter. She was wearing purple slacks and a white blouse, a few more buttons undone than should be legal, in Rio’s opinion. However, it did leave enough space for Agatha’s signature pendant to be perfectly visible, a locket with three female figures on it.

The cafe owner was mid-rant, her hands animated as she spoke to Lilia, an older woman who enjoyed helping out in the shop whenever Billy, the teenage employee, was at school.

“-and then he just plopped the bouquet on the table like it was some grand romantic gesture. Red roses!” Agatha scoffed, flipping her dark wavy hair over her shoulder. “it’s the floral equivalent of a Hallmark card. Zero thought, zero effort.”

Lilia snickered, placing a freshly brewed coffee on the counter. “At least he’s trying?” She let out another laugh when she finished the sentence, knowing very well that this couldn’t be considered trying.

“Trying? Please,” Agatha huffed, rolling her eyes. “If he really wanted to impress me. he’d know I hate roses. After this many years? Come on. And this won’t magically make up for being a shitty father to Nicky, either.”

“So, what would you have preferred then,” Lilia asked, knowing that the florist was listening to their conversation. The older woman wasn’t oblivious to Rio’s small crush, and she figured it wouldn’t hurt to nudge her in the right direction.

“Bring me tulips or something. He should know purple is my favourite colour, so that would work too. Even chocolate might’ve been better. Honestly, anything but roses.”

Rio took this as her cue to make her presence known and approached the counter, her lips curling into a smirk. “So, red roses aren’t your thing, huh? I tried telling him, I swear.”

Agatha turned, startled, but her expression softened slightly when blue eyes met brown. “Oh, hey, Rio. Didn’t see you come in.”

“Don’t mind me,” Rio said, leaning casually on the counter. “Just here for my usual cup of caffeine and maybe a little free entertainment.” She gestured toward the bouquet of roses sitting awkwardly in front of her. She wouldn’t say this out loud, but she was relieved to see that Agatha hadn’t bothered putting them in a vase yet. “Though I gotta admit, these do seem... uninspired.”

Agatha let out a laugh, brushing her fingers against the counter. The brown-eyed florist had to resist the urge to reach out, wishing the fingers brushed over her own skin instead. Now was not the time. “You’re telling me. It’s like he Googled ‘flowers that scream desperation’ and clicked the first result.”

Rio raised an eyebrow, her grin widening. “Ouch. Sounds like he’s trying to make a grand comeback.”

“There’s no coming back for him,” Agatha muttered, crossing her arms. “I swear, the man thinks roses are some kind of magic wand. Like one bouquet and I’ll forget years of... well, being him.”

Rio chuckled, leaning a little closer. “Well, for the record, I’d never insult you with roses.”

Agatha tilted her head, amused. “Oh, really? And what would you bring me, then?”

Rio faltered, her bravado slipping. “Uh... maybe something unique. Like… orchids. Elegant, but a little mysterious. Or, uh, azaleas, cheerful but complicated. You know, flowers with personality. Like you.” She tried to hide her blush when she finished rambling, but she could feel the warmth rising from her neck to her cheeks and knew it was pointless.

Lilia snorted softly from the side, clearly enjoying the interaction, while Agatha quirked an eyebrow, her lips curving into a teasing smile. “Flowers with personality, huh? Sounds like you’ve given this some thought.”

Rio rubbed the back of her neck, her usual sarcasm failing her as her cheeks flushed. “I mean… it’s kind of my job to think about flowers, you know?” she stammered.

Agatha leaned on the counter, her tone light but her gaze curious. “Well, when you’re done analysing flower personalities, maybe you can help me figure out what kind of coffee suits me, too.”

“Easy,” Rio said, recovering slightly. “You’re an espresso. Bold, classic, straight-to-the-point.” She glanced at Lilia to gauge her reaction before she could feel fully satisfied with her answer. 

“Nothing straight about this one,” Lilia quipped with a smirk. “But yes, I’d agree.” Brown eyes skimmed her face in slight confusion, silently wondering if the grey-haired woman was messing with her or not. Agatha just laughed and waved her off vaguely, but didn’t deny anything.

Agatha turned back to Rio, her voice amused yet soft. “Bold? I’m not sure if I should be flattered or offended.”

The florist shrugged, her grin returning. “Take it as a compliment. I think we could all be a bit bolder sometimes.” It was a silent attempt at encouraging herself to be more bold, too.

Agatha shook her head, her messy waves falling back over her shoulder. “You’re ridiculous.”

“Maybe,” Rio said, her voice quieter but still playful. “But I’d still never bring you roses.”

The moment lingered, their gazes locking for a beat too long before Lilia cleared her throat, breaking the spell. Agatha straightened, turning back to the espresso machine.

“Anyway,” Agatha said, her tone breezy but her cheeks slightly pink, “I should probably get back to work. Your usual?”

Rio nodded, her heart racing. “Yeah. And maybe throw in a cookie. You know, for courage.”

Agatha laughed again, the sound sending a flutter through Rio’s chest. As Agatha turned to prepare her coffee, the florist made a silent vow: no more awkward flirting. Next time, she’d say exactly what she meant. Well, maybe. She hadn’t figured out what to say yet. She also didn’t feel like randomly asking her out would work either. After all, they’d never really given each other any hints that they were interested in being more than… friendly neighbours? Business owners on the same street?

Rio's train of thought was interrupted when Agatha held out a cup with her favourite drink: a cappuccino with just a hint of vanilla syrup. Something that the cafe owner had teased her for endlessly, saying ‘Why even bother asking for the syrup if I’m only supposed to add a tiny drop? Go big or go home, Vidal.’

The florist had to hold back a fond smile as she remembered the memory, feeling happy with the way Agatha and she got along, even if the teasing would turn her into a blushing and stuttering mess.

When she took the cup, and cookie, from the owner, their hands brushed briefly. Rio stepped away from the counter and before she had the chance to feel flustered over the slight touch, she heard Agatha ask, “What happened to your hand?”

“Oh, uh, just a little accident after helping a man win back his wife, no biggie,” the florist joked, tongue pressing the inside of her cheek. Agatha gave her a pointed look, rolling her eyes playfully as a betraying smirk grew on her lips. “What a hero, so glad you could save the day,” she replied, bringing her hand to her forehead and using her other hand to fan herself, a sight straight out of a cheesy movie.

“Anything for you, m’lady,” Rio said with an exaggerated bow before turning around and walking to the door, desperately trying to hide how flushed her face was after finally finding some courage to be more playful around Agatha. Just as she reached for the door handle, her attention was caught once more.

“Hey Vidal?” she heard, forcing her to turn around. She hummed in response, raising an eyebrow in question.

“Careful with those fingers, alright? You’ll still need them.”

Rio didn’t need to hold up a mirror to know that she was flushed beyond repair at this point and the wink that Agatha sent her way was definitely not helping. For once, Rio was happy to step back outside after visiting her favourite cafe, embracing the feeling of the cold air against her hot skin. She breathed in deeply, letting the fresh air fill her lungs before she let out a contented sigh.

Tomorrow, she’d be bold. 

Chapter 2: Friendly advice

Chapter Summary

Their friends try to nudge them in the right direction.

Rio and Nicky spend time together at the shop.

Chapter Notes

For my fluff lovers.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

The next day, Rio entered the familiar warmth of Agatha’s cafe during her break. It had been a hectic morning for her with a big order to fulfil, but she knew that the cafe owner would pick up her son from school not too long from now, and she secretly hoped to catch a glimpse of her before she had to leave. Rio figured the big order would still be there when she got back, deciding it was okay to take a quick coffee break.

The chatter of customers and the soft clatter of coffee mugs welcomed her. She spotted Agatha near the counter, leaning against it while talking to the red-haired woman Rio recognised as Wanda, one of Agatha’s close friends.

“…so there he was, showing up at my door all high and mighty after the rose debacle.” Agatha’s voice carried easily over the ambient noise, and Rio paused just inside the door, curious despite herself. “He started preaching about how we should spend more time as a family. Said it’d be good for Nicky.” Agatha huffed and rolled her eyes.

“And you said?” Wanda prompted before taking a sip of her coffee, her eyebrows raised in anticipation.

“I said, ‘Sure, Ralph, let’s sit down for a nice family dinner where I’ll conveniently forget the last six years of you being not even a part-time dad.’” Agatha rolled her eyes dramatically, a sarcastic edge to her tone. “It’s like he thinks showing up once every couple of months with a bouquet erases everything. Honestly, the nerve. It took everything in me not to tell him to just fuck off and go spend time with the woman he cheated on me with.”

Wanda laughed, shaking her head. “Classic Ralph. He’s persistent, I’ll give him that.”

“Persistent or delusional?” she said with a raised eyebrow. “I swear, if Ralph thinks he can just stroll back into our lives like this…” She trailed off with a sigh, washing her hands and drying them off with a towel. Agatha straightened her back, glancing at the clock mounted on the wall.

“Anyway, I need to pick Nicky up from school in ten minutes.” As Agatha moved toward the back room to grab her coat and bag, she caught Rio’s eye and stopped walking. Her expression softened slightly, a hint of a genuine smile replacing her earlier irritation.

“Hey, Vidal. Usual?”

Rio nodded quickly, trying not to look like she’d just been eavesdropping. “Yeah, thanks. Only if you have time, though.” The last thing Rio wanted to do was make life more difficult for Agatha; she had enough on her plate.

“I’ll get it for you before I head out,” Agatha said, pressing a button on the espresso machine before disappearing into the back.

Once she was gone, Wanda turned to Rio, and the florist couldn’t quite pinpoint what the look on the redhead’s face meant. “You heard all that, huh?”

Rio hesitated, then gave an awkward chuckle. She raked her fingers through her dark hair, flipping it over. “Uh, yeah. Kinda hard to miss.”

“She’s really got a way with words,” Wanda said, smirking.

“Definitely.” Rio fidgeted with the strap of her small leather bag, then lowered her voice. “So, uh… do you think she’ll actually, like, you know… get back together with him?” The question tumbled out clumsily, but the curiosity in her voice was genuine.

Wanda raised an eyebrow, her smirk growing. “You’re wondering if she’ll take Ralph back?”

“Well, yeah,” Rio admitted, her ears burning slightly. “I mean, for Nicky’s sake or whatever.” The florist mentally cursed at herself for letting her curiosity get the best of her.  This was none of her business, right?

“Nah, not a chance,” Wanda said firmly, crossing her arms. “Agatha would do anything for her kid, but she knows very well that Ralph isn’t what he needs. It’s also not what she needs.”

Rio felt a strange mix of relief and embarrassment flood her chest. “Good to know,” she murmured shyly while fidgeting with her hands, hoping Wanda didn’t notice how much she cared about the answer.

Wanda, however, was far more perceptive than Rio gave her credit for. She spent a lot of her time at the cafe and had often seen the small yet entertaining interactions between the two shop owners. Her eyes twinkled mischievously and she leaned forward slightly before asking, “You’ve got a thing for her, don’t you?”

“What?” Rio’s voice cracked slightly, and she scrambled to wave her hands dismissively. Her eyes went wide as she looked for words to deny this claim. “No, I mean, it’s not like that, I just-”

“Hey, relax, I’m not judging,” Wanda said, laughing. “In fact…” She leaned in conspiratorially. “You might be just what she needs.”

“What does that mean?” Rio asked, blinking. How could Wanda even know about her silly crush? She hadn’t exactly been obvious about it, because she never dared to make a move. She had heard that Agatha was also into women, but she never once assumed she could be into her.

Before Wanda could elaborate, Agatha returned, wearing her coat and holding her bag and car keys. She quickly finished preparing Rio’s drink and placed it directly in front of her with a warm smile. Then the cafe owner turned back to her friend.

“Wanda, are you still good to watch Nicky for a bit when we get back? I have to meet with one of my suppliers, remember?”

“Actually…” Wanda began, her smile turning sly as she glanced at Rio. “That’s what I came over to tell you before you started ranting about your ex-husband. I can’t look after him today, something came up.” It was a lie, but Agatha didn’t need to know that. Before the cafe owner could ask what exactly had come up so last-minute, Wanda provided her with a solution.

“Why don’t you ask Rio here? I bet she wouldn’t mind having Nicky over at the flower shop for a little bit.”

Agatha blinked, looking between Wanda and Rio with a mix of surprise and gratitude. “Oh, I couldn’t ask you to do that.”

Rio, still reeling from the sudden turn of events, stammered, “Yeah, uh, of course, I wouldn’t mind! If you need help, I mean. Nicky’s a great kid.” She smiled at the cafe owner to reassure her that it was not a problem.

Agatha returned the smile warmly, her hesitation softening. “You sure you’re okay with that? It’s just for a bit. I’d hate to impose.”

“Not at all,” Rio said quickly, trying not to trip over her words. “I’d love to help.” Another big grin was visible on her face.

“Thanks, I owe you one.” Agatha shot her a grateful smile before hurrying out the door.

As soon as Agatha was gone, Wanda turned back to Rio with a victorious grin. “You’re welcome.”

“For what?” Rio asked, narrowing her eyes.

“Getting you a little more bonding time with your favourite cafe owner and her adorable son.” Wanda winked. “Just don’t blow it, okay?”

Rio sighed, her cheeks burning as she tried not to imagine what Agatha would think of her friend’s meddling. If Wanda was on Rio’s side, it couldn’t be bad, right? At least, that’s what the florist told herself when she walked back to her shop, replaying the interaction in her head over and over.

———————

It didn’t take long before the gentle bell chimed through the shop as Agatha stepped inside, Nicky’s small hand in hers. His brown eyes sparkled as he took in the vibrant array of flowers lining the walls. There were buckets filled with all kinds of variants, ranging from big to small in all colours. Agatha looked around, spotting Rio arranging a big and seemingly heavy bouquet near the counter. She had her hair tied up in a ponytail and was wearing an apron over a simple white t-shirt, muscles flexing visibly as she lifted the bundled flowers to move them aside. Agatha’s staring was interrupted when Nicky pulled on her arm, excitedly bouncing and dragging her toward the counter.

“Thanks again for this, Rio,” Agatha said, the weight of her long day clear in her voice. “You’re seriously a lifesaver.”  The florist never grew tired of hearing her name coming from the cafe owner’s mouth.

“Don’t worry, Agatha. It’s no big deal,” she replied, flashing her a reassuring smile. “Hi, Nicky!” She waved at him excitedly, making sure to match his energy.

“Hi, Rio!” Nicky chirped, breaking away from his mother to close the last distance and fully run over toward Rio. “Mama said I get to spend some time here today! Can you show me all the flowers? I wanna learn everything!” His excitement was abundantly clear, the little boy jumping up and down while pointing at all the flowers around him. Instead of thinking that his energy was too much, Rio found it adorable and felt her heart swell a little.

“Everything, huh?” Rio knelt to his level, her smile widening. “That’s a tall order, but I think we’ll manage.”

Nicky immediately pointed to a cluster of violets on a nearby shelf. “What about these? They’re purple! That’s Mama’s favourite colour.”

“Those are violets, buddy,” Rio answered as she glanced over at Agatha, who was unsuccessfully trying to suppress a grin. “Purple’s a great choice,” Rio said, nodding in approval. She gestured toward a small bucket with different purple flowers, making Nicky walk over to inspect them from up close. “These are hellebores, sometimes called winter roses. They’re special because they bloom even when it’s cold and snowy, bringing a little colour and life when it’s needed the most.”

“Christmas flowers,” Nicky said with a toothy grin, as if renaming them on the spot.

“Exactly,” Rio said with a gentle laugh. Agatha watched the interaction fondly and felt herself relax, realising her son was in good hands with the florist. She said her goodbyes and told Nicky she would pick him up later. The 6-year-old didn’t seem too worried about getting picked up or not, his tiny body filled with thrill as he browsed through the shop, asking questions about everything that popped up in his head. Rio gave her one last reassuring nod before turning her attention back to Nicky, and Agatha took that as her cue to leave.

The florist led him around the shop, patiently explaining the names and stories behind the flowers. Nicky soaked it all in, asking a million questions, and Rio answered every single one with the same calm enthusiasm.

At one point, as they crouched by a tray of snapdragons, Nicky spoke up. “These look so cool!” his eyes were wide with excitement. “Do you think they would make Mama smile, too? She seemed sad after my dad came over last night,” he said matter-of-factly, fiddling with a stray petal.

Rio paused, glancing at him carefully. “Yeah? How’d that go?”

Nicky shrugged. “He didn’t stay long. I don’t like it when he comes over. He doesn’t play with me or anything. I never get to show him my new toys. Even when I go to his house, he just talks on the phone a lot. It’s boring.”

Rio’s heart ached at the child’s blunt honesty. “That sucks, buddy,” she said softly. “But you know what? If you ever want to hang out here, I’ve got plenty of fun stuff we can do. You can even bring some of your toys, I’d love to see them. And you can help me with the flowers anytime you like.”

“Really?” Nicky’s face lit up like a lightbulb. “That’d be so cool!”

“Really,” Rio promised, ruffling his hair. “Now, how about we start with these snapdragons? They’re called that because you can make their petals look like dragon mouths. Wanna try?”

“Yeah!”

The next hour passed in a blur of laughter. By the time Agatha returned to the flower shop to pick Nicky up, she found him standing on a stool at the workbench, carefully snipping flower stems with Rio’s guidance. His tiny hands worked diligently as Rio handed him ribbons and showed him how to tie them into bows.

“Mama!” Nicky exclaimed, hopping down from the stool as soon as he saw her. “Look what I made!” He held up a small but colourful bouquet, grinning from ear to ear. “Rio taught me how to do it!”

“That’s beautiful, sweetheart,” Agatha said, her eyes softening as she crouched to his level. “You did such a good job.”

“Please, can I make some for you?” Nicky asked eagerly, bouncing up and down while clapping his little hands. Those big brown eyes were really hard to say no to, but Agatha felt guilty about keeping Rio from her work more than she already had.

Agatha chuckled. “Let’s not take up all of Rio’s time, kiddo. I’m sure she’s got a lot of things to do.”

“It’s fine, I don’t mind,” Rio interjected, smiling at Agatha. “He’s a natural, and I had a lot of fun.” After receiving another gentle smile, Agatha gave the two some space so they could get to work. She started mindlessly browsing through the flowers and looked out the window briefly, seeing people walk in and out of her cafe across the street.

Nicky’s small hands hovered over the assortment of flowers spread across the worktable. “I think she’ll like these,” he said, pointing to a cluster of purple hellebores that he had learned about earlier. “And maybe those green ones to make it look bigger?”

Rio smiled at his careful choices. “Good eye, Nicky. These are eucalyptus leaves. They’ll make the bouquet smell nice, too.” She gently handed him a stem, showing him how to place it around the flowers to create balance. “Remember what I told you before? Start with the bigger ones in the centre and build out from there.”

Nicky’s tongue peeked out in concentration as he arranged the stems. “Do you think she’ll like it?” he asked earnestly in a quiet whisper, careful not to let his mother hear them. Even though she was in the shop, he wanted to surprise her with his choices.

Rio crouched down to his level, her voice soft. “She’ll love it. You’re putting so much thought into it. That’s what makes it special.”

Agatha stood nearby, leaning against one of the tables covered with flower buckets. Her arms were crossed, but her expression was far from detached. Her gaze softened as she watched the two work together, their heads close as Rio patiently guided Nicky’s hands. There was an ease and warmth between them that tugged at something deep in her chest.

Ralph had always been stiff and uninterested when he tried to spend time with Nicky. Moments with him felt like ticking off a box. Nicky had never been this eager to spend time with his dad. But this? This was different. Rio wasn’t just humouring Nicky. No, she was fully present, her gentle encouragement lighting up her son’s face in a way Agatha rarely saw.

“Okay,” Rio said, standing up and holding the bouquet at eye level for Nicky’s inspection. “What do you think? Enough purple?”

Nicky nodded, beaming. “Yeah! It’s perfect!”

Rio grinned. “All right, let’s tie it all together. You can pick the ribbon. What colour do you think your mom would like?” She pointed to her extensive collection of ribbons, waiting patiently while the boy considered all the options.

“Hmm,” Nicky pondered, before settling on a soft lavender ribbon hanging from a nearby spool. “That one!”

“Great choice, Nicky,” she said gently, an encouraging smile visible on her lips. Rio helped him wrap the ribbon around the stems and guided his small hands to tie a bow, her own hands steadying his. When they were finished, Nicky held up the bouquet proudly, turning to Agatha.

“Look, Mama!” he exclaimed. “I made this for you!”

Agatha pushed off the table and stepped closer, her heart swelling as she took in the mix of purple blooms and delicate greenery. “It’s beautiful, sweetheart. You did such a great job.” She bent down to kiss the top of his head. Her eyes flicked to Rio, a soft smile playing on her lips. “And you had an excellent teacher.”

Rio ducked her head, a faint blush creeping into her cheeks. “He did most of the work,” she said modestly.

As Nicky darted off to admire the bouquet in a nearby mirror, Agatha found herself appreciating not just the flowers but the florist who had brought this genuine joy to her son.

Agatha stood up again, her gaze lingering on Rio for a moment longer than intended. “You’re amazing with him,” she said, her tone warm. “If you ever get tired of flowers, you’ve got a real talent for wrangling 6-year-olds.”

Rio smirked, brushing her hands on her apron. “I’ll keep that in mind for my backup career.”

“Seriously, though,” Agatha added, her voice softening just slightly as she stepped closer to the florist, “thanks for everything. You’re kind of wonderful, you know that?”

The faintest blush crept into Rio’s cheeks, but she held her composure. “Well, it’s easy when the company’s good.” She lamely pointed at the little boy, who had returned and was now smiling widely at her.

Agatha tilted her head, a small, amused smile playing at her lips. “Careful, Vidal. Keep talking like that, and we might start hanging around here more often.”

Rio grinned. “I wouldn’t complain.” She didn’t know if Agatha had meant to say ‘we’, but she sure wouldn’t mind having either of them in her shop on a more regular basis.

Agatha laughed, shaking her head. “Come on, Nicky. Let’s go home and put these beautiful flowers in a vase.”

As they left, Rio watched them go, a quiet warmth settling in her chest. Maybe today had been just another day in the shop, but it felt like the start of something more.

———

Later that evening, Rio and her friends found themselves in one of the few bars Westview had to offer. The bar was a haven from the biting cold outside, its warm amber lighting and soft hum of chatter mixed with the background music creating a cozy atmosphere. Rio sat at a small corner table with her friends Jennifer and Alice, a half-empty bottle of beer in her hand. Alice was animatedly recounting a funny story from her workday while Jen, her girlfriend, just listened and laughed along fondly.

The laughter was interrupted when the door opened, letting in a burst of icy air. Rio glanced toward the entrance and froze. Agatha and Wanda stepped inside, unwinding their scarves and dusting the snow from their coats.

“Oh my god,” Jen muttered under her breath, noticing Rio’s sudden rigidity. “You’re blushing already, and they haven’t even looked over here yet. You’re fucked.”

“I am not!” Rio whispered, her face betraying her as it flushed a deeper shade of red.

Alice smirked. “We can’t blame her, babe. She’s gorgeous,” she said to her girlfriend. Then she looked back at Rio’s flushed face and said with a wink, “You’ve got excellent taste.”

“Shut up,” Rio hissed, trying to focus on her drink.

But her resolve crumbled when Agatha caught sight of their table and smiled. She nudged Wanda, and the two walked over.

“Hey, Rio,” Agatha said warmly. “Three times in one day? Lucky me, huh?”

Rio managed a smile, her pulse quickening. “Uh, yeah,” was all she could reply to that. Jen and Alice shared a look, knowing how awkward their best friend could get. “Hi, Wanda.”

“Hey,” Wanda greeted with a grin. “We finally got a girls’ night out. Billy and Tommy are watching Nicky for us. Figured we’d make the most of it.”

Jen leaned forward, her mischievous grin widening. “You should join us! We’ve got plenty of room.”

Agatha hesitated, glancing at Wanda. “That’s very sweet, but we don’t want to intrude on your evening. Besides, I’m sure Rio’s seen enough of me for one day.” Rio tried to hide her blush at the thoughts that were playing in her head. Before she could say she would love to spend more time with Agatha, their friends had already continued the conversation.

“It’s no problem-” Alice began, but Wanda gently declined.

“Another time, maybe,” Wanda said with a polite smile after seeing the hesitation on her friend’s face. She’d question her about it later. “Thanks for the offer, though.”

As they walked to a table on the other side of the bar, Rio couldn’t help but feel a pang of disappointment. Did she not want to hang out with her outside of their workplaces? Or maybe she wanted to talk to Wanda about her situation with Ralph. The idea made her nervous; what if everyone had been wrong? What if she did want to get back together with him?

She tried to focus on her friends, but her eyes kept drifting to Agatha, who looked effortlessly stunning in a deep purple sweater and dark jeans. She was animatedly talking, hands waving in the air as she was saying something that Rio could not overhear due to the noise.

“She’s got you good, doesn’t she?” Jen teased, catching Rio’s distracted gaze.

Rio sighed, resting her chin in her hand. “Yeah. I mean, she’s beautiful, but it’s more than that. When you get to know her, she’s very nice.” Her friends hadn’t exactly seen that side of Agatha, as she was more reserved around people she didn’t know yet. Whenever they visited her cafe, their interactions with the cafe owner were brief but not unkind.

“And she’s such a good mom. Spending time with her and Nicky today… it just felt different, you know?”

Alice reached across the table to give Rio’s hand a reassuring squeeze. “It sounds like you’re already worming your way into their life. Maybe it’s time to grow some balls and ask her out.” Jen quickly joked that Agatha probably wouldn’t be into those balls.

Rio’s cheeks burned. “I can’t do that. What if she doesn’t feel the same way? What if I ruin what we have now?”

Jen rolled her eyes, nudging Rio’s shoulder. “She’s been sneaking looks at you all evening, just like you’ve been sneaking looks at her. Trust me, there’s something there.”

“Besides, she doesn’t have my coffee order memorised, even if I’ve been to the cafe countless times.” Rio blushed at that but tried to cover it up by swatting at her friend’s elbow.

“Yeah, true that,” Alice agreed with a laugh. She gently placed her hand over Rio’s on the table. “And you’re amazing, Rio. She’d be lucky to have you.”

Rio smiled faintly, but her nerves weren’t so easily soothed. “Yeah…” She started playing every scenario in her head, ranging from ones where Agatha said yes, to where she was left rejected and completely heartbroken.

As the conversation shifted, Rio’s gaze inevitably drifted back to Agatha. Each time blue eyes met brown, Agatha offered a shy smile that sent Rio’s heart fluttering. Maybe her friends were right. But for now, she was content to simply enjoy the quiet thrill of possibility.

Chapter End Notes

Thanks for your kind comments, it's been so fun to write again :)
Let me know if you'd like to see more!

Chapter 3: Art with meaning

Chapter Summary

Nicky makes a drawing for Rio, somehow this ends up with Agatha inviting Rio over.

Chapter Notes

I wanted to show Agatha's POV from last chapter too, so a bit repetitive in the beginning - sorry for that :)
Here's a bit of a longer chapter to make up for it.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

(Bar scene chapter 2 - Agatha’s POV)

Agatha opened the door for Wanda before following her inside. She welcomed the warmth as it enveloped her instantly, the soft hum of voices and clinking glasses blending with the lively background music. Agatha began loosening her scarf when her gaze swept the room and froze. There, in the corner, sat Rio. She was surrounded by two women, and her dark, loose curls caught the light as she laughed at something one of them said.

“Oh no,” Agatha muttered under her breath, feeling a sudden wave of nervousness. She didn’t know whether to be excited or bolt out the door.

“What?” Wanda asked, glancing at her.

“Nothing,” Agatha said quickly, averting her gaze and fiddling with her scarf. She tried to compose herself as they made their way further in, but her heart wasn’t cooperating.

When Rio noticed them, her warm brown eyes lit up. Agatha’s chest tightened. She couldn’t pretend she didn’t see them. That would be rude. For some reason, Agatha had suddenly cared about not coming across as rude. Weird, that wasn’t something she usually worried about too much. She went to nudge Wanda to steer them toward Rio’s table despite the fluttering nerves in her stomach. She didn’t need to, though, Wanda was already turning toward the florist’s table to approach them.

“Hey, Rio,” Agatha said, trying to sound casual as they approached. “Three times in one day? Lucky me, huh?”

Rio smiled, and Agatha swore her pulse skipped. “Uh, yeah,” was all Rio said, her usual confidence making room for a more bashful composure. Agatha tried not to pay attention to the way the florist’s cheeks seemed to grow hotter. That must be the alcohol. Totally unrelated.

Wanda greeted Rio and her friends warmly, and all was well until Jen offered them seats at the table. Agatha hesitated, the idea tempting but terrifying all at once. There was a loud voice in her head telling her that sitting down would be the worst idea ever. Warning signs and loud sirens suddenly invaded her brain. As usual, she listened to her anxiety.

“That’s very sweet, but we don’t want to intrude on your evening,” Agatha said quickly, trying to ignore how Wanda’s brows rose in silent question. She forced a smile, adding, “Besides, I’m sure Rio’s seen enough of me for one day.”

To her relief, and disappointment, the group didn’t press further. Wanda followed her lead as they found a table elsewhere in the bar. Agatha told herself there was no particular reason why she chose the seat that was facing Rio’s table. That was purely a coincidence. And if Wanda thought anything of it, she didn’t comment on it.

As Agatha slid into her seat, she felt Wanda’s eyes on her, scrutinising her. “Okay,” Wanda said finally, leaning on the table. “What was that about?”

“What was what about?” Agatha replied, feigning nonchalance as she fiddled with the napkin on the table.

“You practically sprinted away from that table,” Wanda said with a smirk. “Do you have something against her friends, or are you avoiding Rio specifically?”

Agatha shot her a warning glance. She loved her best friend but hated how well she was able to read her. Especially when she was not in the mood to elaborate. “I’m not avoiding anyone. Like I said, I just didn’t want to bother them.”

“Right,” Wanda said dryly, swirling the glass of water that the waiter had delivered as soon as they sat down. “So, what? You’re just going to stare at her from afar all night? Instead of talking to her like a normal person?” Wanda had her suspicions about her friend being interested in the florist, so she wanted to see how far she could push Agatha.

Agatha felt her cheeks heat but didn’t answer. Instead, she focused on the menu, though the words blurred as her mind betrayed her, drifting back to Rio’s easy laugh and the way her curtain bangs framed her face. It was a different look on her compared to earlier today and Agatha decided she appreciated both equally.

Wanda didn’t let up. “Okay, spill. Why didn’t you want to sit with them? And don’t say you were worried about intruding, because since when have you ever cared about that? I know you better than that. If anything, you just dislike being around people.”

Agatha sighed, setting the drinks menu down. She lowered her voice and avoided Wanda’s eyes as she spoke. “Seeing her this much is just.. a lot,” she decided to say.

There was confusion written all over her friend’s face. This wasn’t what she expected. “What do you mean?”

“I don’t know, she just- she always walks in all energetic and excited and nice and-“

Wanda’s expression softened when she realised what was happening. She pursed her lips and didn’t interrupt yet, wanting to let her friend come to the conclusion on her own.

“and she’s always so thoughtful, it makes me feel bad.” Honestly, the words weren’t making sense to Agatha as she said them. She knew she was just making up excuses, eyeing her red-haired friend closely to see if she was finally satisfied with the answer.

“And all of that’s an issue because..?” Wanda pressed further. Okay, so not satisfied then. Agatha took a deep breath as she continued. “Because I’m scared, okay? Happy now?”

Wanda looked at her with a gentle smile on her face, as if to encourage her to go on. “Scared of what?”

Agatha took a deep breath, willing her hands to stop fidgeting. “Of getting attached, I guess. I’ve been down this road before. Even with Ralph, deep down I always had my doubts and reservations — as you know — but he convinced me that we could have something stable, solid, and then it fell apart. I’m not doing that again. It’s better to keep people at arm’s length. Safer.” She resisted the urge to grab at the napkin again and fiddled with her necklace instead.

Wanda’s brow furrowed, but she stayed quiet, letting Agatha continue.

“And even if I wasn’t scared,” Agatha added, glancing toward Rio’s table, “it’s not like she feels the same way. She’s just… nice. She’s like that with everyone.”

Wanda arched a brow, unconvinced. “Agatha, come on. You think she doesn’t know any other cafes in town or babysits random kids at her workplace out of kindness? You’re not just anyone to her.”

“She’s naturally thoughtful,” Agatha insisted. “I guess she just considers me a friend or something. It doesn’t mean anything. And even if it did…”

“Even if it did?” Wanda prompted gently.

Agatha sighed again, staring down at her drink. “I don’t know. I just… I don’t think I could handle getting hurt again. And Nicky… He really likes her, he couldn’t stop talking about spending time with her. I wouldn’t want anything to ruin that. I don’t want him to lose someone else.”

Wanda reached across the table, her hand covering Agatha’s briefly. “You’re allowed to take your time, you know. But don’t sell yourself short. I see the way Rio looks at you, and I don’t think it’s just ‘nice.’ There’s something more, I can feel it.”

Agatha swallowed hard, unsure of what to say. Instead, she glanced back at Rio’s table. The younger woman was laughing at something her friends had said, her smile lighting up her whole face. Agatha couldn’t help but smile faintly herself, her heart betraying her resolve.

When Rio’s gaze met hers again, it lingered a moment longer than before. Agatha gave a shy smile in return but quickly looked away. She couldn’t let her guard down too much, not yet.

Wanda figured she had pushed her friend enough for now and decided to end it on a lighter note before changing the subject. “It wouldn’t hurt to at least explore this friendship more. I can’t be the only one you hang out with, ya know, I also have kids and a husband to take care of,” she said with an exaggerated wink.

Of course, she didn’t mind being the person that the cafe owner hung out with the most, but she also felt she deserved to break down her walls a little. Wanda had a feeling that Rio could be the right person to do so.

Agatha laughed at her in response and said she would think about it. And she did think about it, a lot. When she got home later that evening, she played different scenarios in her head. Maybe Wanda was right; it wouldn’t hurt to gain another friend, right? And if said friend was the beautiful woman from the flower shop across the street, that happened to get along great with her son, that also couldn’t hurt, right?

-----

The next day, Agatha was working at the cafe with Lilia. It was a relatively quiet day, but she didn’t mind it. It gave her time and space to think. She let her mind replay the most recent interactions she’d had with Rio. For some reason, they had felt different than all the other times she’d seen the florist. She couldn’t exactly pinpoint why, but she was hoping to spend more time with her somehow. Thinking about her made her realise that the brown-eyed woman had not visited the cafe that day. Usually, she would’ve swung by for a coffee by now. Agatha wondered why she hadn’t come, and wondered if it had to do with her. Maybe she decided to go to a different cafe today, looking for a change. The idea upset her more than she cared to admit, and she felt her face form into a frown. 

She must’ve looked deep in thought because the older woman nudged her gently to grab her attention. “Honey, are you okay?” she asked.

She looked up from the coffee cup she was fidgeting with and met gentle eyes. “Yeah, just.. thinking,” she replied.

“About?” Lilia pressed further, ready to offer advice if Agatha let her. However, the cafe owner just shrugged dismissively, not wanting to answer. “Nothing, it’s just, more quiet than usual..”

This answer should have been too vague for Lilia to understand. And it would have been, had she not noticed that Agatha kept glancing at the door, as if she was waiting for someone to come in. The glances out the window, toward the shop across the street, also didn’t go unnoticed. Lilia was a smart woman and she knew Agatha well enough to understand what was going on, even if she didn’t want to admit it.

She also knew the younger woman well enough to understand that now was not the time to push her. Instead, she just replied, “Why don’t you take the opportunity to leave a bit earlier today, so you can go home before you pick up Nicky from school?”

Feeling grateful that Lilia decided not to pry, Agatha gave her an appreciative smile and nodded. She grabbed her belongings from the backroom and pretended to be annoyed when the older woman gave her a warm hug before she could leave. Agatha didn’t seem like a very affectionate person, apart from with Nicky, but that was only because she had forcibly put her guard up to protect herself. In reality, she appreciated Lilia more than the older woman could ever know.

-----

Agatha stepped into the brightly lit hallway of the school, her boots clicking softly on the polished floor. She always wondered why the lighting had to be so white and harsh, this wasn’t a hospital. Would it hurt to have more warm lighting in this place? she thought to herself as her eyes readjusted. A cluster of parents was already gathered near the classroom doors, waiting for their children. She joined them, folding her arms against the chill that still clung to her even from the short walk from the car to the entrance.

When the door finally opened, a wave of excited chatter spilled out along with the kids. Nicky was among the first to rush out, his face lighting up when he saw her. Agatha could never grow tired of seeing how excited he got when she picked him up. In his hands, he held a piece of paper with bold, colourful scribbles on it.

“Mama! Mama! Look!” Nicky exclaimed, thrusting the paper toward her with a beaming smile.

Agatha crouched down, taking the drawing carefully. “Wow, what’s this, sweetheart?”

“It’s flowers!” Nicky said proudly. “Mrs. Davis let us draw them today because I told everyone about what I learned at Rio’s shop!”

Behind him, Sharon Davis, his teacher, approached with a warm smile. She was a petite older woman with kind eyes and a gentle demeanour that had made Agatha immediately comfortable during their first meeting.

“Nicky’s been quite the little botanist this week,” Sharon laughed. “He was so enthusiastic about sharing what he learned, we made it part of our art lesson today.”

“Wow, Nicky,” Agatha said, genuinely impressed as she admired the drawing. Bright purples and greens sprawled across the page, vaguely resembling the bouquet he’d made with Rio. The idea of it made her heart swell. “You did such a great job! This is beautiful.”

Nicky grinned from ear to ear, rocking back on his heels. “Do you think Rio will like it? I wanna give it to her next time we see her.”

Agatha’s heart warmed at his excitement, though the mention of Rio brought a slight blush to her cheeks. Instead of taking offence that he decided to give his artwork to someone else, she felt a spark of hope in her chest. “I’m sure she’d love it, kiddo.”

Nicky’s eyes sparkled as he asked, “When can we see her again?”

Agatha hesitated, brushing her long wavy hair behind her ear. “I’m not sure yet, honey. We’ll see.” She hadn’t seen Rio at the cafe that day, which was unusual for her. As far as she knew, the shop across the street had been open, but maybe someone had covered for her.

“But… can we go today?” Nicky asked, practically bouncing on the balls of his feet. “Please? I really want to give it to her!”

Agatha sighed softly, but the look in his big brown eyes made it impossible to say no. Big brown eyes had always been her weakness. Between her son’s and her second favourite pair of brown eyes, she knew she didn’t stand a chance. “Alright, we’ll stop by her shop on our way home. But just for a little while, okay?”

“Yes!” Nicky cheered, nearly leaping with excitement.

As they walked to the car, Nicky eagerly chattered about his day, his words tumbling over each other in his enthusiasm. Agatha listened attentively, asking questions and laughing along with him.

“And Mrs. Davis said my flowers were so good, she wanted to hang it up in the classroom,” Nicky said, swinging his small backpack as they reached the car.

“That’s wonderful, honey, I’m proud of you,” Agatha replied as she helped him buckle into his car seat. She couldn’t help but smile at his infectious energy.

“Yes! But I told her I wanted to give it to Rio instead. Mrs Davis didn’t mind,” he said with a toothy grin. “You don’t mind either, right Mama? I can make you one at home, too!”

Agatha kissed his forehead and ruffled his hair affectionately. “Oh baby, of course I don’t mind. Rio will love it, I’m sure.” Seeing how thoughtful her son was, she tried not to get emotional. Maybe she was doing something right, after all.

Driving toward the flower shop, Agatha stole a glance at him in the rearview mirror. His face was alight with joy, his little hands clutching the drawing he was so proud of, careful not to wrinkle the edges too much. She felt a swell of gratitude in her chest—despite everything, she’d been blessed with such a kind and curious child.

As they neared their destination, Nicky leaned forward as far as his seatbelt would allow. “Do you think Rio will still be there?”

“I think so,” Agatha replied with a chuckle. Then she remembered that she hadn’t seen her yet. To protect her son from potential disappointment, she added, “But even if she isn’t, we can leave the drawing for her. She’ll love it either way.”

Nicky beamed, and Agatha couldn’t help but feel a pang of nervous anticipation herself. She hoped that Rio would be there, but she also didn’t want to think about what it meant if she was. Why hadn’t she visited the cafe today?

Agatha tried to stay positive. She wasn’t sure why seeing Rio again made her heart flutter, but she brushed the thought aside as they pulled into the parking lot. For now, she focused on Nicky’s excitement and the happiness that came from simply spending time with her son.

When she unbuckled Nicky’s seatbelt, he practically jumped out of the car and rushed past Agatha to open the door. The door was just slightly too heavy for him to open on his own, especially with his drawing carefully held in one hand, so Agatha quickly locked the car and went over to help him. She felt nervousness wash over her again, scared to look up and see if Rio was there or not.

However, she didn’t need to look up to know her answer. As soon as the door was open far enough for Nicky to squeeze past it, she heard him squeal, “Rio! Rio! Look what I made!”

He almost tripped over his own feet as he ran to the counter, where Rio’s head was only slightly poking out from behind a collection of beautifully arranged bouquets. She stepped away from her workstation to properly look at the child who was running toward her at full speed. To her surprise, he opened his arms wide and went in for a hug. She noticed too late and was unable to meet him at his height before he reached her, leaving them in a slightly awkward pose, but she hugged him back tightly regardless. The florist looked up at his mom briefly, smiling shyly at her, before she crouched down to Nicky’s level.

He took a small step back and held the drawing out in front of Rio, too closely for her to actually see what was on the paper. She gently took it from his hands and held it out to the right, pulling Nicky into her left side with her arm. “Wow, is this.. what I think it is?” she asked with a huge grin on her face. “The hellebores and eucalyptus? You even got the colours right!”

She might not deal with scratchy drawings on a daily basis, and this surely wasn’t the most realistic drawing of flowers she had ever seen, but she recognised the combinations of purples and greens well enough to know what he had meant to draw.

Nicky nodded frantically and pointed at the purple scribbles on the paper, “Yes, look! It’s the flowers we made for Mama yesterday!”

“I can see that buddy, they look beautiful,” her smile turned more gentle and there was a twinkle in her eyes. “I’m glad you remembered what I taught you.”

Nicky beamed widely at the compliment and continued with the same enthusiasm, “I told my friends at school how cool flowers can be, and Mrs. Davis let us draw them in class!”

“Well, your Mama must be so proud.”

As if on cue, Agatha stepped into their little bubble and ruffled her son’s hair. “Proud? Absolutely. But maybe a little too aware of the difference between hellebores and buttercups now. I guess I’ll have you to thank for that, Vidal,” she teased, sending a wink Rio’s way.

The florist laughed, a hint of shyness creeping into her expression as she stood up straight. “Sounds like I might’ve created a flower enthusiast.”

“Or a florist in training,” Agatha added warmly, catching the slight blush on Rio’s neck and cheeks.

Nicky spoke up again, tugging at Rio’s attention, and pointed at the drawing. “I wanted you to have this one. It can remind you of when we made flowers together!”

Rio’s chest tightened, unexpected emotion washing over her. She looked at the vibrant drawing again, running her thumb lightly over the paper. “Oh Nicky, this is… This is so sweet. Thank you.”

She felt her heart swell at the idea that their day together meant as much to him as it did to her. Her voice was soft, almost trembling, and she cleared her throat quickly to cover it up. “I’ll find the perfect spot for this, I promise.”

“Mama keeps mine on the fridge,” Nicky suggested with a toothy grin, clearly satisfied, and turned to inspect the flowers he had learned about the other day.

Agatha lingered, shifting her weight as she tried to muster the courage to ask the question that had been nagging at her all day. Rio looked at her expectantly. When she finally spoke, her tone was casual. “You didn’t come by the cafe today.”

The florist’s expression briefly startled before she broke into a sheepish smile. “Oh, yeah… I’ve been swamped,” she said, motioning toward the workbench behind them. Several large, finished and half-finished bouquets rested there, a small testament to her busy day. “Big last-minute order for the wedding venue in town. I didn’t even have time to grab coffee. Believe me, I needed it.”

Agatha felt a big sense of relief wash over her at Rio’s explanation. She hadn’t avoided her after all. Once she felt reassured that it had nothing to do with her, Agatha’s relief shifted into concern. “Have you eaten anything today?”

The brown-eyed woman scratched the back of her neck, seemingly hesitant to answer. “Uh, not really? Kinda just forgot, I guess. I don’t think I’ve had a break all day until you guys walked in just now.”

Before Agatha could respond, Nicky’s voice cut in excitedly. “Rio, you should come to our house for dinner! Mama always makes the best food. You can eat and not work so much!”

Rio blinked, caught off guard by the suggestion. Her gaze darted to Agatha, whose cheeks had flushed a faint pink. She didn’t know whether to thank her son or scold him for bringing it up so spontaneously. She told herself she’d do the latter, but she knew that was a lie.

“Oh, I—“ Rio stammered.

“And then you can also see my other drawings! And my toys! Mama, please?” He looked at his mother with the best puppy eyes he could muster.

“Yes, sure honey,” she replied to her son. She looked up at Rio again. “You are welcome at our place,” she reassured, her voice kind but a little flustered. “If you’re free, I mean. After you’re done here.”

Rio hesitated, her heart fluttering. She was sure that if Agatha had stood any closer to her, she would hear the way her heart was beating out of her chest right now. “Well.. if you’re sure it’s okay.”

“It’s more than okay,” Agatha insisted, her own nerves making her speak a bit faster than usual. “You’ve had a long day. You deserve a nice meal.”

Nicky bounced on his toes, clapping his hands. “Yes! This is gonna be awesome!”

Rio couldn’t help but smile at his enthusiasm, feeling a warmth that had nothing to do with the shop’s heating. “Alright,” she said softly. “I’ll come by. Just let me finish these last bouquets.”

Agatha nodded, her nerves settling into a quiet anticipation. “Take your time. We’ll get some groceries and then we’ll see you later.” They exchanged numbers so that Agatha could send her the address, and both women were secretly thrilled to be able to contact each other through the phone.

As they stepped out into the cold air, Nicky skipping to the car ahead of her, Agatha couldn’t stop the faint smile that tugged at her lips. For the first time in a long while, she was looking forward to a Friday night.

-----

Agatha’s house was warm and inviting, the faint smell of creamy chicken pasta — Nicky’s favourite — wafting through the air. In the living room, Nicky was glued to the TV, watching an animated movie, while Agatha busied herself in the kitchen. Ever since Nicky was old enough to entertain himself while she was in the kitchen, she had found a sense of peace in cooking, but tonight, her nerves wouldn’t quite settle. The doorbell rang, snapping her out of her thoughts.

Nicky was already bouncing toward the door. “I’ll get it!” he shouted, yanking it open to reveal Rio standing on the porch, holding a small box of sweets from the famous patisserie in Westview.

“Hi, Rio!” Nicky greeted excitedly.

“Hey there, buddy,” Rio said with a smile, handing him the box. “A little thank-you for inviting me over. You and your mom didn’t have to do this.”

Agatha appeared in the doorway behind him, drying her hands on a dish towel. “Rio, hi,” she said, her voice softer than intended. “Come in. Dinner’s almost ready.”

Rio stepped inside, the warmth of the home a welcome contrast to the frosty evening air. “It smells amazing in here,” she said earnestly, her gaze flicking to Agatha.

“Thanks, he insisted on having his favourite dish tonight,” Agatha said, smiling bashfully as she shut the door behind her. “Nicky, come help me finish setting the table, okay?”

Nicky saluted dramatically and ran to the dining room, then paused, looking back. “Rio, can you help me? I can’t reach everything.”

“Of course,” Rio said, shrugging off her coat and hanging it on the rack. She noticed that Nicky and Agatha weren’t wearing shoes in the house. Just like her own home, this was a shoes-off type of household, she figured. She slipped off her shoes and placed them neatly by the door before following them inside. As she walked through the hallway, she noticed a few picture frames lining the walls. Some had pictures of the two together, others were just of Nicky, and even one of Nicky with Billy and Tommy. No pictures of Ralph, as far as she could see. Rio didn’t want to think about the reason why that made her feel relieved.

In the kitchen, Agatha grabbed plates from a cabinet, but Rio reached past her to retrieve the glasses from the shelf nearby. Their hands brushed briefly as Rio stepped in close, causing both women to freeze for a moment.

“Sorry,” Rio murmured, her cheeks tinged pink as she stepped back.

“No, it’s fine,” Agatha replied quickly, her voice a little higher than usual. She turned back to Nicky, passing him the plates carefully, her heart fluttering for reasons she didn’t want to examine too closely.

By the time everything was ready, the table was set, and the three of them sat down to eat. Nicky was in high spirits, chatting animatedly about school and his favourite shows.

“You know,” Rio said as Nicky recounted a story about his class, “you’ve got a lot of cool interests, kiddo.”

“Thanks!” Nicky beamed. “Mama says I talk too much sometimes, but you don’t think so, right?”

“Not at all,” Rio replied with a laugh, glancing at Agatha, who looked a little ashamed at her son’s admission.

Agatha smiled softly, letting the conversation flow. She enjoyed watching Rio engage with her son, the way her eyes lit up when she encouraged him or asked follow-up questions. It was rare to see someone take such a genuine interest in Nicky’s world.

After dinner, they cleared the table together. When they finished, Nicky tugged at Rio’s hand. “Can I show you my room? I have all my toys there!”

Agatha nodded indulgently. “Go ahead, but don’t keep her too long, Nicky. She’s had a busy day.” She tried to come across as stern, but she knew she would give in to his requests anyway. She just wanted to make sure the florist wasn’t held hostage in a six-year-old’s bedroom, surrounded by stuffed animals and action figures.

Rio chuckled as she was led up the stairs, and Agatha followed behind them.

In Nicky’s room, he proudly showed off his collection of little figurines and his favourite stuffed animal, a well-loved rabbit named Scratchy. Rio listened patiently, kneeling down to examine each item as he spoke.

“This is Scratchy,” Nicky said, holding up the rabbit. “I’ve had him since I was a baby. He’s the best.”

“He looks like he’s been on some adventures,” Rio said with a grin. Frankly, the stuffed animal looked like it had seen a lot of adventures. At least it was well-loved.

Nicky nodded seriously. “The coolest adventures.”

“So I guess Señor Scratchy over here is like your partner in crime?” she asked, recounting how fond he had been of superheroes when he showed his toys. He asked what the word señor meant, and she explained that it was the Spanish equivalent of sir. He nodded excitedly in understanding, happy to have learned something new from her again. Nicky smiled widely, realising that he could reenact superhero stories with his rabbit as his sidekick. 

They spent a decent amount of time listening to Nicky excitedly explain the stories behind each character. Eventually, Agatha stepped in. “Alright, kiddo, time to get ready for bed.”

He started pouting immediately, but Agatha raised her eyebrow with her hand resting on her hip, a tell-tale sign that she meant business. He reluctantly put his toys down, before looking at the book on his nightstand. It was part of his bedtime ritual; Agatha would always read with him before he went to sleep.

“Wait!” Nicky said, eyes wide. “Can Rio read my story today? Please?”

“Oh, I don’t want to intrude,” Rio said quickly, looking at Agatha.

“If you want to, it’s okay. Don’t worry about me, I get to read with him every day,” Agatha said with an exaggerated roll of her eyes, though her cheeks were faintly pink. Nicky happily pulled Rio down onto his bed, plopping next to her as they both lay down. Agatha turned around to leave the room, wanting to give the two some space while they read. She didn’t want to awkwardly stand in the room while they occupied his small bed.

“And you have to stay too, Mama,” Nicky added insistently.

Agatha sighed but couldn’t help smiling. “Alright, scoot over, then. You know very well that Mama can’t fit her big butt here,” she added playfully, patting on the tiny amount of free space on the mattress. They all laughed at the comment, and Rio had to actively stop herself from thinking about the older woman’s body. Now was definitely not the time.

The small bed was in the corner, pushed against the wall. Rio scooted closer to the wall, allowing Nicky to scoot closer to her as she draped her arm lightly around him. This allowed just enough space for Agatha to squeeze herself into the bed, too. The arm that Rio had wrapped around Nicky, was now awkwardly squished between his and Agatha’s body. Her hand brushed against Agatha’s side, and both women stiffened slightly, their cheeks heating. They avoided eye contact and focused on the book Nicky had chosen.

They took turns reading sentences, Rio’s voice calm and soothing, Agatha’s soft and melodic. It felt oddly domestic, but neither of them seemed to mind. By the end, Nicky was nodding off, his head resting against Rio’s shoulder.

Agatha tucked him in while Rio carefully crawled out of bed and placed the book on the nightstand.

“Goodnight, Mama. Goodnight, Rio,” Nicky murmured sleepily.

“Goodnight, sweetheart,” Agatha said, leaning down to kiss his forehead.

“Goodnight, Nicky,” Rio added with a warm smile. She felt a strong urge to mimic Agatha’s action; to also place a kiss on his forehead. She considered it carefully but eventually refrained from doing so, telling herself it was not her place. She fidgeted with her hands awkwardly instead. Agatha noticed the nervous look on the florist’s face but didn’t say anything. They walked out of the room and closed the door softly.

Downstairs, the air felt different—charged, almost hesitant. Agatha considered what to do next. “Would you, uh, like a coffee? or wine?” she asked shyly, glancing at Rio.

Rio shook her head with a small smile. “Thanks, but I should get going. Big day tomorrow. I need to be available in case that wedding venue needs anything else.”

Agatha nodded, hiding her disappointment. “Alright, of course. Thanks for coming. Nicky had a great time.” It was easier to put it on Nicky, rather than admitting she had been equally as thrilled about spending more time with the younger woman.

“So did I,” Rio admitted softly, her gaze lingering on Agatha for a moment before she put on her shoes and grabbed her coat.

Both women were lost in their own thoughts as the evening came to a close, each wondering if the other had felt the same subtle spark that had flickered between them.

Chapter End Notes

The amount of times I've reshuffled the order of this story in my drafts is insane. Hope you enjoy this chapter, next one's also almost finished. If I'm not too hungover tomorrow, I'll post it.

I assume it's a bit too early for most of you, but: Happy New Year!

Chapter 4: Jealousy, jealousy

Chapter Summary

Alice and Jen interfere in their dating lives; Agatha gets jealous, Rio gets jealous, everyone's a bit jealous at this point.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

The house was quiet, save for the soft hum of the heater and the faint sound of cars driving through the street. Agatha lay in bed, staring at the ceiling. She had turned off the lights over an hour ago, but sleep stubbornly eluded her. The events of the evening replayed in her mind on an endless loop. Forced to watch, like an unskippable ad. She let out a soft sigh, turning onto her side, then onto her back again, unable to find a comfortable position.

The memory of Rio’s smile, the warmth in her deep brown eyes, the faint brushes of her hand against Agatha’s body—all of it lingered like an ache.

“What am I doing?” she whispered to herself.

Her heart waged war with her better judgment. Agatha had spent years building walls, keeping herself and Nicky safe from the complications of letting someone new into their lives. It wasn’t just about her. It was about her son. He had already been through enough with the divorce, she couldn’t afford to make a mistake. But here she was, letting the evening’s memories unravel her defences.

She thought of Nicky first, his giggles at the dinner table. The way he had lit up when Rio asked him questions, listening with genuine interest, made Agatha’s heart swell. Not to forget his uncontainable excitement as he had shown his favourite toys in his room. The warmth in Rio’s eyes when she listened to him chatter on and on about Scratchy. It was rare for Agatha to see Nicky connect so effortlessly with someone outside their little bubble. He was a very sweet child, but often felt shy around people he didn’t know very well. He’s taken to her so easily, she thought, biting her lip. Even with their first proper bonding time at the store, he hadn’t been reserved at all. And Rio had done it so naturally, without hesitation or pretence. To her own surprise, it hadn’t made her want to bolt out the door for once. It had felt... safe. Comfortable, in a way that both soothed and unnerved her.

She sighed again, heavier this time. Rio was younger than her, by nearly a decade. That was an undeniable fact. And Agatha couldn’t help but wonder about the possibilities. Would Rio even consider getting involved with someone like her? She wasn’t just older, not just a 42-year-old pining after a 34-year-old; she was a business owner and a single mother with a six-year-old. Her life was full of routines, responsibilities, and the occasional chaos of parenting.

What if Rio wanted something different? A simpler life, someone without baggage?

Her stomach twisted at the thought, insecurity creeping in like a dark shadow. And yet, she allowed herself to entertain the idea of the two of them together for a bit longer.

Rio had fit into their evening so seamlessly, taking on that caring role at the flower shop, during dinner, and even at bedtime—it all felt too natural to ignore. Agatha had caught herself stealing glances at Rio, admiring how easily she had connected with Nicky. Her patience, her humour, her warmth. It was more than Agatha had allowed herself to hope for after everything.

A spark of hope flared in her chest, cautious but insistent. Maybe, just maybe, it didn’t have to weigh so heavy on her. At least with Rio, she didn’t need to worry about whether her partner would get along with her son. That part seemed to take care of itself.

But that didn’t make the other doubts go away. Agatha clenched her fists against the comforter, willing the feelings to dissipate. It was too soon, too complicated. She couldn’t let herself get swept away.

She couldn’t help herself, though. Her mind drifted to the moment in Nicky’s bed, when all three of them had squeezed together, with Nicky happily nestled in the middle. Agatha blushed, remembering the warmth of Rio’s hand pressed against her side. It had been so innocent and fleeting, but impossible to forget. The proximity had made her hyper-aware of every detail: the scent of Rio’s perfume, subtle, unsurprisingly floral and dangerously intoxicating; the soft sound of her voice as she read; the way Agatha’s eyes had wandered, unbidden, to her lips. Agatha pressed a hand to her forehead, her cheeks feeling undeniably hot as she remembered every small detail.

She groaned softly, burying her face in her pillow. What was she doing? She couldn’t think about things like that. Not about how close they’d been, or her lips, and what they might feel like—

“No,” Agatha muttered, her voice firm in the stillness. She refused to entertain those thoughts any further. It was ridiculous, irresponsible even, to let her mind wander down that path. She had no idea how Rio felt. This was inappropriate, all too complicated, too soon.

“No,” she repeated sternly to herself, as if she were scolding a child. “Stop it. Stop thinking about her.”

She squeezed her eyes shut, determined to banish the thoughts, to force herself into sleep. But the stubborn heat in her chest — and frankly, between her legs — refused to be ignored. In a desperate attempt at ridding herself of these thoughts, she reluctantly got up to take a quick, cold, shower.

She felt much better afterwards, her head partially cleared, and settled into bed more comfortably now. For the first time in a long time, Agatha felt something shift. Something she wasn’t sure she was ready to face yet, but she was ready to name it. At least to herself. It was undoubtedly there—a spark she couldn’t quite extinguish, no matter how hard she tried. That night, Agatha fell asleep with a faint smile on her lips.

—————

Over the weekend, Agatha had spent time with Nicky, savouring the moments of peace and routine with her son. This meant that she hadn’t been able to steal glances at the flower shop across the street. It didn’t worry her, though. Now that they had exchanged numbers for the sole purpose of texting the address, it had opened doors. It wasn’t much, but the two had shared a few casual texts. It started with Rio thanking her again for the nice dinner, and asking what her plans were with Nicky. The florist herself mentioned that she would just be getting some rest, as she had another big order to fulfil during the next week. She had received a request for dried flowers, which took careful planning on her end, meaning she’d need to start well in advance.

Monday mornings were usually hectic, having to get herself and Nicky ready before dropping him off at school and going to the cafe. She put extra effort into her appearance today, though she wouldn’t admit it if anyone pestered her about it. Her long waves were carefully put into a sleek high ponytail. She wore denim pants that hugged her waist  just right and a form-fitting turtleneck, her signature necklace hung around her neck. Today, she had a mission.

The morning at the cafe passed by easily, with Billy taking care of most of the orders that came through. When Agatha suddenly made her way to the espresso machine, the teenager looked at her in confusion

“Did I forget an order? I thought I took care of everything,” he asked nervously. Agatha wasn’t a mean boss, but he was still intimidated by her. He looked up to her and respected her, which made him determined to avoid mistakes at all costs.

“Oh, this? No order, I just— I’m just making a coffee.” She tried to wave it off casually, but Billy could tell something was off. He didn’t press further, but just eyed her curiously as she poured a cappuccino into a to-go cup, with a small pump of vanilla syrup from the counter. He recognised the order, smirking at Agatha knowingly. The older woman ignored the look he was giving her, but the faint blush on her cheeks betrayed her. She felt grateful that he was too scared of her to tease her for it.
