Agatha walked into the flower shop with steady resolve, the rich aroma of freshly brewed coffee wafting from the to-go cup in her hand. The door chimed as she entered, and her eyes were immediately drawn to Rio at the counter, ringing up a sale. The florist’s usually calm demeanour was tinged with awkwardness, her smile polite but strained as she spoke to a blonde woman across the counter. Agatha slowed her steps, her ears catching bits of their conversation.

The woman leaned forward on the counter slightly, her voice sweet and overly daring. “So, what are you doing tonight? You must get some time off from all this,” she said, gesturing vaguely at the shop.

Rio shifted, her hands fidgeting slightly. “Uh, not much. Probably just staying late to finish my work,” she replied, her tone noncommittal. She knew it sounded lame, but it was the truth.

“Oh, come on,” the blonde woman pressed. “Surely you deserve a little fun. Maybe dinner? My treat.”

Agatha’s stomach twisted. Jealousy had become a foreign feeling, one she hadn’t allowed herself in years. Well, she hadn’t cared about any of her dates enough to feel jealous. But now, standing there holding a coffee for Rio, she couldn’t deny the sharp pang of irritation flaring inside her.

She wasn’t sure if she’d be able to hear Rio’s response with the way her heart was beating out of her chest. Was she going to witness the brunette securing a date, right here, right now?

She allowed her eyes to take the florist in; she wore her apron as usual. Under it was a denim shirt, sleeves rolled up, showing off her toned forearms. The bottom part of the outfit was blocked from her vision by the counter, but she saw the same colour denim peeking out at the waist. Her hair was pinned up with a large clip, a few loose strands framing her face. Agatha couldn’t blame the customer for flirting with Rio; she strongly felt the same urge.

Rio’s response couldn’t come soon enough. When it finally came, however, Agatha let out a breath she was very aware she was holding. The florist cleared her throat, shaking her head. “That’s very nice of you, but I’m not looking for anything right now.”

Agatha wasn’t sure what that meant. It meant ‘no, I won’t go on a date with you’, she knew that much. I’m not looking for anything right now, she replayed in her head. That means she doesn’t want to commit, right? Or that she had already found something, possibly? The fear of Rio being unavailable —emotionally or out of commitment to someone else— spread over her. Agatha felt relieved that she had turned the other woman down, yet nervous about what she had implied. Maybe this wasn’t going to go as smoothly as she thought.

The customer’s disappointment was evident, but she smiled and shrugged. “Well, if you change your mind, you know where to find me. My number’s on the order.” With a wink, she took her purchased flowers and left the shop, the bell above the door jingling as she exited.

For a brief moment, she imagined taking the order sheet and ripping it up, just to ensure that Rio wouldn’t call her. She knew very well that it wasn’t her place, though, so she shook her  head in attempt to physically rid herself of those thoughts. Agatha stepped forward then, trying to soothe her swirling emotions. Rio glanced up and immediately smiled, her face softening. “Hey, Agatha.”

“Hey,” Agatha replied, keeping her tone light. She held up the coffee cup casually, not wanting to expose the storm that was brewing inside of her. It was easier now that Rio’s attention was on her, and she felt the tension in her body ebb slowly. “Thought you might need this. Just in case you’re too busy to come get one again.”

Rio’s eyes lit up with surprise, and she walked around the counter to meet her. “You didn’t have to do that.”

Agatha shrugged casually, though her heart was pounding, both from witnessing the interaction before, and having the florist in her proximity again. “It’s no trouble. Besides, I remember you saying how much you needed coffee last week. Can’t have an ambulance parked outside my shop just because you collapsed in here from caffeine withdrawal.”

Rio chuckled, taking the cup from her. Their fingers brushed briefly, sending a small jolt through both of them. “Thank you. Seriously, this means a lot.”

“You’re welcome.” Agatha’s gaze drifted briefly toward the door. “So... she seemed persistent,” she teased, referencing the blonde customer.

Rio rolled her eyes with a laugh. “Oh, you saw that, huh?”

“Hard not to. She wasn’t exactly subtle,” Agatha quipped. Then, more hesitantly, she added, “She didn’t seem too bad, why’d you turn her down? Are you, uh, not looking to date?”

“Sorry, I don’t know why I asked, I—” she fumbled over the words, regretting her question when she realised how invasive it sounded. She hated how her confidence seemed to slip so easily. She didn’t want to show the florist how much she cared, but she couldn’t resist the urge to ask.

“No, it’s okay,” the florist interjected gently, taking Agatha out of her misery.

Rio’s expression shifted, her cheeks tinged pink. She looked at Agatha, her brown eyes sincere. There was a look in them that made the older woman feel hopeful, but she stowed it away quickly. “It’s not that I don’t want to,” she continued shyly. “She just wasn’t my type, I guess.”

Relief flooded Agatha’s chest, although she tried not to let it show. “Fair enough,” she said, a small smile playing on her lips. She wanted to ask what her type was, but bit her tongue this time, swallowing the words before they had the chance to escape.

Rio tilted her head, studying her. “Did you really come all the way here just to bring me coffee?”

Agatha shrugged again, fighting the urge to fidget. “You were too busy last week. I figured I’d save you the trip.”

Rio smiled softly, clearly touched by the gesture. “That’s very sweet, but you didn’t have to.”

“It’s not a problem at all. Actually, if you’re ever too busy to swing by again, just shoot me a text, and I’ll take care of it.” The cafe owner looked at Rio with a look that showed nothing but sincerity.

“I can’t expect you to bring me coffee just because I’m swamped,” the brunette added, still unable to accept the generous offer.

Agatha smirked, a teasing edge in her tone. “Don’t worry, next time I’ll just send Billy. He could use a break from annoying me at the cafe.”

Rio laughed, the sound warm and genuine. “As much as I like Billy, I think I’d rather get the coffee from you.”

Agatha blinked, her heart skipping at the flirtatious glint in Rio’s eyes. She opened her mouth to respond, but her brain betrayed her, leaving her momentarily speechless.

Rio seemed to enjoy her reaction and decided to take advantage of the situation. “In fact, if I text your phone number and suddenly Billy shows up, consider yourself blocked,” she said, sipping her coffee with a smirk.

The cafe owner’s eyes widened in disbelief. “You wouldn’t dare, Vidal,” Agatha said, narrowing her eyes playfully.

Agatha watched as Rio worried the inside of her cheek with her tongue and raised an eyebrow. “Try me, Harkness,” she teased back.

There was a growing tension between them, finding themselves on new territory due to the reciprocated teasing. They held eye contact for a while, neither of them wanting to look away. For a moment everything around them seemed to fade. It was just the two of them, in this moment, together.

Rio is the first to break, trying to cover her blush by taking another sip of her beverage. “It’s perfect, by the way. Exactly how I like it. Thanks again.”

“You’re welcome,” Agatha managed, her voice softer now. The moment lingered between them, unspoken words and unacknowledged feelings swirling in the air.

Finally, Agatha cleared her throat. “Well, I should get back to work. Can’t have Billy slacking off too much.”

Rio chuckled. “Wouldn’t want that.”

Agatha nodded, brushing her coat pocket as she turned toward the door. “Enjoy the coffee.”

“Always do,” Rio replied, her tone layered with meaning.

Agatha left the shop, the cool air hitting her flushed cheeks as she stepped outside. She couldn’t help the small smile tugging at her lips. Their newfound playful dynamic had surprised her, but she already couldn’t get enough of it.

—————

The rest of the week passes by quite uneventfully. The cafe had been crowded as usual, keeping Agatha busy. After work, she’d spend her time with Nicky, who only occasionally asked about their new friend, Rio. The florist had come by the cafe consistently, but all her visits were shorter than usual, given that she still had a lot of orders to manage.

Later that week, on Friday afternoon, Agatha worked behind the counter, the faint scent of espresso mingling with the sweet aroma of fresh pastries. Billy was on barista duty today, expertly steaming milk for a latte while chatting animatedly with Lilia, who was wiping down tables between customers.

Billy was telling them about his plans for the weekend. He and his boyfriend had been trying to decide what to do, not wanting to go on another movie date.

Agatha smiled faintly at their conversation as she arranged a tray of croissants on display. But her mind was elsewhere, the words and laughter around her blurring into background noise.

“Earth to Agatha,” Lilia said, waving a hand in front of her face. “You good, honey?”

Agatha blinked, startled out of her thoughts. “Hm? Oh, yeah. Just tired.”

Billy eyed her with concern as he passed a latte to a customer. “You sure? You’ve been more quiet than usual today. Even for you.”

Agatha shrugged, trying to brush it off. “I’m fine. Really. Now what were you saying?”

“Eddie said we should try rock climbing,” Billy said, his tone dripping with mock horror. “Me! Hanging from a cliff! I told him, ‘Baby, I love you, but if you want me to die for you, there are less humiliating ways to go about it.’”

Lilia snorted. “Come on, Billy. It could be fun! He just wants to try something new with you.”

“Lilia, please,” Billy replied with a dramatic eye-roll. “I look amazing in anything, but I draw the line at dying in a harness. They’re not flattering.”

“I’m sure Agatha over here would disagree,” Lilia joked with a smirk, pulling Agatha’s attention back to the conversation once again. “Harnesses can be quite hot, can’t they?” the older woman continued to tease her.

Agatha felt her face flush and eyes widen at the insinuation. Her mind drifted briefly to the brown-eyed woman across the street, and she felt heat bubbling inside of her at the idea of ways they could use a harness.

She wasn’t one to back down, though, so she replied, “Not much experience in that department yet, but I see the appeal, yes.” She added a wink of her own for good measure, happy she didn’t lose this round.

“Ew! Please, stop!” Billy covered his eyes as if that was going to stop him from hearing this conversation or picturing it in his mind. “What happened to not discussing these things in front of kids…”

Agatha and Lilia laughed loudly but felt bad enough for the teenager not to continue talking about it in more detail. Once their laughter and Billy’s embarrassment died down, Billy leaned on the counter resting his chin in his hand. “What about you, Agatha? Any exciting plans for the weekend?”

Agatha hesitated, her lips pressing into a thin line. She’d been overthinking this more than usual today, and she couldn’t figure out why. She tried to act casual, not wanting to show how much she was bothered by it. “Nah, not really. Nicky’s spending the night at Ralph’s tomorrow. Those aren’t my favourite weekends.”

Billy made a sympathetic noise, and Lilia frowned. “That’s gotta be tough. You should plan something fun for yourself. Go out, see a movie, hit the town, or whatever you young people call it these days.”

Agatha chuckled wryly. “I’m hardly that young anymore, Lilia. I’m past the big 40, in case you forgot.” Lilia looked at her in disagreement, she had always felt protective over Agatha and had been some sort of mother figure for her. The cafe owner would always be young in her eyes.

“But anyway, I usually just stay home with a tub of whatever ice cream I can find in the freezer and a sappy rom-com. Bad reality TV occasionally. It’s tradition.”

Billy placed a hand on his chest like she’d just personally offended him. “Agatha, no. That’s not a tradition, it’s a cry for help!” He waved his hands in the air dramatically. “You’re not allowed to wallow like that. We won’t let you.”

“Exactly,” Lilia chimed in. “You need to go out, do something that makes you happy. Even if it’s just a little thing. Just because Nicky isn’t with you, doesn’t mean you’re not allowed to have fun.”

Agatha opened her mouth to protest, but the clock on the wall caught her attention. Relief washed over her as she felt saved by the bell. “I’d love to stay and debate my sad life choices, but it’s time to get Nicky from school.” She walked to the back room to grab her coat.

Lilia rolled her eyes, but her smile was affectionate. “Fine, dodge the intervention. For now. But we’re not done with this conversation.”

Billy blew her an exaggerated kiss as she headed for the door. “Have fun with Nicky! And think about what I said: ice cream nights are for breakups, not weekends off while you’re still in your prime.”

Agatha waved them off with a laugh, but as she stepped outside, the cold winter air bit at her cheeks, grounding her thoughts. Despite their teasing, she couldn’t deny the truth in their words. Maybe she did deserve to find a little joy this weekend.

The question was, what - or who - could make that happen?

—————

After Agatha left, the cafe still buzzed with its usual afternoon energy. The only change now, however, is that Rio was sat at a corner table with Alice and Jen, the remnants of their drinks pushed aside as their focus turned squarely to their friend.

“You actually turned her down?” Alice asked, raising an eyebrow.

Rio shrugged, poking at the edge of her saucer. “She wasn’t my type. So, I didn’t want to lead her on.”

Jen smirked, nudging Alice. “You’re impossible. How many more weekends are you going to spend doing nothing, though? You’re still on that dating app, right?”

Rio hesitated. “Technically, yes. But I don’t know… I’m not really finding the type of people I want to meet.”

Jen tilted her head with mock curiosity. “And what exactly is your type? Mysterious cafe owners with a cute kid?”

Rio gave her a sharp look, but her cheeks tinged pink. “That’s none of your business. And anyway, the app’s not working. I only get matches with weirdos. And sometimes men, even though I specifically put it on women only,” she huffed, crossing her arms.

Jen wasn’t letting her off the hook. “Let us see your profile.”

“What? No way.” Rio shot forward and protectively placed her hand over her phone, gripping it as if it was going to be ripped from her hands.

But Alice already had her hand out, palm up. “Come on, we’re professionals. You clearly need our help if you’re not getting the right matches.”

Reluctantly, Rio handed over her phone. Alice and Jen immediately burst into laughter as they skimmed through her profile.

“Rio, you look so serious in these!” Jen exclaimed. “No one’s going to swipe right if they think you’re interviewing for a job!”

“Ever heard of looking approachable?” Alice teased. “You should try it.”

They went to work, scrolling through her camera roll. They selected a few pictures, including one where Rio was at a basketball game with her friends and a video of Rio at a bowling alley, rolling the ball into the gutter without hitting any pins before turning around and saying ‘No, erase that!’ to the camera. As caption they put: you’re either good at bowling or you’re good in bed. Both of them giggled, and Rio made a mental note to check out whatever they had done to her profile before letting it go live.

Then, Jen found the picture—a candid shot of Rio in a fitted dress with a deep V-neck showing off her cleavage, her hair cascading down her shoulders.

“This one,” Jen said, holding it up triumphantly. “This is hot.”

Sometime during the conversation between the friends and their relentless teasing, Agatha walked back into the cafe. Lilia noticed immediately. “Back so soon? Where’s Nicky?”

Agatha set her bag down behind the counter and put on her apron again. “He wanted to play at a friend’s house, so I figured I’d come back here.”

Alice and Jen exchanged a look and Rio could only guess what that meant, but she knew it wasn’t in her favour. Before Rio could snatch her phone back, Alice called out, “Agatha! Come here for a sec.”

Agatha approached cautiously, brushing her hands on her apron, more out of awkwardness than to wipe them clean. “What’s up?”

Jen grinned, holding up the phone to show off the picture. “What do you think of this one?”

Agatha’s breath caught for a split second as her eyes landed on the picture. Rio looked… stunning. She didn’t know where to focus; her eyes, her hair, her lips, her incredibly deep neckline. Her eyes roamed over all of it and she felt herself grow hotter. Composing herself quickly, she smiled, eyes briefly drifting to Rio. “You look great, Rio. Really good.”

Rio shifted awkwardly in her seat, already feeling her face heat up. She wished she could respond properly to the older woman, but couldn’t do more than mumble a quiet, “Thanks.”

“What’s this for?” Agatha asked, curiosity getting the upper hand.

“We’re revamping Rio’s dating profile,” Jen responded before Rio could reply. Rio’s response, however, came in the form of a kick under the table, carefully directed at her friend’s shin.

Agatha didn’t notice, too startled by the response, and she wondered what other parts of the conversation she had missed while she was gone. Instead she asked casually, “Dating profile, huh?”

Alice nodded enthusiastically. “Yeah, we’re making sure she gets all the right swipes. Gotta find her someone who’ll sweep her off her feet.”

Agatha felt a pang of jealousy, she didn’t want anyone else to ‘sweep her off her feet’. Rio deserved the best, but Agatha hated picturing her with someone other than herself. She masked it with a chuckle, praying that it wasn’t obvious that she cared about this more than she admitted out loud.

“Well, this should certainly do the trick,” she said, glancing at Rio once more. It was the truth. The picture showed a side of Rio that the cafe owner didn’t get to see more often, and it left her very willing to sweep her off her feet, or so to say. She turned to head back to the counter before the florist could let her compliment sink in, but Jen wasn’t done.

“Hey, you’re also single right? Maybe we should set up a profile for you, Agatha,” Jen teased, her grin widening.

Agatha laughed nervously. “Yeah, no, thanks. That’s not my thing.”

“Why not?” Alice pressed. “It could be fun!”

“Fun? It’s ridiculous,” Agatha replied with a wave of her hand. “Look at me, I’m not going to find someone on an app.”

As if on cue, Billy popped up beside her. “Wait, what’s this about Agatha dating?” Of course, he had to have heard that part of the conversation. Agatha wished she could disappear.

Alice grinned wickedly. “We’re thinking about setting up a profile for her.”

Billy gasped dramatically. “Agatha, are you finally taking my advice? The heavens have opened!”

Then, he turned to Alice excitedly, hands clasped together. “Please, can I help? She can’t keep spending weekends moping on the couch.”

The cafe owner shook her head, but Billy wasn’t willing to back down. “I can even do the swiping for you, just let me know what your type is and I’ll set you up.”

Agatha groaned. “No one’s setting me up, Billy. End of discussion.”

Billy crossed his arms, ignoring her protests. “What about that date you went on last month? Anything happen there?” Upon hearing this, Rio’s interest was piqued. She was suddenly very invested in hearing about Agatha’s recent date.

“Nope,” Agatha replied curtly. “She wasn’t a match, so I didn’t bother exploring it further. Now that you mention it, I don’t think I even texted her back, actually.”

Billy sighed. “You’re hopeless. This could’ve saved you on your weekend without Nicky.” He gestured toward Alice and Jen. “You two have my blessing. Fix her.”

As laughter erupted, Rio, who had been quiet, spoke up. “Wait…Where’s Nicky?”

Agatha hesitated. “Oh, he’s gonna be staying at Ralph’s. It’s nothing.”

Billy chimed in before she could stop him. “I wouldn’t call 'wallowing on the couch with a tub of ice cream’ nothing.”

Rio’s brows knit together in concern. “That sounds… lonely.” Alice and Jen nodded in agreement. Great, just what Agatha needed: people judging how she spent her own free time.

“Sure,” Agatha admitted, very aware of how it made her look, “But not lonely enough to let myself be set up on a date by these three,” she finished with narrowed eyes, pointing at the others as if they were up to no good. Rio laughed softly at that.

Alice and Jen shared another knowing look, as if communicating telepathically was a perk that they unlocked when they started dating. Jen leaned back in her seat, a smirk playing on her lips.

“Well, if neither of you will let us set you up to avoid spending your weekend alone like losers, why don’t you spend time together instead?”

Agatha’s eyes widened in surprise and Rio choked on her own saliva. They looked at each other, not wanting to reply without knowing what the other was thinking. Agatha regained her composure quickly, her years of building up a wall making it easy for her to act indifferent, unaffected. “Let’s spare her from having to deal with me on my pathetic Saturday night.”

“I mean, I’d be down to do something,” Rio responded when she finally found her voice, her friends’ encouraging smiles giving her a newfound strength.

“No really, it’s okay, you don’t need to—”

“I want to,” Rio interjected firmly, her gaze steady.

Agatha hesitated, glancing at Lilia, who was looking over at them from a distance and gave her an encouraging nod. Finally, she smiled shyly.

“Okay, fine. It’s settled then.” She had almost said 'it’s a date’, but caught the words before they slipped. It wasn’t a date. It was just two people hanging out, because they didn’t want to be set up on a date. That’s all it was.

Alice and Jen exchanged a triumphant look as Rio grinned.

“Guess you’ve got plans now,” Billy teased, winking at Agatha.

Rio and Agatha locked eyes briefly, both feeling their hearts race but unsure what exactly the weekend might bring.

Chapter End Notes

tbh I'm just in need of some fluff in my life, so that's what you're getting throughout this fic. Maybe I'll switch it up in my next work.

Also, thanks for the comments and kudos, means a lot <3

Chapter 5: Dinos, dragons, caterpillars

Chapter Summary

Both Nicky and Agatha are desperate for more time with Rio. Only one of them is open about it, though.

Chapter Notes

double post incoming bc I was determined to include their hang-out sesh in my next update, but suddenly that chapter became 14k+ words. so, I split it in two just to feel less deranged.

also; lesbian Agatha truthers rise

See the end of the chapter for more notes

The next morning, Agatha prepared breakfast while Nicky finished packing his favourite toys into his bag. She had helped him pack the essentials but left him in charge of choosing what else he wanted to bring. She flipped over the pancakes skilfully and fought the urge to take one from the plate sitting next to her.

“Nicky! Are you almost ready?” she called out, tilting her head vaguely into the direction of the stairs but still keeping her eyes on the pancakes. Contrary to her usual chaotic mornings with him, she was determined not to let them burn this time.

“Yeah!” Nicky’s voice was muffled coming from upstairs. “Just need to get my dinosaurs!”

When Agatha finished setting the table, she heard her son racing down the stairs. He wore jeans and a crewneck sweater with dinosaurs on it; it wasn’t hard to guess his latest infatuation. Nicky bounced into the kitchen and sat at the table, his eyes lighting up when he saw the pancakes. She served him a generous portion, topping it with syrup and fresh fruit. She cut it up in smaller pieces and teasingly stole a piece off of his plate.

“Hey! You have your own right there Mama,” he said, pointing at the other plate on the table. He didn’t waste another second before eagerly digging into his pancakes, face already becoming sticky with syrup.

“But yours are always better,” she responded, feigning innocence. She slid into her seat across from him and took a bite from her own serving.

“So, what’s the plan with your dad today?” she asked, keeping the conversation light.

“We’re going to the park and then to the movies,” he said between bites. “He said I can pick whatever snacks I want!”

“Sounds like a fun day, honey,” Agatha smiled gently at him, watching him fondly even with syrup all over his face. “Just don’t eat too much candy, okay?”

“I won’t,” he promised, mouth half-full, and Agatha knew he’d try to push the limits. She couldn’t blame Ralph for giving into Nicky’s demands, though. She never could resist those beautiful brown eyes, either. “What about you, Mama? What are you doing today?”

Agatha hesitated and fidgeted with her necklace, contemplating whether she should tell the truth. She didn’t want Nicky to feel bad if she said she didn’t have any plans, but she knew he might want to stay if he knew Rio was going to visit. As much as she disliked Ralph, she didn’t want to interfere with his time with his dad. And truthfully, she had become quite thrilled about the idea of hanging out with Rio without any other company. Selfishly, she didn’t want anything to interfere with that, either.

“I’ll probably just stay at the cafe,” she said. “I haven’t decided what else yet.”

Nicky shrugged, content with her answer, and polished off the last bite of his pancake. Agatha stood up to wipe his face and urged him to wash his hands.

After tidying up, they headed out the door. The walk to the cafe was short, just a couple of blocks down the street, and the crisp morning air was refreshing. Nicky skipped along beside her, his little legs moving faster than hers. It was one of those simple, happy moments; walking hand in hand, the sun just starting to peek out behind the clouds, making the cold winter morning more pleasant.

Inside the cafe, Lilia and Billy had already started preparing for the day. Lilia spotted them immediately and waved from behind the counter. “Well, if it isn’t my favourite little customer!” she called out warmly. Nicky’s face lit up and he rushed to the counter, where Lilia greeted him with a big hug and an even bigger smile. “Would you like anything, sweetheart?”

Before Agatha could tell her that he had already had his daily dose of sugar during breakfast, Nicky enthusiastically responded. “I want hot chocolate, please!”

“Coming right up!” Lilia beamed, preparing the hot chocolate and adding whipped cream as a special treat. Nicky took his cup and put it down at the nearest table. He went over to the back room to grab his colouring book and pencils, and Agatha followed him to put away their coats. He walked over to a table near the register, flipped the book open and quickly went to work, his little tongue sticking out in concentration.

Agatha watched him from the counter, feeling a small sense of contentment. Once she realised that he was fine entertaining himself, she helped Lilia in the kitchen. They always prepared fresh pastries in the morning, filling the entire cafe with the scent of homemade treats. As time passed, Nicky became distracted from his drawing and started walking around the cafe curiously. He’d occasionally say hi to the customers seated at tables, but Agatha tried to stop him from bothering them too much. They never seemed to mind, but she felt it wasn’t appropriate for him to hop around the tables and ramble on and on about his latest obsessions. He stopped in front of the window, his eyes landing on the flower shop across the street. His face lit up and Agatha knew where this was going immediately.

“Mama! Mama!” he said as he rushed over to her, grabbing her hand and tugging on it. Agatha, in the middle of wiping down the display, raised an eyebrow and hummed in response. “Yes, dear?”

“Can I go see Rio?” he asked hopefully, big brown eyes staring into his mother’s. Agatha had hesitation written all over her face, and Nicky noticed. Before she could respond, he added a very convincing, “Please? Please?”

Agatha bit her lip, unsure. “I don’t know… You know how busy Rio gets. Besides, your dad’s coming to pick you up soon, remember?”

Unwilling to take no for an answer, Nicky thought for a moment before trying again. “Please, Mama? Only for a little while, I have more questions about the plants!”

Agatha looked at him, her heart softening at his curiosity. She wanted to make sure that Rio was up for it and decided to send her a text.

Hey, you busy?

The response came almost immediately.

Rio: I’ve got a little room to breathe for once

Rio: why, what’s up?

She looked over at Nicky again before responding.

Nicky’s begging me to let him go over there

Rio: Aww, I’d like that

Rio: I’ll show him the new plants I got in

Agatha started to wonder how Rio did it. She always welcomed Nicky so warmly, allowing him to pester her with questions to no end. If it did ever bother her, she never showed it. Even as his mother, Agatha sometimes felt like it cost her a lot of energy to keep up with his eagerness to learn. She would never discourage him from being curious, but it did exhaust her at times.

Alright, he’ll be right there

Please send him back if it gets busy, okay? I don’t want him to bother you

Rio: It’s never too busy to have him around, don’t worry

Agatha should’ve expected this response from the florist, typical. Of course, she wouldn’t admit she’s swamped with work if it meant disappointing a six-year-old. Of course, she was too kind for that. Agatha, however, already felt bad as it was and did not want to push her luck with this woman any further. She didn’t want to push her away by overbearing her with their presence, too much, too quickly. She made sure to reinforce her boundaries.

I mean it, if I see a crowd forming in the shop, I expect him to be back here. Please don’t make me come get him.

Rio: Yes ma’am

Agatha chuckled at the final response and rolled her eyes. She could imagine the other woman standing in a military-like pose, saluting her, before sticking out her tongue in that teasing way she does. Leave it to Rio to make a joke out of the situation. Joke or not, the text made her wonder if Rio liked to be ordered around. The cafe owner hated to admit it, but hearing those words from Rio awakened something within her.

When she put down her phone, Nicky was staring at her expectantly. He was smart enough to understand that his mother had been texting the florist to double-check. When she nodded at him, he practically bounced in place. “Okay,” she said, letting out a small sigh, “go grab your jacket.”

He sprinted to his jacket, putting it on hurriedly, not even bothering to zip it up all the way before running back to the door. Agatha quickly followed him, stopping him before he zoomed out the exit. “Not so fast, buddy, let me walk you there. I need to get my coat, too.”

“But Mama! It’s only across the street, can’t I just go?” he pleaded, unable to wait any longer. “It’s right there!”

Agatha’s eyes moved back and forth, from him to the street and back. She knew it was only across the street —a narrow, one-way street— but she still felt uncomfortable letting him walk to the shop alone. Right as she was about to decline his request, she felt a hand on her shoulder. She turned around and stood face-to-face with Lilia. The older woman looked at her with a look of understanding. She recognised a mother’s protectiveness like no other. “He’ll be fine, Agatha. It’s just across the street and there’s barely any traffic.”

The cafe owner sighed again and knew there was no real reason for her to worry. It was a safe neighbourhood, and it’d take him no more than a few steps to walk from the cafe’s door to the flower shop’s.

“Alright,” she said finally, crouching down to Nicky’s eye level. She adjusted his jacket to shield him from the cold. “You can go, but I’ll be watching the whole time, and you go straight to Rio. Got it?”

He nodded and held up his pinky to make her a promise. She interlocked her finger with his and kissed his forehead before opening the door. She reminded him to look both ways —you never know what idiots are on the road— and to cross quickly.

On the other side of the street, Rio had been looking out the window ever since receiving Agatha’s texts. She waited for Nicky’s arrival, secretly hoping that his mother would tag along. When she finally saw him walk out of the cafe, she noticed that Agatha stayed behind, watching closely through the shop’s window. Although Rio couldn’t see into the shop very clearly, Agatha stood close enough to the exit that she could tell the other woman was anxious: arms crossed, brows furrowed, teeth worrying her lip. She noticed how intently the cafe owner had been eyeing Nicky’s every movement and it suddenly clicked; she was worried about him getting here safely. She made a mental note of this, before grabbing onto the door handle to open it for him, right as he reached her doorstep. The door was heavy and she knew he wouldn’t be able to open it on his own, so she made sure to help him get into the warmth of her store quickly.

“Hey there, buddy!” she greeted warmly, stepping aside enough to make room for him to zoom past her.

“Hi, Rio!” he responded excitedly, mindlessly shrugging off his jacket while his eyes were already hyper-focused on the greenery and vibrant colours around him. She closed the door and noticed that Agatha was still watching. She waved at her shyly, and Agatha finally allowed herself to relax again.

Nicky wandered through the aisles, looking up at the flowers and plants towering over him. “So, do you remember any from last time?” Rio asked, picking up his jacket off the floor and leaning against one of the tables as she watched him with a fond smile.

He squinted, deep in thought, before pointing to a cluster of small succulents on a shelf. “Those! And… um, those dragon flowers over there,” he said, pointing at the snapdragons they had studied before.

“Good memory,” Rio said, impressed. “Do you want to see the new plants that came in this morning?”

“New plants?” Nicky’s face lit up with excitement, bouncing on the balls of his small feet. “Yes, please!”

Rio chuckled and motioned for him to follow her to the back of the shop. She left his jacket on one of the tables and opened a box she’d been unpacking earlier and pulled out a delicate plant with glossy leaves and tiny purple flowers. “This one’s called a purple shamrock. It closes its leaves at night like it’s going to sleep.”

“Wow,” Nicky whispered in awe, his eyes wide. “That’s so cool!”

The florist grinned, loving his curiosity. He looked at her with those big brown eyes and she was reminded of the time they had spent in his bedroom when he introduced her to all of his toys. Her gaze softened at the memory, and she asked, “How’s Señor Scratchy doing these days? Still your favourite?”

The boy giggled and nodded, “Yeah, Scratchy’s the best!” When his laughter died down, his face became slightly more serious — or well, as much as a six-year-old’s face can. “His name is not Señor Scratchy, silly Rio! It’s just Scratchy,” he corrected her.

Rio smirked playfully. “I think Señor Scratchy has a nice ring to it,” she teased, crossing her arms. “It makes him sound sophisticated.”

“Sophisticated?” he asked confused, sounding out the word and trying to understand what it meant. Rio mentally face-palmed herself, oblivious to the fact that a child would not know the meaning of the word, even if said child is incredibly smart.

“It’s like, uh, fancy or cool, or grown-up, in a smart way.” Not sure how to explain herself more than that, she wished she had chosen a different word. Nicky, however, seemed pleased with the answer. “Well, he is smart and cool. Even if he’s just a bunny.”

“See, even bunnies can be sophisticated,” Rio replied, pretending to be serious before breaking into a wide grin, causing the boy to laugh.

When Nicky finally stopped laughing, he looked up at her with excitement in his eyes. “Guess what? I’m in a play next week at school!”

“A play? How fun!” Rio said, crouching down to his level. “What’s it about?”

“Mrs. Davis said it’s called The Very Hungry Caterpillar,” Nicky explained proudly. “I didn’t get the role of the caterpillar, though. I’m the apple that gets eaten first, and then the ice cream cone.”

Rio gasped dramatically. “Two roles? Nicky that’s amazing! You’re like a superstar already!”

He ducked his head shyly, his cheeks turning faintly pink. “It’s only because we don’t have enough kids in my class,” he mumbled. “But I like my parts. Who wouldn’t want to be ice cream!”

“Hey, it doesn’t matter why. Two roles are still super impressive,” Rio said, giving him a little fist bump. “You’re gonna be the best apple and ice cream anyone’s ever seen, buddy, I know it.”

Nicky’s grin returned as he looked up at her. “I asked Dad to come, and he said yes! So he’s gonna sit next to Mama. They always get a bit weird when they need to do that, I don’t really know why… But I’m happy they’ll both be there.”

The brunette felt her heart melt a little at the boy’s openness. “That’s great, Nicky. I bet they’ll both be so proud of you.”

Nicky nodded eagerly. "I would invite you too," he said earnestly. "But we only get to pick two people, and we weren’t friends yet back then."

Rio’s heart gave a little pang. She wished she could see him in the play, but a strange sense of pride washed over her when she heard him call her his friend. It was endearing that he considered her as such. She smiled warmly at him and ruffled his hair. "That’s okay, kiddo. I’m glad your parents will be there for you. That’s what matters."

Nicky beamed at her before his eyes caught on a large green plant with broad leaves. He pointed at it excitedly. "Hey, that leaf looks just like the one in my play! We made a big leaf for the caterpillar to eat at the end, and it’s like that one!"

Rio glanced at the monstera leaf he was pointing at and chuckled. "You’ve got a good eye. That’s called a monstera. It’s pretty cool, right? They can grow quite big.”

"Yeah! I helped Mrs. Davis paint the leaf and it took forever," Nicky said, puffing up with pride. "But it looks so good now."

"I bet it does," Rio said. “We should remind your Mama to send me a picture of it during the play, okay? I want to see your masterpiece."

"Okay!" Nicky agreed, his excitement bubbling over.

As they continued chatting, Rio couldn’t help but feel a sense of joy from his enthusiasm. Moments like these made her realise how much she appreciated her growing friendship with Agatha and her son. She dwelled on it for a moment before she felt her phone vibrate. She stood up, swiftly took it out of her pocket and looked at her notifications.

Agatha: Hate to interrupt, but can you send Nicky over? Ralph’s almost here.

She briefly glances over at Nicky and tries to hide her disappointment. She could feel the resentment toward Ralph growing, feeling annoyed that he was getting in her way not once, but twice; first, with taking up Nicky’s second spot for the school recital, and now interrupting her time with him. She knew she didn’t have the right to feel this way, she wasn’t entitled to doing any of these things with Nicky, but she couldn’t help herself. She quickly typed a message in response before grabbing both of their coats, slipping her own on already. 

No worries. He’ll be right there.

“Hey, Nicky? It’s time to go…” she called out to Nicky, who went back to wandering in the store. He turned around and pouted sadly, not wanting their time together to end.

“Don’t be sad, you’re gonna be doing cool things with your dad! And you’ll see me again soon,” she reassured him, holding out his puffer jacket for him to slip his arm into, before turning him around and zipping it up for him. When he looked up, he noticed Rio was wearing her coat, too.

“I can walk back alone, Mama said so. I’m a big guy!” he said with a toothy grin. Rio chuckled but was determined to walk with him. She had seen how worried Agatha looked when he came over on his own, and she wanted to make an effort to calm her nerves. She felt a strange pull to show how attentive and thoughtful she could be. It felt stupid to want to impress the other woman this much, even in the most simple way, but Rio couldn’t help it. She had made up her mind from the moment he had walked into the shop: she’d bring him back personally. Being able to see Agatha at the cafe was a bonus, of course.

“Oh, I know you’re super grown-up,” she said with mock seriousness. “But I need my caffeine fix,” she used as an excuse and grabbed his hand, leading them out the door. Nicky didn’t look entirely convinced but shrugged in acceptance. He waited patiently as Rio locked up the shop, and grabbed onto her hand again before crossing the street.

Anxious for Nicky to walk over again, Agatha stood by the window, watching and waiting. When she saw not just one, but two people exit the store, she felt her heart swell unexplainably. The sight of the two of them walking together, hand in hand, moved her more than she had expected. She would’ve acted like she wasn’t staring, but she genuinely had been waiting for Nicky to come back safely, so she allowed herself to watch them.

When they reached the entrance, Rio used her free hand to push the door open and led the two of them inside. They were greeted immediately by Agatha, who looked at them with a gentle smile. Nicky immediately zoomed past her, rambling about everything he had learned from Rio. Agatha didn’t hear everything though, her attention partly focused on the other woman. She made eye contact with Rio and felt a fierce tug in her chest, pulling on her heartstrings. The florist stepped closer to her, smiling shyly.

“Hi Agatha,” she said, tucking her hair behind her ear.

“Hey,” Agatha greeted. “You didn’t have to walk with him,” her tone a mix of gratitude and mild scolding. “What about the shop?” she continued, looking out the window over Rio’s shoulder.

“Nah, that’s okay, it’s not that busy. Besides, I had to make sure he got here safely. So that, you know, you wouldn’t kill me,” she teased, but Agatha could tell that there was a genuine message there.

She wanted to say something witty, to tease her back, but there was no proper response besides gratitude at the kind gesture. She gave her a knowing look, the corner of her mouth twitching up.“Thanks, I appreciate it.”

Rio nodded in understanding, knowing how the woman had truly felt about the whole situation. She undid the buttons of her coat, letting it fall open to battle the sudden warmth inside the shop, and leaned against the counter casually. Agatha let her eyes look her up and down briefly, taking in the outfit hidden under the long black coat; today, the florist had been wearing a black pair of denim pants and a dark green turtleneck. It suited her, she thought to herself. Her eyes snapped back up to the other woman’s face when she heard her speak. “He was great, by the way. I’m surprised how much he remembered from last time, and how eager he is to learn even more.”

“Yeah, he never really gets enough, does he?” Agatha smiled, looking over at Nicky, who appeared to be sharing his stories with Billy this time, always looking for anyone willing to hear him out. Rio nodded, feeling a swell of affection for both mother and son. While Agatha looked at Nicky, Rio seized the opportunity to let her eyes roam the older woman’s body. She was wearing navy, tailored pants with a black button-up, buttons done all the way to the top, necklace sitting right in the gap of her collar. Her long waves were falling loosely down her back, and from this angle, Rio had a perfect view of her side profile. She took the time to appreciate her features, eyes drifting from the bridge of her nose to her lipstick-covered lips and her strong jaw. The other woman was stunning; Rio had always seen that, but the longer she looked at her, she found herself appreciating small features that she hadn’t noticed before.

“Is it time for your coffee yet?” Agatha asked the florist, already moving behind the counter to get started.

“You should know by now that I wouldn’t say no to that,” Rio replied cheekily.

Right when Agatha grabbed a mug for the order, the cafe door swung open, and Ralph stepped inside. As usual, he wore an outfit that was way too formal for someone not currently at work in a corporate office, wearing one of his many suits. His polished shoes clicked against the floor and his presence immediately shifted the energy in the room, though Rio worked hard to keep her expression neutral. Agatha stopped what she was doing and rounded the counter to greet him.

“Hey,” Ralph said, walking over to Agatha to meet her halfway. “Ready?”

Agatha nodded, motioning for Nicky to grab his bag from the back. As the boy disappeared, Ralph turned back to Agatha. “Why don’t you come meet us tonight, after the movies? Dinner would be nice—some family time.”

Her lips pressed into a thin line. “I can’t,” she said simply, her gaze firm.

Ralph frowned. “Why not?” he pressed, stepping closer to her and reaching out his hand to rest on her arm. Before he could touch her, though, Agatha took a small step back, her body just out of reach.

“Oh, come on, it’s not like you have plans.”

Agatha’s jaw tightened, but her tone stayed polite. Her walls were already incredibly high around Ralph, but she felt herself adding a few more layers of bricks for good measure. “My plans stopped being your business a long time ago, Ralph. I just can’t. Focus on your time with Nicky.”

Still standing only a few feet away, Rio clenched her jaw, her hands gripping the counter tightly. She forced herself to stay quiet, though her glare could have cut through steel. Lilia, noticing, raised an eyebrow but stayed silent, her curiosity piqued.

Before Ralph could press any further, Nicky reappeared with his bag slung over his shoulder. “Ready!” he said brightly, oblivious to the tension.

“Great,” his dad said, tone softening as he looked at his son.

Agatha bent down to hug Nicky, pressing a kiss to his cheek. “Be good, okay? I’ll see you tomorrow night.”

He nodded enthusiastically before taking Ralph’s hand. The two of them left, the door swinging shut behind them. It felt strange seeing him walk hand in hand with his dad now, whereas it felt so easy when she had watched him do the same with Rio. Everything had felt so much easier with Rio, which was incredibly scary. As the cafe grew quiet again, Agatha let out a slow breath, her shoulders sagging slightly. She turned back to the counter, busying herself with Rio’s coffee, her movements brisk and precise.

The atmosphere had shifted slightly, and everyone could feel it. Lilia exchanged a glance with Billy, who arched a questioning brow. Neither of them said anything at first, giving Agatha the space to work. Rio, still propped up against the counter, watched Agatha intently, concern flickering in her warm brown eyes.

Finally, Lilia broke the silence. “You okay, hon?” she asked gently, wiping her hands on a dish towel as she moved closer to Agatha.

“I’m fine,” she replied quickly, her tone clipped. She didn’t meet the older woman’s eyes, focusing instead on the espresso machine. “It’s nothing.”

Rio frowned at the response, resisting the urge to step closer and express her worry.

“Agatha…” Lilia pressed softly.

The cafe owner shook her head sharply, finally turning to hand Rio the coffee. “Here you go,” she said, her voice cool but not unkind. “Let’s not make a big deal out of it.” Agatha wasn’t sure if she was saying that to the others or herself, but she just wanted to move on with her day.

The words hung in the air for a moment, and Rio hesitated before accepting the mug. She wanted to press further, to tell Agatha that it was okay to open up, but she could see the tension in the other woman’s expression and decided against it. Instead, she offered a small, understanding smile that didn’t fully reach her eyes. “Thanks,” she said, attempting to keep her tone light. “Looks perfect as always.”

Billy, however, couldn’t help himself. “That guy,” he muttered under his breath, loud enough for everyone to hear. “He just has a way of walking into a room and making it worse, doesn’t he?”

Lilia shot him a warning look, but Rio didn’t stop him. If anything, her lips twitched in reluctant agreement.

Agatha however, turned to him with a sharp glare. “Billy, drop it, please,” she said firmly. Her tone wasn’t angry, but it carried enough weight that the teen immediately backed down, raising his hands in surrender.

“Sorry,” he mumbled, retreating behind the counter.

Lilia sighed, crossing her arms. “Agatha, honey, you don’t have to act like nothing’s wrong. If you need to talk—“

“I don’t,” Agatha interrupted, her voice softer now but still guarded. She turned away from them, busying herself with wiping down the counter even though it was already spotless. “Really, I’m fine. Let’s just... move on.”

Rio pushed her tongue against the inside of her cheek, her fingers tightening slightly around the warm mug in her hands. She didn’t like seeing Agatha like this—so closed off and distant. But she also knew that pushing too hard might only make things worse. Instead, she shifted her gaze to Agatha once again and spoke in a casual tone, hoping to lighten the mood.

“Well, Nicky told me all about his school recital. Can you believe the kid has two roles? That’s impressive.” she said, drawing Lilia and Billy’s attention. “Apparently, he’s the apple and the ice cream cone. He’s very proud of that.”

Agatha glanced over her shoulder, a flicker of warmth slowly returning to her expression. “Yeah, he’s been talking about it non-stop,” she admitted. “I think I’ve memorised the entire script just from hearing it so much,” she chuckled softly. She felt herself relax considerably, memories of her son always easing her mind. “He worked hard on those props, too.”

Rio smiled, seizing the opportunity to bring the focus back to something positive. She didn’t notice that Lilia looked at her fondly, appreciating the way she handled Agatha’s sour mood and was able to turn it around so easily. “He told me he made a leaf that looks like one of my plants. Can’t wait to see it.”

Billy perked up at this. “Nicky’s so talented,” he said brightly, clearly eager to shift the conversation away from Ralph. “He’s probably gonna steal the show.”

Lilia chuckled. “That kid’s got a lot of charm, just like his mom.” She shot Agatha a pointed look, her tone light but meaningful.

Agatha rolled her eyes, but her lips quirked into a faint smile. “Thanks, Lilia,” she said dryly.

The tension in the room began to ease, and the group settled into a more comfortable rhythm. But as Agatha returned to her tasks, Rio couldn’t help but steal glances at her, the worry still lingering in the back of her mind.

She didn’t know exactly what had happened between Agatha and Ralph, but one thing was clear: Agatha deserved better than whatever stress that man was causing her. And if Rio could help lighten her load—even just a little—she was determined to do so. She finished her coffee faster than she would’ve liked, but she had to return to her work eventually.

She stretched herself over the counter to place the mug by the sink before straightening her back. “I should get going, but I’ll still see you later, right?” she redid the buttons on her coat, getting ready to step out again.

The reminder caught Billy’s attention, but before he could say anything to piss Agatha off again, Lilia dragged him with her, pretending that the tables needed to be wiped.

At the mention of their plans, Agatha’s smile became more genuine. “Yeah, of course. Looking forward to it.”

“Me too,” Rio said with a warm smile, analysing Agatha’s face to look for hints of hesitation. There were none. “Are you sure you don’t want to come to mine instead? You already cooked for me last time…” she rubbed the back of her neck awkwardly, feeling bad that she didn’t get to repay the favour.

“It’s fine, don’t worry. I like cooking for people that don’t request dino nuggets every night. We can also order in if you prefer,” the older woman said, shrugging it off casually. In reality, she had preferred being in the comfort of her own home, as she was not really in the best mood. The idea of spending time at Rio’s place thrilled her, excited to see the little parts of her that were reserved for one’s safe space, but she wasn’t in the right headspace for it. Perhaps another time. The evening hadn’t even passed yet and Agatha already felt herself hoping that there would be a next time.

“Alright then, only if you’re sure. Good luck today, let me know if you need anything.”

“Thanks, you too. I’ll see you later, Vidal.”

“Te veo,” Rio said with one last look over her shoulder and exited the cafe.
