Chapter 6: Ice cream (you should have some to cool off)

Chapter Summary

Bless their friends for nudging (forcing) these idiots to spend time together.

9k words of (mostly) fluff.

Chapter Notes

warning: (implied) homophobia

still heaps of fluff, I swear

See the end of the chapter for more notes

The rest of the day passed by rather quickly, both shops on the street welcoming enough customers to keep them busy. In the back of their minds, they had been nervously thinking about the evening ahead of them, wondering what it was going to be like to finally hang out one-on-one. Agatha finished closing up the cafe and shot a quick text to the florist.

Agatha: Ready?

Rio: Do you mind if I finish up quickly? I can meet you at yours in 20

Agatha: All good, see you at home

Upon reading the word ‘home’, Rio felt her heart skip a small beat. She knew what Agatha meant; at her own home. Not theirs. But something about the sentence felt domestic, and the florist allowed herself to dwell on it a little longer.

She took a deep breath and returned to the chaos around her. She hadn’t been working on an order, but with the way her workstation looked right now, you’d think she had a last-minute wedding to cater to. She ran a hand through her hair and bit her lip, trying to decide what to do. She recalled her conversation with Agatha earlier, after Ralph had given her roses. The older woman had mentioned purple, tulips, and chocolate, she recounted. Tulips were not yet in season, so that was out of the equation. Purple, she could do, but which ones? She considered all her options, from flowers to plants and back to flowers.

It was normal to bring flowers for a casual hang-out, right? That was something even friends could do, right? She tried to picture what she’d do if she had had plans with Alice or Jen instead, concluding that she also wouldn’t show up empty-handed at their place. It was normal, no ulterior motives. Perfectly normal, as friends. Right.

Her eyes fell on the Phalaenopsis in a pot, among the plants she had received earlier today. She mentally skimmed all the facts she knew about the plant. The purple orchids would last Agatha a while, not wilting as quickly as regular flowers would. The plant bloomed from January until March, some even into May. The next year, they’d bloom again. Rio considered it’d be a good way to add more of Agatha’s favourite colour to her house. And if the long-lasting plant reminded Agatha of her connection to the florist for a longer period of time, that was just a welcome bonus. Platonically, or whatever. She was overthinking this way too much, she realised, and decided that this would work.

She carefully selected a dark green pot for the plant to sit in and headed out the door. After a quick stop at the convenience store next door, she headed to her car. With all her necessities collected, she made her way to Agatha’s.

She parked her car in front of the house, grabbed her tokens of appreciation, and walked to the wooden front door. Rio stood outside, plant awkwardly clutched in one arm, a small plastic bag hanging loosely from that same hand. She rang the doorbell with her free hand and it only took a few seconds until the door was opened by Agatha, who greeted her with an excited smile.

“Hey, come on in!” she said, stepping aside to let Rio —and her plant?— in. She eyed the florist curiously but didn’t comment on it.

“Thanks,” Rio mumbled softly, walking in quickly to set down her belongings before taking off her shoes and coat. She hung her coat on the rack by the door and turned to face Agatha. She wasn’t sure if she should give her a hug as a greeting, or just awkwardly stand there, waiting for her to make the next move. She settled for the latter.

She took in Agatha’s appearance; still in the same outfit as earlier, but her sleeves were rolled up as a result of cooking, showing off her forearms. The top few buttons of her blouse were now undone, showing off more skin, yet not blatantly inappropriate. Other people wouldn’t have noticed, but Rio did. She was very attentive to the small details — and this was a detail she loved discovering. Interesting.

“I hope you’re hungry, because I’m starving,” Agatha said. She walked past Rio, grabbed her arm, and guided her further into her house. Rio wanted to turn around to pick up her gifts but didn’t want Agatha to let go of her arm just yet. She’d come back for it in a minute, she told herself.

As they walked into the kitchen, Rio could hear the faint sound of music playing in the background and her nose caught the scent of a home-cooked meal. If she recognised it correctly, it was a mix of garlic, basil and cheese. “Whatever you’re making, it smells so delicious,” she complimented.

“Thanks,” Agatha responded shyly, quickly turning to the oven, hoping that the heat radiating off of it would cover her blush. “I made lasagna, it should be ready in about 5 minutes. Make yourself comfortable, please.”

“Yep, just, hold on one moment,” the florist responded. Agatha watched as she walked back to the hallway and reappeared with the mysterious plastic bag and plant from before.

“This is for you,” Rio said, placing the plant on the counter. She had practised what she would say, but the words seemed to fail her now. She awkwardly managed to stumble out a few words. “I know you like purple, and uh, yeah. I don’t know, thought it would be fitting. Didn’t wanna show up empty-handed.”

Agatha’s blue eyes softened, stepping closer to take a better look. A warmth spread through her chest. “Oh, you didn’t have to do that... But this is beautiful, Rio. Thank you.”

The sincerity in the older woman’s eyes made Rio’s cheeks flush. She held eye contact for as long as she could without melting on the spot. Then, in an attempt to lighten the mood, she reached for the plastic bag. Agatha’s eyes followed her movements, curious to see what she’d whip out.

With an endearingly mischievous grin, Rio held up two tubs of ice cream, proudly showing them off to Agatha. “And I brought these, since it’s tradition, or whatever. I didn’t wanna miss out.”

She was referring to Agatha’s tendency to spend her nights wallowing on the couch with ice cream and movies, information that Billy freely had exposed to everyone, to Agatha’s dismay. Agatha’s heart swelled at the gesture. She’d been insecure about the others finding out about her pathetic habit, worried that they’d ridicule her for it. But no, Rio managed to hold onto that piece of information and spin it in a way that made her feel normal, instead of it being a sign of loneliness and defeat. Rio wasn’t trying to change her, she wasn’t judging her, she simply offered her company so Agatha wouldn’t have to go through it alone. With each passing moment between the two, it was becoming harder and harder to keep her guard up. Damn.

Agatha chuckled, trying to mask how touched she was by the thoughtfulness of it all. If she allowed herself to express her feelings, she was worried she’d get emotional. Instead, she smirked, playing along. “Two tubs? Are you planning to outdo me on my own tradition? That’s ambitious.”

Rio smirked, handing the tubs over. “Hey, if I’m gonna do it, I’m gonna do it right. Besides, I needed backup in case you didn’t like my flavour.”

Agatha raised an eyebrow as she examined them. “What did you even bring?” She glanced at the labels. “Salted caramel and triple chocolate? Bold choices.”

“Bold is my middle name,” Rio shot back, leaning on the dining table. Agatha had already set the table, her favourite ceramic plates carefully placed across from each other, along with 2 glasses and a bottle of red wine.

“I don’t know if I should feel flattered or worried that you’re trying to give us both a heart attack,” Agatha said. There was probably way too much sugar in these, but who was she kidding, her own ice cream choices weren’t much better.

“You’ll be fine, Agatha. If that happens, I promise I’ll pull out my best CPR skills to save you. If I’m not already dead on the floor by then, that is,” she deadpanned.

Neither of them would admit at any point later in time that they were both imagining Rio’s hands on Agatha’s chest, possibly in a completely different scenario. That would be entirely inappropriate, of course.

“You better,” Agatha narrowed her eyes playfully. “Anyway, I’ll put these away for now. Dinner’s almost ready, and I refuse to let you ruin your appetite with dessert first.”

“Oh god, it’s so obvious that you’re a mother,” Rio laughed, her lips curling into a teasing smirk. “But I’ll listen this time. Only because your lasagna smells like it’s going to be the best thing I’ve eaten all year.”

I’ve got something else that could be the best thing you’ve eaten all year, if you want, Agatha thought to herself. She almost said it out loud, but stopped herself before it was too late. She wanted to test how far she could go with their banter, see if it could turn more teasing and playful, but she didn’t want to push too quickly. Small steps. Baby steps.

She stored the ice cream in the freezer, letting the cold air hit her face for a second longer than necessary, desperate to cool off her heat flush. The oven’s timer beeped, indicating that their meal was ready. She put on her oven mitts, took the dish out of the oven and placed it in the centre of the table.

“Please, sit down,” she told Rio, looking at the woman still awkwardly standing around the table. “Would you like some wine? I’ve also got sodas if you prefer not to drink.”

“Wine is perfect, thanks,” she responded with a smile, sitting down in the seat across from Agatha’s. “I drove here, but a glass or two should be alright.”

The older woman poured them both a generous glass of red wine. She held out her hand, gesturing for Rio to hand her the plate. Agatha placed a large serving of steaming hot lasagna on it before handing it back. She repeated the action for herself before settling in her seat.

When Rio took a bite of her food, an involuntary moan slipped from her lips, catching them both by surprise. Agatha’s gaze shot up at her, eyes widened ever so slightly and a faint blush made its way to her cheeks. Feeling embarrassed by the inappropriate sound she made, yet empowered by the other woman’s reaction, Rio spoke up quickly. “Sorry— this,” she said, swallowing her food before continuing — she had manners, after all. “This is so fucking good, Agatha.” She pointed at the lasagna with her fork, as if to emphasise it even more.

“I— um, thank you?” Agatha felt flustered and tried to regain her composure. “I’m glad you like it.” She took a sip of her wine, silently wishing it would’ve been appropriate to down the whole glass in one go.

“I don’t know why Nicky asks for dino nuggets when his mom’s secretly a chef,” she joked, enjoying Agatha’s reaction. The older woman was usually so confident, but seemingly could never take a compliment.

Agatha flushed, ducking her head to hide her smile. “Hardly. It’s just lasagna. Anyone can make it.”

“Oh, I don’t know, mine never comes out like this. It’s really good.”

“Well,” Agatha muttered, still pink in the cheeks, “you’re just being polite. But thank you.”

Rio smirked, seeing right through her modesty. “Oh, trust me, I’m not polite when it comes to food. I don’t play around. If it wasn’t good, I’d start plotting how to skip to dessert so I could eat both tubs of ice cream on my own.”

Agatha laughed softly, shaking her head. “Good to know I’ve avoided that fate, then.”

The conversation stayed light as they ate, touching on small, easy topics: a funny customer Rio had dealt with at the shop earlier in the week and Nicky convincing Agatha to reorganise her spice rack because it just didn’t make sense.

At one point, Rio paused mid-sentence, tilting her head slightly. “This playlist—is it yours?”

Agatha blinked as if just registering that she had put on music, and nodded. “Oh, I forgot it was still on. I usually just have something play when Nicky’s not here. The house feels… too quiet without him.”

Her voice softened at the end, and Rio felt a flicker of empathy for the quiet honesty in her words. She decided not to press further, not wanting to upset her. Instead, she simply nodded. “I get that. Sometimes a little background noise is the best company.”

“Yeah.” Agatha gave her a small, grateful smile. “But I must say, my current company is not so bad, either.”

It was Rio’s turn to blush this time. “I might not be as fun as Nicky, but I’m trying my best.”

“Well, it’s working,” she said, her piercing blue eyes staring right into Rio’s. Agatha was the first one to break eye contact, finishing off her glass of wine. “Anyway,” she said, standing up, “Let me clear the table and then—.”

“Nope,” Rio cut in, rising to her feet before Agatha could argue. “I’m helping. You cooked, I clean. It’s non-negotiable.”

Agatha sighed, rolling her eyes but unable to suppress her smile. “You’re stubborn, aren’t you?”

“Extremely,” Rio said with a grin, already gathering their plates.

The two of them moved around the kitchen with an easy rhythm, stacking dishes in the sink and wiping down counters. Rio’s hand brushed Agatha’s as they passed utensils back and forth, and their shoulders bumped gently when they both reached for the same dish towel. Each small touch sent little shocks of electricity through their bodies, yet it felt natural, like they’d been doing this for years instead of just one evening.

“Alright, that’s everything,” Agatha said, giving the counter one final wipe.

“Great,” Rio replied, grabbing the bottle of wine in one hand, and the two empty glasses carefully balanced in the other. She didn’t know what came over her, feeling extremely at ease in someone else’s house. She felt encouraged by the wine and their interactions from the entire day and decided to take more initiative. Agatha had already done so much for her. “I’ll take these. Let’s go get comfortable.”

Rio had been in Agatha’s house not too long ago, but she hadn’t really taken the time to examine the living room before. It was warm and inviting, with soft lighting —none of that overhead, big, bright lighting going on— and a few scattered throw blankets. She saw hints of Nicky’s presence too, a few toys here and there, but nothing that made it seem too cluttered. Everything looked like it belonged there, even the random action figure on the windowsill. It was obvious that the house was lived in, which only added to its charm.

Agatha sat down first, settling on one end of the couch. Rio followed, carefully sitting down with her hands still preoccupied, leaving a safe distance between the two women.

They both hesitated, the space between them feeling both too much and not enough. Rio, still feeling emboldened, shifted slightly. She refilled both glasses with wine and placed the bottle on the coffee table. When she sat back down, she tucked one leg under herself, angling her body to face Agatha more directly. “Here,” she said, handing her a glass.

Agatha took it, smiling faintly. “Thanks.”

They sipped their wine in silence for a moment, the quiet hum of the mellow playlist still playing softly in the background. The awkwardness began to melt away, replaced by a growing sense of comfort.

Rio placed her elbow on the back of the couch, propping her head up against her hand. “Thank you for tonight, Agatha. You’ve set the bar ridiculously high for dinner invitations. I’m kinda intimidated to return the favour now.”

“Well,” Agatha said, trying to sound casual even though she felt a flutter in her chest, “you don’t have to feel intimidated. I didn’t do anything special.”

“Okay, if you didn’t do anything special, are you hiding a secret Italian grandma somewhere?” she teased, looking around the room as if said grandma was playing hide-and-seek. “Because then you should definitely let her come over to my place sometime.”

Agatha snorted, relaxing on the couch. Rio decided that little laugh was the most adorable thing she’d ever heard. It was now her mission to draw it out of Agatha as often as possible. “No secret grandma, just a recipe I found online one day and never stopped using. It’s nothing fancy, I promise.”

“Stop downplaying it,” Rio said, mock stern. “You could just accept the compliment, you know.”

Agatha raised her hands in mock surrender, chuckling. “Fine, fine. I’ll take the compliment.”

“Good.” Rio leaned back, grinning. “Because I’m not done giving them. That little snort-laugh thing you do is adorable. How come I’m only just now finding out about this.”

The older woman laughed again and hid her face behind her hands, feeling embarrassed that she had let that slip. She hated it when her laughter made her snort. “Oh god, please don’t start— I hate it.”

Rio leaned forward, eyes widening in exasperation. “No way, how could you hate that? It’s so cute.” She hesitated before continuing, gaze turning soft as she made eye contact with Agatha. “And it feels good to have made you laugh so freely.”

Agatha received hit after hit at this point, not sure how it was possible for her cheeks to grow even hotter than before, but she still felt it. The warmth now covered her cheeks, her neck, and deep inside her chest.

“Okay, you have to stop now. I can only accept one compliment every 5 business days, I don’t have more in me,” she tried to shrug off the comments. As much as she appreciated the florist’s words, she always felt uneasy when someone said anything remotely positive about her. She wasn’t used to it, nor did she feel deserving of it.

“Good thing it’s a weekend then,” Rio deadpanned. “But fine, I’ll go easy on you. Maybe you should have some of that ice cream to help you cool off.” She winked at Agatha, happy to have the upper hand for a change. Seeing how much the other woman was affected by her made her feel hopeful. Maybe there was a chance after all.

Agatha rolled her eyes in mock annoyance. She took another sip of wine, but she couldn’t hide the small smirk that tugged at her lips. “If you want me to save you some, you better watch your mouth, Vidal.”

“You wouldn’t dare!”

“Watch me,” Agatha teased, standing up from the couch and walking to the kitchen. And Rio did. She watched. She shamelessly watched as Agatha’s figure moved—was that sway of her hips always there, or was it laid on extra thick tonight?

Rio caught herself staring at the way Agatha’s clothes clung just right, and shook her head with a quiet chuckle, leaning back into the couch.

Alright, Rio, calm down, she thought, inhaling deeply. Everything’s been going great. Don’t fuck it up now.

She glanced around the room again, taking in more of the personal touches that made it feel so distinctly Agatha: a few framed photos of her and Nicky, a collection of books stacked on a shelf, and a small collection of various candles scattered around the room.

Agatha returned a moment later, two large bowls of ice cream in hand, her expression warm but casual. She handed one to Rio and settled back onto the couch, slightly closer than before. Unintentionally, of course.

“I got you some scoops of each flavour, hope that’s okay,” she said, turning to face her again.

“It’s perfect,” Rio said with a grin. Her eyes twinkled mischievously as she continued, wiggling her eyebrows. “So, what’s the bad movie of the night? You know, the next step in your tradition.”

Agatha smirked, tucking her legs under herself. “We can skip the movie, but the ice cream stays. Honestly, talking to you is a nice change of pace. I’m enjoying this.”

Rio’s grin softened into something more genuine. “Me too.”

“So, Miss green thumb, what’s the best place to put my new plant?” she asked, looking around the living room.

“It’s called a Phalaenopsis. Also known as a moth orchid.” She explained it as if Agatha had the same curiosity as Nicky. She loved sharing details about her plants.

The florist considered the ideal location carefully, eyes settling on the windowsill next to the couch.

“This window faces east, which works well generally,” she said matter-of-factly. “It’ll bloom nicely there, and you’ll be able to see it when you’re just lounging here.”

With a small nod, Agatha showed her approval. She looked at Rio for a moment, another question forming in her head. “Why’d you choose this one? If I’m allowed to ask.”

Feeling shy all of a sudden, yet excited to talk about her passion, Rio’s eyes twinkled. “Well, Nicky made it abundantly clear that you love purple,” she gave her a knowing look. Agatha chuckled, unable to deny that one. “And I, uh, wanted something that’d last you for a while, I guess?” she added shyly, avoiding eye contact. The older woman just hummed, encouraging her to go on.

“These orchids bloom from now until maybe April or May, so you’ll get to enjoy them much longer than flowers. And they bloom again next year,” she continued. She met Agatha’s soft gaze and allowed herself to open up more about her intentions. “I figured, with Nicky not being around all the time, maybe it’d brighten up the space a bit, you know? And also, I personally like that it blooms year after year. It’s a reminder that the good things will come back to you eventually, even when it feels empty.”

She searched Agatha’s face, worried that maybe she’d pushed too far. All she saw, however, was pure admiration. The woman was undoubtedly touched by the words and the gesture. “That— wow, I don’t know what to say... Thank you, Rio, that means a lot."

“I’m here for you. So, if you feel uneasy about not having him here, know that you can just give me a call.” Agatha nodded, still looking for words to express her feelings. Her cheeks were warm and her heart was beating erratically. Rio wasn’t doing much better, equally affected by her own openness.

They ate their ice cream quietly for a moment before Rio glanced over, her tone even gentler now. “How are you feeling about Nicky being with his dad? Are you okay?”

Agatha hesitated, scooping up another bite. She sighed softly. “It’s always hard. Ralph’s not exactly the most dependable person. I know he tries in his own way, but it’s frustrating. I want better for Nicky, you know?”

Rio nodded, her eyes full of empathy. “You’re doing an amazing job, Agatha. He’s such a bright, happy kid. That’s a reflection of you.”

The older woman smiled faintly, the compliment clearly touching her. “Thanks. Although I’m not sure everyone would agree.” She let out a soft sigh, distracting herself with another bite.

“It shouldn’t matter what other people think. I can see it clearly, and I don’t even know you that well. You just seem to, I don’t know…” she hesitated, searching Agatha’s face to see if it was okay to continue. “You seem to have your guard up a lot of the time. Maybe that’s why people aren’t seeing it.” Rio decided that honesty was the best policy, and hoped she wasn’t crossing any lines.

“Yeah, I mean, you’re not wrong. I do. I’ve had my walls up for so long I don’t remember what it’s like without it, if I’m being honest.” Agatha didn’t know whether it was Rio’s calming presence or the wine or just the fact that she’d kept it all bottled up for so long, but she found herself opening up to the other woman. “The one time I let someone in, not even whole-heartedly, just out of convenience, he broke that trust right away. But it started way before that.”

“My mother, ugh— I hate calling her that. She doesn’t deserve that title,” she said, lips pressed into a thin line. “Evanora made me feel like I wasn’t deserving of love. She didn’t exactly accept who I was. She’d tear me down at every chance she got, emotionally and sometimes physically. Nothing was ever good enough. Said I was evil, even.”

Rio’s chest tightened at the revelation, but she stayed quiet, letting Agatha continue.

“That’s why I’m so guarded,” Agatha admitted. “Because deep down, I still feel like I don’t deserve love. When I met Ralph, I was desperate for Evanora’s approval and thought I could just use him as a shield, hiding the real me and protecting me from my mother’s harsh judgment. I figured that tolerating a person would suffice, I didn’t need to love him.”

Hearing the woman talk about love this way tugged at Rio’s heart. Nobody should settle for anything less than a devoted partner. Agatha deserved so much more than the cards she was dealt. This only justified her resentment toward Ralph more, and she added another enemy to her mental list: Evanora Harkness.

“Of course, it still wasn’t good enough. Her comments would be a bit more mild, but they were still always there. She’d say that she didn’t understand how Ralph could be with me, and that I should make sure he doesn’t leave me. So you can guess who she blamed when he cheated,” she rolled her eyes and huffed.

“Typical,” Rio scoffed, feeling angry at the story. “I’m so sorry, Agatha. You didn’t deserve that.” She wanted to say so much more, but she could tell Agatha wasn’t done sharing yet. Now that she had finally started opening up, Rio didn’t want to do anything to get in the way of that.

“In the end, it worked out. I got Nicky out of it, and I wouldn’t change that for the world. He changed everything for me. I want to be better for him. I have to be better for him.” Her voice grew firmer, her resolve shining through. “I refuse to let him feel what I felt growing up. That’s why it hurts so much when Ralph doesn’t meet those expectations. It feels like I have to be twice the parent to make up for it.”

Rio reached out, her fingers brushing Agatha’s wrist lightly. “You’re not just doing a great job—you’re giving Nicky everything you didn’t have. That’s no small thing. That’s incredible.”

Agatha looked down at her bowl, the ice cream long forgotten. “It’s hard to let people in when I feel like I have to carry all of this on my own. I’m terrified that if I show anyone the chaos that comes with me, they’ll leave.”

The florist wanted so badly to reach for Agatha’s face, lifting it so she could look her in the eye. She resisted, feeling intimidated by the added intimacy it would bring, and hoped her words would convey the message regardless. “Agatha, you shouldn’t have to put on a front to be loved. You deserve someone who wants all of you—the good, the messy, the chaos, and everything in between. You shouldn’t be some version of yourself that’s easier for them. That’s the only way it can work.” Rio’s voice was soft but steady.

Agatha’s throat tightened at the sincerity in her voice. She gave a small nod, but the weight of the conversation was too much. She leaned back, deflecting slightly. “Enough about me. What about you, what’s your story? Why are Alice and Jen so set on getting you back out there?”

Rio chuckled lightly, fidgeting with the small spoon in her hand. “I haven’t been in a relationship in a long time. My last one… well, I loved hard. Gave it everything I had. Once I’m in, I go all in. I don’t do anything half-heartedly, ever. But my ex wasn’t ready for that. She felt smothered, I guess. Instead of talking about it, she packed her things and left. Just like that.”

“Just like that?” Agatha said with surprise written all over her face. She couldn’t imagine someone doing that to Rio. How anyone could be unappreciative of Rio’s care was beyond her. “I’m sorry, that must’ve been really shitty.”

“Yup, just like that,” Rio shrugged, trying to seem nonchalant. “It took me a long time to recover. Felt like I was too much, you know? Like maybe loving hard was a flaw.”

She took another small bite of her ice cream, or whatever was left of it anyway. The small scoop that was still left slowly melted as the conversation continued.

“And if I can’t love hard, can’t love fully, it’s not worth it to me. I don’t want it if it’s half-assed. When I finally started dating again, I tried to take a step back, to not go all-in immediately. But it just wasn’t me. There’s no middle ground for me when it comes to love or relationships, so if that’s my flaw then so be it.”

It made sense to Agatha, the picture that Rio was painting of herself. It was obvious that Rio had a big heart and held a lot of love for the people around her. It was a shame that she felt like that had to be diminished to fit into people’s lives.

“It’s not,” Agatha said firmly, leaning forward slightly. “A flaw, I mean. Your loud love—that’s one of the best things about you. I can see it in how you treat the people around you. Your friends, Nicky.. even me.” Her voice softened. “The right person will see how special that is. And they’ll cherish it.”

Rio’s breath caught for a moment, but she smiled, her cheeks warming. “Thanks, Agatha.”

For a moment, they sat there, the weight of their shared vulnerability hanging in the air. Neither of them said it aloud, but they both felt the connection growing stronger between them. It had been a while since either of them had opened up this much, to anyone. But it had felt so safe to do so.

The moment lingered, and they held eye contact; their eyes holding a private conversation between them. Unspoken, yet understood. Agatha wasn’t a physically affectionate person, but she felt a strong pull to reach out—to comfort her, touch her, to do something, anything at all.

The ring of the doorbell shattered the fragile stillness between the two women. Both of them jumped slightly, startled. Agatha immediately felt a pang of dread in her chest, her heart picking up speed. She set her empty bowl down and hurried to the door.

When she opened it, Ralph stood on the doorstep with Nicky beside him, his little face red and streaked with tears. Ralph’s expression was a mix of frustration and impatience.

"Nicky? What are you doing here?" Agatha asked, her voice laced with concern as she crouched to her son’s level.

Upon hearing Nicky’s name, Rio hurriedly made her way to the hallways as well. She appeared behind Agatha, curious about the interruption, taking in the scene with quiet attentiveness. She hovered in the hallway, close enough to step in if needed, but far enough to not fully intrude.

Nicky sniffled, clutching at Agatha’s shirt. "I forgot Señor Scratchy," he whimpered. "I couldn’t sleep without him."

The name stopped Rio in her tracks. Señor Scratchy. The nickname she’d given his stuffed rabbit. Her heart swelled as she glanced at Agatha, silently wondering if she had noticed too. Agatha, at the same time, made a mental note to bring up the new nickname later.

Ralph huffed, crossing his arms. "He’s been crying about it for the past hour, refusing to sleep. I texted you, Agatha, but apparently, you were too busy to care."

Agatha bristled at his tone, her protective instincts flaring. "I didn’t see your messages, doesn’t mean I don’t care.”

Ralph snapped, his eyes darting past her to Rio. "I’m guessing she’s the reason you were too busy to check your phone?"

Rio tensed, but her expression stayed neutral. She subconsciously felt her muscles tense, as if her body was preparing her to step in.

"Ralph, don’t start," Agatha said sharply, keeping her voice low so Nicky wouldn’t be more upset. She stood up and placed a hand on Nicky’s back. "We’re not doing this in front of him."

“Come on, baby,” she said to him, taking Nicky upstairs. Rio and Ralph were left alone in the hallway. She carried him to his room, where he immediately dove into bed, clinging to Señor Scratchy as if his life depended on it. She sat beside him, running her fingers through his hair.

"It’s okay, sweetheart. Scr— Señor Scratchy’s here now,” she quickly corrected herself, using the new nickname. “You’re safe."

Nicky began to relax under her soothing words, his breathing evening out. "Mama, what’s Rio doing here?"

Agatha hesitated, caught off guard by the question. "She came over for dinner. We were just talking, that’s all."

“That’s nice.” Nicky’s lips curved into a small smile. “Can she come say goodnight later?"

Agatha kissed his forehead. "Of course. I’ll send her up before she leaves."

Once Nicky was settled, Agatha reluctantly made her way downstairs, her heart heavy with the thought of dealing with Ralph. As she descended, she heard the unmistakable sound of Rio and Ralph arguing.

“So, you are the reason we didn’t get to spend time as a family tonight,” she overheard Ralph say.

Rio scoffed. “Please, what family time? Maybe if you put in the effort to be a present father, you wouldn’t be in this situation. Don’t blame me for your failures.”

"You’re the one who’s distracting her," Ralph retorted.

"You think this is my fault?" Rio didn’t back down, voice sharp yet calm.

“Yes, and you know what— she probably did this on purpose, so she’d have Nicky gone long enough to get some alone time with you, only to have him come back home at the end of the night.” he spat out. He looked at her with a look of disgust. It was a look Rio recognised; as a lesbian, it was one that she had received many times. She knew what he was angry at, and her nostrils flared.

“You can say whatever you want about me, but I draw the line at questioning Agatha’s intentions. She wants nothing but the best for Nicky.” Rio kept her voice soft enough to not startle Nicky if he overheard anything, but her tone was undeniably firm. “I’d consider your next words very carefully if I were you.”

Agatha stepped into the hallway just as Ralph sputtered in indignation.

“I don’t even understand why he’s so attached to that thing,” he refused to give up. He looked at Agatha, who had appeared behind Rio. “You coddle him too much, he’s becoming too dependent.”

"Ralph," she said, her voice stern. "This isn’t the time or place. And for the record, Nicky is six years old. If he needs his stuffed rabbit to feel safe, that’s perfectly normal."

Rio squared her shoulders and added, "Agatha’s right. You’re being unreasonable. He’s a kid, cut him some slack.” She subconsciously took a small step forward, shifting her body ever so slightly, as if forming a shield in front of Agatha. It was a small gesture, not even fully aware she was doing it. Her first and foremost instinct was to physically protect Agatha from Ralph’s harsh words.

Agatha’s chest warmed with gratitude at Rio’s defence. She found it endearing—and admittedly, incredibly attractive—how fiercely she stood up for her and Nicky.

“Of course you agree with her, you’d do anything to get in her pants,” he huffed. Ralph shifted his focus to Agatha, who clenched her jaw. “I remember your mother warning me about this, Agatha.”

Rio had heard enough bits and pieces of information throughout the night to complete the puzzle; everything fell into place. She knew what he was insinuating, and she knew how hard the words would hit Agatha on a barely healed wound. She didn’t even bother to respond to his inappropriate comment, because that wasn’t the point right now. She shook her head, tongue working against the inside of her cheek. “I think it’s best that you leave now, Ralph. And trust me, you don’t want me to repeat myself.”

The older woman had never seen this side of Rio before. She stood tall, confident, firm. Like a bear protecting its cubs. It became clear to everyone involved that Rio wasn’t messing around. This was one of those moments in which Rio loved loudly and would do anything for the people she cared about. Agatha could tell in the way the muscles in her arms had visibly flexed, her fist clenched tightly. She knew that Rio wouldn’t purposely do anything to escalate the situation, but she was ready to act if needed. Still, Agatha placed her hand softly against the back of Rio’s arm, a gentle reminder that she could relax. She felt Rio’s tricep lose some of the tension, but she still looked ready to attack.

Ralph finally threw up his hands. "Fine. Whatever. I’m going home."

Without another word, he stormed out, the door slamming shut behind him.

Agatha sighed heavily and Rio turned to face her. She let her hand linger, now holding onto Rio’s other arm, rubbing it gently. "Thank you. For standing up for me. And for him."

Rio’s features softened. Her hand reached for Agatha’s, the one that was now placed on her arm, squeezing gently. "Of course. You shouldn’t have to deal with that alone."

Agatha smiled faintly. Then she remembered her son upstairs and his request. "Nicky asked for you. He wants you to come say goodnight.”

They went upstairs together, Rio following closely behind Agatha. As they stepped into Nicky’s room, the soft glow of his bedside lamp cast a warm, golden hue over everything. Nicky’s face brightened the moment Rio entered, his small hands still clutching Señor Scratchy tightly to his chest. He looked so small and vulnerable under the covers, tear streaks faintly visible, and both Rio and Agatha felt a tug in their hearts.

Rio hesitated for a moment at the edge of the bed, unsure of how to proceed. She’d been in his room before, she’d even been in his bed before, but the circumstances were entirely different. With this tense situation, and the sensitivity of it all, she didn’t want to overstep. She felt protective over both of them, but she didn’t know if it was justified. She glanced at Agatha, silently asking for permission, and Agatha gave her a gentle nod of encouragement.

Rio smiled softly and sat down carefully on the side of the bed, not wanting to disturb Nicky. “Hey, buddy. I heard you finally embraced Señor Scratchy’s new name, huh?” she said, her voice light and playful.

Nicky giggled softly, his face still damp but now lit up with happiness. “Yeah, it’s sophisticated.”

Upon hearing this word, Agatha and Rio shared a look; the mother wordlessly understood that this must’ve been part of their conversations at the flower shop. It warmed her heart that Rio had been teaching him new things, in so many ways.

“Yes, it is,” Rio laughed.

“I couldn’t sleep without him,” he confessed, feeling embarrassed after the whole ordeal. No child should have to feel insecure about something so normal.

“Well,” Rio replied, “I don’t think Señor Scratchy could sleep without you either. He told me he missed you just as much.”

Nicky beamed, his earlier sadness fading away. This was the second time today that Rio had managed to flip a Harkness’s mood around so effortlessly, having done the same at the cafe earlier. Agatha had noticed, too, her fondness for the other woman ever-growing.

“Will you stay for a little while?” he asked, looking between the two women with hopeful eyes. Those big brown eyes, Agatha’s weakness.

Rio glanced at Agatha again, her expression shy and a bit unsure, but Agatha gave her a small smile. “Of course,” Agatha said, her voice soft. She moved to the bed and stopped next to Rio, giving her an encouraging pat on the shoulder, urging her to move over.

Just like last time, Rio settled in between the wall and Nicky. She eased herself onto the bed, lying on her side so she could face Nicky. He scooted closer to her, making room for his mother next to him. Agatha mirrored Rio from the other side, the two of them unintentionally creating a protective cocoon around the little boy. The bed was small, meant for a child, so when they all settled in, it brought them close—closer than either of them had anticipated.

The proximity was intimate and unfamiliar, feeling different than when they had read his book together, and both women felt a flicker of shyness. Agatha focused on adjusting the blanket over Nicky’s shoulders, while Rio kept her gaze on Nicky’s face, afraid to look up and meet Agatha’s eyes.

The weight of the moment was palpable. The faint scent of Agatha’s perfume mixed with the faint floral sweetness of Rio’s shampoo, creating a blend that made the closeness even more noticeable. Their hands accidentally brushed as they both reached to tuck the blanket tighter around Nicky.

“Sorry,” Rio whispered, her cheeks warming as she quickly drew her hand back.

“It’s okay,” Agatha murmured, her voice soft. There was a slight tremor in it, betraying her own awareness of how close they were. They had shared a few brief touches throughout the evening—in the kitchen and in the hallway after they spoke to Ralph—but none of it felt as intimate as the situation they found themselves in right now; in bed together, consoling the child between them, hearts still heavy from their interaction with Ralph.

Nicky turned to his mother, eyeing her curiously. Agatha looked back at him, waiting for him to voice what was on his mind. She didn’t know what she expected him to say, but it wasn’t whatever words followed next.

“Mama,” he started, looking between her and Rio, eyes narrowed. “Can I ask you something? But you have to be honest.”

Both women felt their hearts race, worried about where this was going. If he was going to question what was going on between them, neither of them would know what to say. They’re friends, right? That would be the only reasonable answer, right?

“Okay, sure. What is it, honey?” Agatha replied, not showing her nervousness at the question. She glanced briefly at Rio, whose shoulders seemed to be less relaxed than before. Cool, she was nervous too, then.

“Did you and Rio…” he started, eyes widening in realisation, “have ice cream without me today?”

As soon as the words rolled off his tongue, Agatha snorted in laughter. This kid was going to be the death of her. Rio laughed as well, confirming Nicky’s suspicion.

“Mama! That’s not fair, you said I wasn’t allowed too many sweets!” he said, clearly offended by his mother’s betrayal. Rio laughed even harder, and he burst into giggles as well.

“How did you know?” Agatha asks, still snort-laughing.

“You spilled some on your shirt,” he said matter-of-factly.

Agatha looked down, and there it was, a small stain from the salted caramel ice cream that had dried up on her blouse. The kid had a good eye for detail, that was certain.

He turned to Rio now, as serious as a child could be. “I can’t believe this.”

“Sorry, kiddo. Next time we’ll eat it together, okay?” she ran her fingers through his hair instinctively, her brown eyes meeting his.

“Promise?”

“If your Mama allows us, then yes, I promise.” She glanced at Agatha, trying to stay out of trouble with her.

“Tomorrow?” he pushed his luck.

“I don’t know about tomorrow, but definitely soon, alright?” Rio didn’t know about the family’s plans for the next day, and she didn’t want to intrude. She felt like an intruder in this intimate setting and didn’t want to overstep more than she already had. If he had asked her, though, she would’ve said yes in a heartbeat.

Nicky accepted her answer and nestled deeper into his bed. His little hand reached out to rest on Rio’s arm, his other clutching the stuffed rabbit tightly. Agatha’s heart swelled at the sight, a rare sense of peace settling over her. The tense ending of the evening was slowly being overshadowed by this moment. All that mattered to her was that Nicky was safe. And he was. Safely tucked between her and Rio.

Rio felt the weight of Nicky’s small hand and glanced up briefly at the other woman, finally finding the courage to look at her. Their eyes met for just a moment, and Agatha offered a small, grateful smile. It was a fleeting but profound connection, an unspoken understanding passing between them.

As Nicky’s breathing started to slow, the silence in the room grew comfortable. The bed was small, and now and then, their hands would brush lightly as they took turns stroking Nicky’s hair, arm and back. The feeling sent tiny sparks of awareness through both women and neither of them moved away.

Agatha shifted slightly, propping her head up with her hand. The motion caused her knee to bump against Rio’s, now fully enclosing Nicky’s small body in the cocoon formed by the two women. Rio stilled for a moment before relaxing again. The touch lingered, neither of them acknowledging it but not pulling away either.

Nicky, now drowsy with sleep but still holding on to his newfound happiness, mumbled, “I’m glad you’re both here.”

Rio’s heart melted, and she reached out to gently pat his hand. “Me too, buddy,” she said softly.

Agatha glanced at Rio, her expression tender. There was something so natural about this moment, so effortless, that it made her chest ache. It was something she hadn’t realised she’d been longing for until now.

As Nicky drifted off to sleep, the two women stayed where they were, not wanting to disturb the tranquil atmosphere. Agatha’s hand rested near Nicky’s head, playing with his hair occasionally, and Rio’s fingers lightly grazed the edge of the blanket. Though the space was small, it didn’t feel cramped. It felt safe, warm, and—for the first time in a long time for both of them—complete.

After a while, both women realised they couldn’t stay in Nicky’s bed forever, no matter how much they wanted to preserve the moment. Rio’s attempt at preservation was taking the feeling she had right now and locking it safely into her heart, stowed neatly next to her other interactions with the two. She would hold on to this feeling for a long time.

They exchanged a look, the silent agreement to move, and carefully began easing themselves out of the small bed. Agatha was the first to rise, gently adjusting the blanket over Nicky. She leaned down and placed a tender kiss on his forehead, whispering, “Goodnight, sweetheart.”

Rio hesitated for a moment, eyes trained on the blanket covering the child. The last time she had been in this situation, she had decided against following her instincts and didn’t kiss his forehead, afraid to overstep. However, the entire evening had been so charged, her protectiveness over the child being more fierce than ever. She had grown to care about him deeply in a short amount of time. Her eyes darted to Agatha, who only watched her with a soft yet curious expression. Rio leaned down, her heart pounding in her chest. Who would’ve thought she’d ever be so nervous over something so small?

She placed a gentle kiss on Nicky’s forehead, her movements shy and tentative, as though testing her boundaries. “Goodnight, buddy.”

As she pulled back, she glanced at Agatha again, unsure of what to expect—disapproval, or confusion, maybe? Instead, Agatha’s eyes held something else entirely. Something warm and appreciative. If anything, Agatha found the gesture incredibly endearing, though she didn’t voice it. It was written all over her face, anyway.

They stepped out of Nicky’s bedroom, closing the door softly behind them. They made their way downstairs in comfortable silence. In the living room, they began gathering the empty ice cream bowls and wine glasses left on the coffee table, the earlier events of the evening hanging in the air between them.

Agatha broke the silence first. “Are you okay?” she asked, her tone gentle, with an edge of insecurity.

Caught off guard, Rio looked at her with a faint smile. “Me? I should be asking you that. After all, it was your ex who decided to show up with a temper.” This was her way of telling the other woman that yes, she was okay. She was only worried about Agatha.

The older woman shook her head, a faint smile tugging at her lips. “I’m okay,” she said, setting one of the bowls in the sink. “Mostly because of how you handled everything. You were amazing tonight, Rio. With Ralph, with Nicky… everything. I don’t know what I would’ve done if you hadn’t been here.”

Rio felt a blush creeping up her neck. “It wasn’t a big deal. I couldn’t let him say those things about you guys. I hope I didn’t overstep, though. You can tell me if I did…”

“You didn’t,” Agatha reassured, her voice firm yet kind. “Not at all. If anything, you were exactly what we needed tonight.”

They fell into a comfortable rhythm, tidying up together. The clinking of the wine glasses and the sound of running water in the sink filled the quiet space. The atmosphere felt warm despite the tension that had unfolded earlier.

As they finished, Agatha leaned against the counter and glanced at Rio. “Are you okay to drive home? Or… if you’d rather, you can stay here tonight,” she offered. “I—I have a guest bedroom,” she added quickly, her cheeks colouring slightly. “Just to be clear. No pressure. You don’t have to, obviously. I just thought—“

Rio smiled, cutting off Agatha’s ramble with a wave of her hand. “Thank you, but I think I’ll head home. Maybe another time.”

Agatha nodded, both relieved and a little disappointed. The idea of waking up with Rio still in her house excited her, but she also appreciated the space to reflect on everything that had happened. Rio had weighed her options carefully and came to the same conclusion.

When they reached the front door, Rio paused, turning to face Agatha. “Thank you for tonight. Despite, you know... I had a good time.”

Agatha smiled warmly. “Me too. And thank you. For everything. You don’t even know how much I appreciate it.”

Rio hesitated for a moment, then stepped forward, wrapping Agatha in a warm hug. Agatha returned it without hesitation, feeling comforted by the gesture. Her hand instinctively found its way up, settling over Rio’s hair, right at the nape of her neck. As they pulled back, Rio hesitated again before leaning in and placing a soft kiss on Agatha’s cheek, torturously close to the corner of her mouth.

The touch was light, barely there, but it sent warmth rushing through Agatha. She felt the blush creeping up her cheeks and smiled shyly at Rio, her heart thudding in her chest.

“Goodnight, Agatha,” Rio said softly, her voice full of affection. Her warm brown eyes stared into Agatha’s blue ones, trying to memorise every small detail.

“Goodnight, Rio. Text me when you get home,” Agatha replied as Rio stepped out into the night. She lingered at the door for a moment, watching Rio walk to her car. When Rio turned back and gave her a little wave, Agatha waved back, a smile tugging at her lips.

As she closed the door, Agatha leaned against it. Her skin burned hot where Rio’s lips had touched her cheek. She let out a soft breath, her thoughts swirling with everything that had happened.

On her short drive home, Rio couldn’t help but replay the evening in her mind. Her chest felt light, her heart full. Despite the challenges they had faced during the night, they had navigated them easily, together. It had been a night neither of them would forget.

When Rio reached her apartment, she allowed all of her feelings to fully envelop her. She walked to the kitchen to grab a glass of cold water, which she downed in one go. She whipped her phone out of her pocket, and opened the chat with Agatha, ignoring the messages in her group chat with Jen and Alice inquiring about her night. She’d update them later.

I’m home!

The older woman must’ve been waiting for her text because the response came immediately.

Agatha: Good

Agatha: Sleep well, Rio

Sweet dreams, Agatha

That night, both of them went to bed thinking about how it felt when they were in Nicky’s bed, all squeezed together. They held onto that feeling, remembering how their scents mixed due to the proximity, and how their skin tingled each time they touched, no matter how brief. Rio remembered how Agatha’s skin felt under her lips, and Agatha’s cheek still burned.
