Chapter 7: (definitely not) out of shape

Chapter Summary

“It’s just ridiculous,” she lamely tried to defend herself, clearly flustered.

Her best friend raised an eyebrow, giving her a knowing look. “What is?”

“Does that look out of shape to you? How dare she say that?” she huffed, crossing her arms.

Chapter Notes

I've been feeling more inspired for this fic than for my actual job. oh well.

between my office job, reading fics & writing this, my screen time is getting ridiculous. no regrets tho I <3 agathario

See the end of the chapter for more notes

The next morning, Agatha was stirred from her sleep by the sound of little feet padding across the hardwood floor. Moments later, Nicky climbed into her bed, clutching Señor Scratchy tightly. Without a word, he nestled himself against her, his head resting on her shoulder.

“Morning, sweetheart,” she murmured sleepily, wrapping her arm around him.

“Morning, Mama,” he replied in a small voice, still groggy from sleep.

Agatha pressed a kiss to his forehead, her fingers moving absentmindedly to comb through his messy hair. The action made her think of the night before. The image of Nicky’s tear-streaked face flashed in her mind, and her heart clenched. She hated how embarrassed Ralph had made him feel, and the guilt she carried for not noticing Ralph’s messages weighed heavily on her.

Then her thoughts shifted to Rio. She recalled how protective Rio had been, standing up for them without hesitation. The way Rio had defended both Nicky and Agatha filled her with gratitude, and something more. A warmth she hadn’t felt in a long time. It was a weird feeling. She had always taken pride in being independent, not needing anyone other than her son. The need to not rely on anyone else had made her distant, and she’d even be annoyed when people tried to meddle with her life. Her personal business was exactly that: personal business. But for some reason, it had felt awfully comforting to have someone by her side. Agatha couldn’t help but think it was only comforting because it was Rio. The older woman hadn’t needed her to stand up for them, but it felt good for a change. It felt good to not always have to be the protector, but to be looked after.

Agatha’s lips curved into a faint smile as she remembered their easy banter over dinner, the comfort of sitting so close to Rio on the couch, the shy kiss Rio had placed on her cheek at the end of the night. The memory brought a faint blush to her cheeks. She wished she could’ve turned her head ever so slightly, just to feel the lips against hers. This made her cheeks burn even hotter, though Nicky, too engrossed in Señor Scratchy, didn’t notice.

The thought of seeing Rio again filled her with a mixture of anticipation and nervousness. She wanted to ask her to spend more time together, but a part of her hesitated. What if she was pushing too much? Was it selfish to ask her to spend their entire weekend together? Maybe it was better if they wouldn’t see each other today, Agatha reasoned. It would give Rio space. She didn’t want to overwhelm her with her presence, and surely she must’ve had better things to do.

She pushed away the thoughts and turned her attention back to her son, who was comfortably tucked in the crook of her neck. “I’m sorry about last night, Nicky,” she began gently. “I didn’t see your dad’s message. If I had, I would’ve driven over to bring Señor Scratchy to you.”

Nicky leaned back so he could look at her. He shrugged and gave her a reassuring smile. “It’s okay, Mama. I had fun with you and Rio anyway.”

Her heart softened at his words, delighted that he had valued their time together just as much as she had. “Alright, honey. I’m still sorry. Did you have a good time with your dad, though?”

Nicky nodded, but then his smile faltered. “Yeah, but I did get mad at Dad. He said we’d go to the park, but he was on his phone the whole time, so we couldn’t go. And we were almost late at the movies!”

Agatha’s chest tightened. She reached out to smooth his messy hair, her voice soft and comforting. “I’m sorry, I know you were really looking forward to going to the park.”

“It’s okay,” Nicky said, though his voice was tinged with sadness. A child shouldn’t have to justify his parent’s actions and shrug them off. He had every right to be upset, and Agatha’s anger towards Ralph only grew. “He bought me three sweets at the movies to make up for it. Three, Mama!”

His mother chuckled at that. Of course he did.

Nicky perked up slightly, looking at her with hopeful eyes. “Can we go to the park today, Mama? Please?”

Relieved to see his excitement return, she happily obliged. It was still cold outside, but the weather wasn’t bad enough to stay inside. Some fresh air would do them both good. “Sure we can. I’ll text Wanda and see if Billy and Tommy want to come, too. How does that sound?”

His face lit up and he nearly bounced off the mattress, shoving the covers off of his little body. “Yes! They always let me pick what to play!”

Although Wanda’s kids were 10 years his senior, they always made sure Nicky was having a good time. They practically grew up with him, given that the friendship between their mothers had been going strong for long before the kids were around. They’re good kids, Agatha thought to herself.

Not wanting to waste any time, Nicky hurriedly hopped off the bed. He ran over to her side of the bed, tugging on her arm. “Come on, Mama! Let’s get ready so we can go soon!”

Agatha rolled her eyes playfully as she stood, allowing herself to be dragged out of bed. “Alright, alright! Let’s go pick out some clothes.”

Before she knew it, Nicky zoomed out of her room and into his, determined to find an outfit at record speed. She quickly put on a pair of jeans and her favourite cozy, loose-fitting, purple sweater. This would keep her warm at the park at least. She didn’t feel like dealing with her hair today, so she put it in a low ponytail. When she walked into Nicky’s room, the scene in front of her made her shake her head and laugh: Nicky was standing up, one leg inside his pants while the other half of the clothing dangled freely on the side. His top half wasn’t much better, as he was frantically trying to find the armhole of his sweater. She crouched down to help him, knowing that his excitement had gotten the better of him. He often spoke so intelligently that Agatha forgot he was still just a child. It was a welcome reminder that time wasn’t moving as quickly as she thought. Good.

They walked into the bathroom together to finish getting ready. In between tasks, Agatha grabbed her phone and sent a quick text to Wanda.

Hey, Nicky wants to go to the park today. Any chance you and the kids are free to join?

Wanda’s response came quickly, and Nicky’s excitement shot through the roof again. Agatha looked at him with a fond smile, but she couldn’t help but feel a small pang of guilt for Nicky. He deserved so much better than broken promises and half-hearted attention from Ralph. At least he had a good support system that was always determined to make it up to him.

—————

When Rio squinted at the time on her phone for the umpteenth time, she sighed heavily. She stared at the ceiling, her mind churning like a restless sea. She hadn’t slept much. It wasn’t a bad night exactly; her night was full of Agatha, of Nicky, of the moments that lingered long after she’d driven home. It was just… a lot to think about.

She gave in to the inability to get more rest and scrolled through her notifications. She saw a flurry of messages from Alice and Jen in their group chat. She unlocked her phone and read the messages, groaning softly as their teasing came into view.

Alice: omg why hasn’t Rio replied?

Jen: obviously too busy with the mysterious Agatha

Alice: should we send a search party or just assume she’s… preoccupied?

Jen: definitely preoccupied. probably screaming so loud rn that she can’t hear our notifications

To be fair, she had been basically screaming. Just not in the way they all had hoped. She’d been cussing out Agatha’s ex-husband. She continued scrolling through the texts.

Alice: lmao real. RIP our girl

Alice: Rio, blink twice if you’re alive

Why were her friends having a conversation amongst themselves as if they didn’t literally live together? Rio rolled her eyes and typed a response, her fingers flying over the keyboard.

Y’all are insufferable. Fuck off.

Alice: ooh, she lives!

Jen: limbs still working?

Rio smirked, shaking her head, and replied.

Not telling you shit till later. Free to hang out?

She hadn’t exactly figured out what she would and wouldn’t share about her evening with Agatha, but she knew she had to talk to her friends to make sense of everything.

Alice: YES

Jen: oh this better be good

Rio laughed softly, feeling a little lighter. She set the phone down and swung her legs over the side of the bed. There was no chance of falling back asleep now and she didn’t have plans till later. The winter sun was shining brightly and suddenly the chill of a morning run sounded appealing. Running always helped clear her head, and right now, her mind needed all the help it could get.

She changed into weather-appropriate running gear: black leggings that hugged her legs like a second skin, a fitted thermal long sleeve and her trusty running shoes. She tied her ponytail high, her hair bouncing as she pulled on a headband for extra warmth. After a quick sip of water, a banana for breakfast and a good stretch, she grabbed her keys and stepped outside.

The cold air bit at her cheeks the moment she stepped onto her stoop, but the sun was shining brightly, glinting off the frost-dusted cars along the street. “Alright, here we go,” she muttered to herself, adjusting her earbuds and queueing up a playlist.

She started her usual route, jogging at an easy pace down her block before heading to the neighbourhood park. Her breath puffed out visible clouds and her shoes crunched softly on the pavement. She could tell that she’d skipped a few workouts lately due to work, but it felt good to be back to it again. As she ran, the tight knots of thought in her mind began to loosen. Exactly what she needed.

—————

The two mothers were perched on the park bench, cold air nipping at their cheeks. Both of them had their hands tightly wrapped around a steaming to-go cup of coffee, shielding their hands from the cold.

Nicky was animated, telling Wanda all about his upcoming school play. It seemed as though this was the only thing he could think about these days, sharing the information with anyone willing—or forced—to listen. Wanda, reminiscing the time her boys had their first school play, listened intently, her usual warmth shining through. “Do you get to wear something cool?” she asked, her excitement matching his.

He beamed. “Yeah, ice cream! And tomorrow we’ll finish making the props, I get to help with the big moon next!”

Agatha smiled softly, watching Nicky’s enthusiasm. Her best friend chuckled. “You’re going to steal the show, kiddo. I can’t wait to see the video.”

The kids moved to the jungle gym, Billy and Tommy quickly getting caught up in their own game. They made some kind of bet about who could do more pull-ups on one of the bars, their laughter ringing out as they each tried to trick the other into giving up. Nicky watched them for a moment, bouncing slightly on his shoes, before suddenly darting off without a word.

Agatha’s heart leapt. “Nicky!” she called, standing quickly. Wanda looked over in concern, following Agatha’s gaze as Nicky sprinted across the park. “Nicky, come back!”

But he didn’t listen. He was focused on a figure jogging near the edge of the park. He had spotted her from a distance; Rio was running, eyes trained on the path in front of her.

She noticed him at the last second, her eyes widening as Nicky barrelled into her legs. Rio staggered slightly but caught him, her hands on his shoulders as she steadied them both. Pulling out her headphones, she looked down at him, puzzled but amused. “Nicky? What are you doing here?”

He pointed back toward the playground, where Agatha stood watching with a mixture of relief and exasperation, hand over her heart. Wanda had relaxed too, realising there was no danger, and sat back down to silently take everything in.

The boy grinned up at Rio. “I saw you! I wanted to say hi!”

Rio chuckled, readjusting her headband slightly. “Well, hi, buddy,” she said, ruffling his hair. “Let’s get you back to your Mama, okay?”

She took his hand and walked him back to the bench. Agatha’s expression softened at the sight, though she couldn’t hide her lingering frustration. “Nicky, you can’t just run off like that,” she said, her voice firm but kind.

“But I saw our friend!” Nicky protested, gesturing to Rio as if that explained everything.

Taking advantage of the attention being on the child, Rio took a moment to stare at Agatha. Her cheeks and nose were reddened by the cold, and a few fly-away strands fallen from her ponytail framed her face. The sun made her blue eyes twinkle and her purple scarf complimented her complexion very nicely. She looked effortlessly beautiful.

Agatha sighed, her lips twitching in a reluctant smile. “I know, honey. Next time just let me know before you run off, okay?” He nodded and Agatha could only hope he actually listened. She didn’t want to think about what would’ve happened if he had been mistaken, if it hadn’t been Rio.

She looked to Rio apologetically. “I’m so sorry he interrupted your run.”

The other woman shook her head with a small smile. “It’s fine. Honestly, any minute longer and I would’ve passed out anyway,” she said, still slightly short of breath from the run. “Damn, I’m out of shape.”

She looked past Agatha and saw the red-haired woman on the bench. Knowing that this was Agatha’s best friend, Rio desperately wanted to stay on her good side. Not that it was easy to get on Wanda’s bad side; as far as Rio knew, she was practically an angel. She lifted her hand in a shy wave and smiled, “Hey, Wanda.”

The woman returned the greeting with a gentle, acknowledging wave. She let her curious gaze shift between Rio and Agatha, excited to sit back and watch their interaction to see if anything had changed since the last time.

The moment was cut short when Nicky tugged on her sleeve. “Rio! Wanna watch me on the monkey bars?” he pleaded.

Rio grinned. “Of course!” She turned to Agatha and Wanda. “Mind if I stick around for a bit?”

“Not at all,” Agatha replied, trying not to sound too eager.

The florist obediently kept her gaze on Nicky as he swung across the monkey bars, his face scrunched up in concentration. There were ten bars in total, and he had made it to the fourth before he was starting to lose his strength. Rio clapped her hands lightly, cheering him on. “You’re doing amazing, Nicky! Keep going!”

While Wanda silently watched the scene unfold, Agatha’s thoughts were scattered, her gaze flitting between Nicky and Rio. She had been deep in thought when Rio’s voice broke through.

“How was Nicky this morning?” she asked, her tone careful. Without shifting her body away from the playground, she turned her head toward Agatha, her concern evident in the soft knit of her brow. “After… you know, last night. Did he notice anything about the fight?”

Agatha blinked, momentarily startled out of her silent observation of Rio. She registered the question, her shoulders relaxing slightly. “He seemed okay,” she answered, her voice low. “He hasn’t brought it up, and I don’t think he really understood what was happening. By the time he got upstairs, he was too distracted, I think. Thank you for asking, though.”

Wanda’s ears perked up at this, her sharp eyes darting between the two women. Though she didn’t interject, her curiosity was clearly piqued. Agatha caught the look and sighed inwardly, knowing that she still had to share the entire story with Wanda.

Rio didn’t press further, nodding at Agatha’s response. “I’m glad to hear he’s okay,” she said genuinely. Then, raising her voice, she called out, “Good job, Nicky! You’re a pro!”

Nicky beamed proudly as he jumped down from the monkey bars before reaching the end. His chest puffed out as he yelled back, “Rio, can you go the whole way?”

The woman grinned, her playful side surfacing. “You bet! I’ll do you one better, I can do it with you on my back!” She turned to the two women once more, pushing her tongue against the inside of her cheek while raising her eyebrows, as if to say ‘watch this’. Agatha was surprised at her confidence and intrigued to watch it all happen from the front row. She sat down next to Wanda while Rio jogged over to the monkey bars, her ponytail bouncing with each step. She bent down in front of Nicky, letting him climb on her back, arms and legs wrapped around her body. With an effortless movement, she pulled herself up, her arms strong and steady as she swung gracefully from one bar to the next. Nicky’s giggles were so loud that they could probably be heard through the whole park.

Agatha’s eyes were glued to her.

For the first time, she noticed the way Rio’s workout clothes fit. Her athletic leggings hugged her curves perfectly, and her fitted top emphasised her toned frame. The light sheen of sweat on her skin caught the sunlight, her cheeks pink from exertion. Her muscles flexed as she swung from bar to bar. Her shirt had ridden up ever so slightly from the movement, leaving a sliver of her stomach exposed. And the smile on her face could light up the whole world. Agatha swallowed hard, trying not to stare too obviously, but her gaze lingered anyway.

Wanda, ever observant, noticed and smirked. She leaned closer to Agatha, her voice low. “You’re practically drooling, sweetie.”

Agatha snapped out of her daze, her face flushing. “I—what? No, I’m not.”

“Uh-huh,” Wanda teased, sipping her coffee.

“It’s just ridiculous,” she lamely tried to defend herself, clearly flustered.

Her best friend raised an eyebrow, giving her a knowing look. “What is?”

“Does that look out of shape to you? How dare she say that?” she huffed, crossing her arms.

Wanda laughed at her, wiggling her eyebrows. “Nope, definitely not out of shape. Hopefully you’ll get to see just how fit she is, soon,” she winked.

The blush creeping onto Agatha’s face settled deeply now. It would be a lie if she said she hadn’t thought about it. However, she wanted to do more than just sleep with her. She didn’t want this to be one of those dates that she just fucks and never calls again. Something about Rio made her want to do better than that, more than that. If it were to ever happen, she wanted it to be more meaningful than just a one-night stand. Even if it was incredibly hard to resist her. She’d try. Her eyes shifted back to Rio, who was now helping Nicky move across the bars again, supporting his little body to give him the additional strength he needed. Agatha couldn’t help but smile at the sight of them together, warmth blooming in her chest. She was becoming overwhelmed with all the different feelings she was fostering for the woman.

“So,” Wanda said in a playful tone, “when are you inviting her over again?”

Agatha groaned softly. “Not now, Wanda.”

“Mhmm.” Wanda’s smirk widened as she turned her attention back to Rio and the kids. When Nicky reached the finish, the two gave each other an overly-excited high five. Rio glanced back toward the bench and her eyes caught Agatha’s for a brief moment. She smiled softly, a faint shyness in her expression. Rio waved at them, and it encouraged Nicky to do the same. Agatha waved back and then quickly averted her gaze, trying to compose herself, but the blush rising to her cheeks betrayed her. Turns out Agatha could spend hours outside in the cold, because as long as Rio was around, she’d be burning hot the entire time.

Wanda didn’t miss a thing. She chuckled quietly to herself and took another sip of her coffee, clearly enjoying the show.

“Well,” Wanda said under her breath, “this just got a lot more interesting.”

Agatha shoved her friend playfully, rolling her eyes. She didn’t mind it though, she was just happy that she still got to see Rio even if they didn’t have plans. The odds were in her favour today.

Nicky excitedly ran to his mother, a proud grin on his face. “Mama! Mama! Did you see that?”

“I sure did, baby. That was really impressive.” She glanced at Rio, who trailed behind him.

“Did you see the way Rio carried me? That was so cool! She’s so strong!” he beamed, thrill still rushing through his body.

Rio sheepishly rubbed the back of her neck, feeling embarrassed now. It was adorable, Agatha thought. She looked back at her son and patted his head affectionately. “Yes, very strong. Lucky you, huh?”

He nodded, grinning wide. “You should try it too, Mama! It’s so fun!”

“Oh honey, I can’t carry you like that, your Mama’s getting old,” she joked. In all seriousness, though, she really didn’t think she could do it.

“No, silly! I know that!” Okay, ouch? “I mean… you should go on Rio’s back!” he said innocently, not knowing the effect it would have on both women. In the corner of her eye, Agatha could see Wanda desperately trying to hold in her laughter.

Rio’s eyes went wide and blush coloured her cheeks. “I— uh, I mean…”

Apparently the older woman’s best friend wasn’t entirely cruel, and she decided to help them out. “I think that it should stay reserved for you, Nicky. That way it’s extra special.”

He thought about this for a moment, before deciding that indeed, Wanda was right. They relaxed as he seemingly accepted that answer. Thank you, Wanda.

A buzzing sound rang softly and Rio checked her phone. She smiled at the notification, which made Agatha wonder who it was from. She felt a tug in her chest, wanting to know why someone else was making Rio smile through the phone. She kept her expression neutral, though. It could also be nothing.

“I should get going.” Or maybe it wasn’t nothing.

Nicky looked up at her and pouted. “Aren’t you staying with us?”

For once, Agatha was thankful for her son’s ability to be so straightforward. Maybe she’d let slip why she had to go so suddenly. Agatha’s heart raced in both anticipation and dread, waiting for the answer.

Rio cupped his cheek and rubbed it gently. “I’m sorry, buddy, I can’t today… I promised Jen and Alice I’d hang out with them. I actually had to leave a while ago, but I was having more fun playing with you.”

Hearing this information, Nicky seemed satisfied with the answer. He mainly focused on the part where Rio admitted to enjoying their hangout session, which was more than enough for him. Agatha exhaled deeply, feeling reassured that it was just her friends texting her.

“But we should definitely hang out again soon, okay?” she added, unable to stand the idea of disappointing him. “As long as your Mama is okay with it, of course.”

Agatha and Rio made eye contact, and the older woman nodded with a smile. “Yeah, sure. We’ll plan something soon.”

“Cool,” Rio responded. “It was also nice seeing you again, Wanda.”

“You too, Rio. Hope to see you around more often.” The redhead’s lips curled into a mischievous smile. She hoped for Agatha’s sake that the florist would be more present in their lives, because she noticed the positive effect she had on her best friend. It was good to finally see her happier again, more at ease. She also appreciated the way Rio had interacted with Nicky. So far, Wanda was not at all against the two women pursuing each other.

Shortly after Rio left, they decided it was time to head back home, the chill slowly starting to settle into their bones. The kids walked ahead, Tommy and Billy bickering about their bet while Nicky listened. Agatha and Wanda trailed behind at a leisurely pace.

“So,” Wanda began casually, her eyes glinting with curiosity, “are you finally gonna tell me about last night?”

“It was nice,” she said. She wasn’t sure if she was ready to admit anything out loud, she didn’t want to make her feelings real. Whatever those feelings might be.

“Just… nice? Come on, you’ve gotta give me more than that, I’ve been waiting all morning!”

Agatha hesitated for a moment, then let out a soft sigh, her lips curling into a small smile. She knew she would have to share eventually. “It was… really nice,” she admitted. “It felt so comfortable, you know? Like we’ve been friends for years. It wasn’t forced or awkward at all, it just flowed.”

“Do you think you guys connect on a deeper level?” Wanda was never one to beat around the bush. She knew her best friend got along well with Rio, that much was evident. She needed to know if it ran deeper than just a platonic connection. She believed she knew the answer already, but she had to know if Agatha was able to admit that to herself.

“I mean, we talked about all sorts of things. She allowed herself to be vulnerable and opened up to me about her past. At some point I even told her about… Evanora.” Agatha paused, knowing what kind of reaction this would elicit from the redhead.

“No way! That’s huge Agatha. You never talk about her. How’d she take it?” A sense of pride washed over Wanda; it was good to see her friend make such progress.

“Really well, actually. She was super understanding and even managed to make me feel better about some of it.” Agatha’s smile grew bigger, though she had tried to hide it.

Wanda nodded thoughtfully, giving Agatha the space to continue. “There was also a lot of banter, and I think… she might’ve been flirting with me?”

“Agatha, please. She most likely was, you’re just too stubborn to admit it.” Wanda rolled her eyes and gave her a playful shove.

“Well, anyway, of course Ralph had to show up,” Agatha said, her tone darkening slightly. “Nicky had forgotten his favourite plushy, and Ralph brought him back. He was angry, though, and not just about the toy. He was already annoyed that I wouldn’t join them for dinner and then got so upset seeing Rio there. He was about to turn into Evanora 2.0.”

Wanda frowned, “Oh, shit. What did you do?”

“I took Nicky to bed because I refuse to let him witness all that,” Agatha said firmly. “But Rio… She looked like she was about to beat him up. She even comforted Nicky afterwards. I didn’t ask her to do any of that, but she did it anyway.” Thinking about it tugged at her heartstrings, still not fully having processed the previous evening.

Her friend’s expression softened, a proud smile spreading across her face. “She sounds like a keeper,” she teased gently. “And I bet she must’ve looked hot getting all defensive over you!”

Agatha chuckled nervously, blush washing over her. She had looked hot. Incredibly so.

“So, from my understanding, this could actually lead somewhere?” Wanda concluded, though she could tell her friend wasn’t fully convinced yet. Her aim was to find out why.

“It’s just… I don’t know. Everything with her has been so easy. It’s scary, like it’s too good to be true. I don’t deserve someone like that, or the connection we’re building.”

Wanda stopped walking for a moment, forcing Agatha to pause as well. She turned to face her friend, her expression serious but kind. “Agatha, listen to me. You deserve every bit of good that comes your way. Hell, you deserve more than that. You’ve been through so much and you’re so focused on building this amazing life for Nicky. It’s okay to let yourself have something, or someone, that feels easy.”

The other woman swallowed, her eyes darting to the ground. “But what if it’s too much? What if I ruin it?”

She felt a comforting hand on her arm. “You won’t ruin it. And you’re not alone in this; Rio seems just as invested as you are. It’s clear that she’s a great person to have around, even if it’s just platonically. Don’t be afraid to explore what’s there. That’s the only way you could ruin it.”

“Thanks, Wanda. I needed to hear that.” Agatha smiled faintly, a flicker of hope lighting up her features.

“Anytime,” she replied warmly, linking her arm with Agatha’s as they continued walking. “And remember, if anything goes wrong, I’ll always be here for you. You’ll never get rid of me.”

Agatha laughed, feeling a little lighter. As they hurried to catch up with the kids, she realised her friend was right. She shouldn’t shut it down just because she was scared. Rio had effortlessly fit into their lives and she didn’t want that to end. She’d let Rio take on whatever role she wanted, even if it meant just staying friends with her.

—————

Rio arrived at Jen and Alice’s apartment building, staring up at the looming staircase with mild dread. The elevator was out of service, and their fourth-floor apartment suddenly felt like a trek up a mountain. She took the stairs two at a time at first, her determination fuelled by frustration, but by the time she reached their door, her legs felt like jelly.

"This is the worst timing for your elevator to be broken," Rio announced breathlessly as Jen opened the door. "My legs are killing me."

Jen smirked, exchanging a knowing look with Alice, who lounged on the couch behind her. "Oh, I bet they are," she replied with a suggestive lilt.

Alice chimed in without missing a beat, "Yeah, we get it. You had a very busy night."

Rio rolled her eyes as she stepped inside, tossing her bag onto the floor. "Can’t you two ever behave?" she muttered, though the corners of her mouth betrayed a grin.

Jen and Alice both broke into laughter. "Not when you’re out here acting all mysterious," Alice teased, patting the spot next to her on the couch. "Come on, spill it. What’s the story? Why are your legs sore really?"

Rio, thoroughly amused, decided to play along for a bit longer. She settled on the couch, pretending to stretch out her legs dramatically. "Well," she started, pausing for effect, "I did go for a run this morning."

Alice groaned. "Oh, come on. That’s not what we meant, and you know it!"

Jen perched on the arm of the couch, narrowing her eyes at Rio. "You’re just dragging this out to torture us. Stop being evil and tell us everything."

Rio laughed, holding her hands up in mock surrender. "Fine, fine. But you two are the worst." She took a deep breath, gathering her thoughts. “So, you know, I went over to Agatha’s house for dinner. It was really nice, actually. We had a good time, just talking and hanging out."

Jen leaned forward, her expression eager. "Define ‘really nice.’ Did something happen?"

Rio shook her head. "Not like that. I mean, I wish.” There was no denying that she was attracted to Agatha and her best friends would see right through her if she tried to convince them otherwise. If the night hadn’t taken that Ralph-shaped turn, she would’ve been desperate to dig up some courage and make a proper move. Sure, she’d kissed her cheek, but that almost sent her into cardiac arrest. If things kept moving at this pace, she was lucky to hold her hand by the time Nicky graduated school.

“It was just... comfortable. Easy. Had dinner, had wine. We talked about our pasts, opened up a bit about things we’ve been through. Ran into some trouble with her ex-husband, though. But I’d like to say we handled it quite well. And we shared some moments with Nicky, which I think she really appreciated. It’s obvious how much she loves him.” She didn’t want to share too many details about the content of their conversation or what had went down with Ralph, because she didn’t want to upset Agatha. It didn’t feel like it was her story to tell, so she figured she’d only share the top-line information.

Alice raised an eyebrow. "So, what’s the problem? Sounds like everything went well, no?”

Rio sighed, leaning back into the couch. She ran her hand through her hair, gathering her thoughts. "It did. That’s what scares me. What if I’m doing that thing again?"

Jen tilted her head, confused. "What thing?"

"You know," Rio said, gesturing vaguely. "Loving too hard, too fast. Being too much. I don’t want to overwhelm her. You Know Who left because I was too intense, and I can’t help but wonder if I’m going to repeat that mistake."

Jen reached over and squeezed Rio’s arm reassuringly. "First of all, screw your ex for making you feel like that. Second, you’re not doing anything wrong. You care deeply—that’s a good thing."

Alice nodded in agreement. "Yeah, Rio. Don’t let someone else’s inability to handle your love make you think you have to change who you are. You shouldn’t have to make yourself smaller to fit into someone else’s box."

Rio smiled faintly, though her worry still lingered. "I just don’t want to scare her away. She’s been through a lot, too. I don’t want to mess this up."

Jen nudged her playfully. "Here’s a wild thought: what if you just... don’t mess it up?"

Alice laughed. "What she means is, be yourself. You’re an incredible person, Rio. Agatha is lucky to have you in her life, and if she’s the right person, she’ll see that. Don’t pretend to care less—it’s not you."

Rio let their words sink in, feeling the tight knot of anxiety in her chest begin to loosen. Her friends always had their own typical way of showing their support, and moments like these only highlighted that difference. She felt grateful to have them both. ”Thanks, you two. I needed that."

"Anytime," Jen said, reaching for the bowl of popcorn on the coffee table. "Now, if you’re done overthinking, let’s finally start that sip-and-paint session you promised us. Maybe that’ll distract you from spiralling."

Alice grinned. “I’ll grab the good wine, you look like you need it.”

“And I’ll get you a refill on that moisturiser, because all that worrying will only get you wrinkles, Vidal,” Jen teased. Oh what a blessing to have a best friend who’s also a skincare guru. “Just because you like older women doesn’t mean you need to look older.”

Rio laughed, feeling lighter than she had when she’d arrived. As the three friends settled in, Rio couldn’t help but feel a flicker of hope. Maybe things with Agatha didn’t have to be so scary. Maybe, for once, she could just let herself be. Maybe Agatha wasn’t used to feeling cared for, but that shouldn’t intimidate her. Rio was determined to show her just how nice it could feel.
