Chapter 8: Anything for you

Chapter Summary

“Of course. Unless she’s busy?” she aimed the question at Rio, who quickly shook her head.

“Nope, not busy. Never too busy for you, Nicky.” She looked at the boy and quickly glanced at Agatha, whose lips curled up into a smile. She was never too busy to spend time with either of them. Rationally, she knew it wasn’t a competition, but she’d never be like Ralph.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

After the quite eventful weekend that passed, the following days were a welcome change of pace. On Monday, Nicky went back to school and Agatha stepped into her usual routine. She found herself getting in her head about her situation with Rio, and her favourite thing to do when overthinking was just to drown herself in work. It was one of the reasons she never took a step back from the cafe. It was her refuge. That’s how she found herself going straight to her workplace after taking Nicky to school. It was still before the cafe’s opening hours, but there was plenty of work to do to prepare for the day. Heading into the kitchen area, she put on her trusty apron, embroidered with her name, and tied her hair up. This was generally not her favourite part of the job; she much rather left it up to Lilia, whose treats were famous among all customers. Occasionally they’d bake together, but in all honesty, this was entirely Lilia’s domain. However, when ridden with anxiety, Agatha needed to work with her hands. The employees knew very well what it meant whenever they found her in the working space, hands covered in flour. It read like a neon, flashing, do-not-disturb warning sign.

And so they didn’t. Lilia was the only other person in the cafe that morning, as Billy was off to school, and she could sense Agatha’s mood from miles away. The older woman was determined to make life as easy as possible for the owner by handling all the other tasks that needed to be taken care of. She’d ask Agatha about it later—now was definitely not the time.

Agatha stood at the wide wooden counter, back facing toward the rest of the cafe, flour dusting her apron and streaking across her cheek where she’d subconsciously brushed away a stray curl. Her hands were kneading the soft dough for her iconic cinnamon rolls with a steady pressure, mirroring the unrest inside of her. Each push and fold of the dough felt like an attempt to press away the unease swirling in her chest.

She focused on the tangible sensations: the way the dough stretched and softened under her palms and the faint stickiness that clung to her fingers. She leaned into the motions, her breathing syncing with the repetitive act of kneading.

Her thoughts, however, refused to stay quiet.

She thought of Rio—how effortlessly she had wormed her way into her life, how safe and comfortable she made her feel, and how terrifying that feeling was. Did she deserve something so simple, so good? Would she corrupt her, simply by being around her too much? If her mother’s words held any truth, Rio deserved better than that, better than her.

And then, Ralph. Agatha’s hands pressed harder into the dough, her jaw tightening as she recalled his raised voice, the way he always knew how to stir old wounds. No wonder he got along so well with Evanora, their favourite pastime seemed to be taking jabs at Agatha. And pouring mountains of salt over the reopened wounds.

She worried for Nicky, for how her history with Ralph might colour his childhood, no matter how much love she poured into him. A part of her was worried that he’d resent her when he grew older, getting in the way of his relationship with his dad. That was why she made an effort to include Ralph wherever possible; alternating weekends, keeping him in the loop when it came to Nicky’s progress at school and even spending an evening sat next to each other for his school recital. Anything for Nicky.

With a deep sigh, Agatha paused, brushing her forearm across her brow and leaving a faint streak of flour behind. She divided the dough into neat sections, rolling each one out with precision, occasionally using more force with the rolling pin than necessary. She sprinted the cinnamon-sugar mixture across the top, spreading the filling evenly, and rolled the dough into tight spirals before slicing it into perfect rounds.

As the rolls took shape, her mind quieted. She placed them gently onto a greased pan, arranging them with care, as if the act of creating something beautiful could bring order to her chaotic thoughts.

By the time the first tray was in the oven, Agatha stood back, hands on her hips, and inhaled deeply. The warm aroma began to fill the rest of the cafe, wrapping around her like a comforting, fluffy blanket. It wasn’t a solution to her worries, but it was enough to make the weight in her chest feel just a little lighter.

She finally turned around, tuning back into her surroundings. Her eyes found Lilia’s, who was watching her with tenderness and care. The older woman worried about her, she knew that. Agatha gave her a reassuring smile and a gentle nod, as if to convince her that she was okay now.  A moment of silent understanding passed between them, and they went back to work as if nothing had happened.

It was a few minutes before opening time, cinnamon rolls already placed neatly in the display on the counter, along with banana bread and mini cherry pies. Agatha had clearly kept herself busy. There was a timid knock on the cafe’s entrance, and Agatha turned her head to see who was so eager for a cup of coffee that they couldn’t wait until she unlocked the door.

And there she was, the woman that Agatha had spent half her morning overthinking about. Upon seeing her, a vaguely unfamiliar feeling washed over Agatha. It wasn’t unpleasant, not like her anxiety from before. There was cautious excitement, mixed with something she couldn’t name. All she felt was that perhaps it hadn’t been necessary to be so anxious about Rio.

Still clad in her flour-covered apron, she hurried to the door to open it. Casually, of course. She couldn’t show how eager she was to see the florist. That’d be entirely too much. She opened the door and finally nothing was blocking her view of the other woman anymore. Rio greeted her with a shy smile. “Hi.”

“Hey.” Agatha couldn’t suppress the smile that was tugging at her lips. She raised her eyebrows slightly in surprise. “You’re early today, Vidal.”

“Yeah, uhh, I—” she stammered, suddenly feeling embarrassed about showing up so early. She probably should’ve waited till her usual lunchtime, but something just pulled her into the direction of the cafe before opening her own shop. “I thought I’d get a good start today. With the coffee, of course.”

“Hmmm, well,” Agatha pretended to be deep in thought, looking at the clock on the wall, “You’re actually three minutes early…” She leaned in closer, as if she was about to share a secret. “I heard the owner can be quite a bitch, so I’m not sure if we can serve you yet.”

Rio held back a laugh and decided to play along. “Oh really? I’m actually quite close with her, I think she’d make an exception for me,” she batted her eyelashes in an exaggerated manner. “If anything, I think she’d be more pissed off if my order wasn’t taken care of.” Great, Rio, lay it on real thick.

They both burst into laughter, unable to keep up the act any longer. Agatha opened the door wider now, allowing Rio to step in. She flipped the “closed” sign around, as there was no use to lock the door just two minutes before opening.

“Damn, something smells delicious,” Rio said, sniffing the air curiously, trying to pinpoint what it was exactly. She walked over to the glass cabinet on the counter, where various treats were sitting proudly on display. She looked at Agatha from over her shoulder, taking in her appearance. She noticed the apron and it didn’t take a rocket scientist to put two and two together.  “Someone’s been baking, I see.”

“Yeah, thought I’d let Lilia start up easy for once,” Agatha responded lamely, obviously not wanting to give the real reason. What was she supposed to say? ‘I was overthinking every single interaction I’ve had with you in the past weeks and it’s driving me crazy’, or ‘I pretended the dough was my ex-husband just so I could punch him in the gut’? None of those sounded like anything a sane person would say. And Agatha was perfectly sane. Obviously.

Agatha stepped closer to the younger woman — or well, the display. That was the only reason she was approaching. To explain what she had made. No other reason.

“Agatha Harkness, are those your cinnamon rolls? I haven’t seen those in ages!” Rio exclaimed with more excitement than she would’ve allowed herself if she had thought about it for one second longer. Her eyes were fully trained on the display, scanning the different baked goods and the little hand-written signs in front of them.

“Yeah, I haven’t made them in a while… Would you like one, along with the usual?” Agatha had already moved behind the counter, ready to grab a roll for her before she even responded.

“Yes, please!” The smile on Rio’s face made her brown eyes sparkle. It reminded Agatha of when Nicky would get excited; his big, brown, shiny eyes looking at her so intently that she would melt on the spot. The effect Rio’s eyes were having on her right now was not much different. Thankfully the glass display was partially shielding her, hiding the blush that was certainly covering her cheeks right now.

Rio took a seat on the bar stool by the counter, tapping her fingers on the surface as she waited for her order. Agatha placed a small plate in front of her along with a napkin, before turning away to prepare the coffee. She took a big bite right away, immediately savouring the burst of cinnamon and nutmeg in her mouth. “Ohhhh my sweet, sweet, sweet cinnamon roll, how I’ve missed you!” she said dramatically. “Mommy has missed you so much, I’m never letting you out of my sight again,” she continued, staring at the roll so intently, seemingly worried that someone was about to steal it if she blinked for a milli-second too long.

Mommy? Agatha’s eyes widened slightly. With her back turned to the woman, if she let herself be delusional enough, it sounded just like the florist was talking to a child. It suited her. Mommy.

Lilia’s laughter rang through the room. “Agatha, look what you’ve done to the poor woman. I think you broke her.”

However, it was Agatha who felt like she was malfunctioning, allowing herself to entertain the fantasy of Rio being a parent. With the way she had treated Nicky lately, it wasn’t that hard to imagine.

Snapping back to reality, she finished pouring the coffee into a cup and called over her shoulder, not fully ready to face the two women just yet. “Maybe yours just aren’t that good, Lilia,” she teased. “You’ve deprived her of good cinnamon rolls this entire time. That’s not my fault!”

“I think she’s just biased,” Lilia retorted before focusing on wiping the tables again.

By the time Agatha served the hot beverage, Rio took the last bite. She smiled sheepishly as she made eye contact with her barista, taking the cup from her hand with a grateful nod. Not wanting to speak with her mouth full, she quickly swallowed her food before mumbling a quick, “Thank you.”

“You’re welcome.” Agatha leaned against the counter, across from Rio’s seat. She held eye contact, appreciating the way the brown eyes still seemed to sparkle just that little bit more than usual.

Rio took a sip of her drink, eyes scanning over Agatha’s face. She lowered the cup and pointed at Agatha’s forehead. “You’ve, um, got a little—“ she started, gesturing to the faint streak of flour above her brow.

Agatha’s fingers moved to her forehead, trying to wipe it away quickly, but mostly missing the spot. In the process, her face turned slightly and Rio noticed the mark on her cheek as well. “Oh and um, also, like, right there,” she pointed out with a chuckle.

Feeling embarrassed about looking this dishevelled in front of the florist, Agatha desperately wiped at her cheek, which actually only made it worse. Rio laughed at the scene in front of her and shook her head.

“C’mere,” she said, her arm stretching over the counter to reach for Agatha’s face.

She considered using the napkin next to her, but she had just used it after eating the cinnamon roll, and the purpose wasn’t to make Agatha’s face even dirtier. The older woman froze as Rio’s fingers touched her forehead, wiping the streak of flour away. Once satisfied, she lowered her hand to cup Agatha’s cheek gently. Agatha felt her breath catch in her throat, her heart beat erratically in her chest. Her neck and face flushed slightly, and she was certain Rio could feel it under her palm, too. If she did, though, she didn’t mention it. She rubbed the spot on her cheek, removing the remaining evidence of Agatha’s anxious baking.

As if finally realising what she was doing, Rio looked up at Agatha’s eyes. The blue eyes pierced into her, and Rio had difficulty maintaining eye contact. She noticed the flush on the older woman’s cheeks and secretly felt a sense of pride, happy to have stirred such a reaction within her. She briefly lowered her eyes to Agatha’s lips, taking in their plump shape. Before she knew what was happening, her thumb started tracing the woman’s lower lip, brushing it lightly. She felt a small gasp escape through Agatha’s lips, which snapped her back to reality. Rio cleared her throat. She couldn’t believe she just did that. Hopefully, Agatha would simply believe that there were traces of flour there, too. That was the only reason Rio would’ve done something like that, of course.

“There you go, all cleaned up,” Rio said finally, hand still lingering on Agatha’s cheek, before ultimately retracting it slowly. The two women made eye contact, and it felt more intimate than any other time they had held each other’s gaze.

“Thanks,” Agatha said, a red blush still deeply colouring her cheeks. She suspected that Rio’s last movement hadn’t been entirely necessary, but she wasn’t going to call her out. If anything, it eased some of Agatha’s anxiety about this situation being one-sided. It didn’t make it any less scary, but eliminating the factor of rejection out of disinterest was already helping. Agatha could still think of plenty of other reasons to reject her, all words that were ringing through her head in Evanora’s voice, but that was to be discovered later. Physical attraction seemed to be there, at least. Great. That’s a step. Another fucking baby step.

“So, this week’s the big day, right?” The florist tried to change the topic casually, being more affected by the situation than she cared to admit.

“Hm?” Agatha questioned, her mind still foggy.

“Nicky’s debut as performer,” Rio clarified. If she recalled correctly, it would be this week. She just wasn’t entirely sure what day it was, but she’d find out soon enough.

“Ohh, yeah, you’re right. It’s on Wednesday. Can you believe they scheduled it in the middle of the day? That’s so inconvenient, it’s like they don’t consider that we have jobs…” she shook her head. “Not to mention it ends right when it’s dinner time. Like, hello? When was I supposed to prepare?” The older woman rambled, finally letting out some of the frustration toward the school’s poor planning.

Rio quietly thought to herself that perhaps she should offer for them to come over to her house for dinner. It’d be a good way to repay the favour. Then she remembered Ralph would be there, too. He’d most likely convince them to eat together, seeing that he had mentioned the importance of ‘family time’ more than Rio could count. The grip on her coffee cup tightened involuntarily.

“But anyway, he’s super excited, so I am too.” Agatha looked up again and exhaled deeply, shoulders visibly relaxing as her rant finished.

“I love how passionate he is about it. It’s the cutest thing I’ve ever seen,” Rio admitted, a smile tugging at her lips. The chokehold around her cup loosened, the thought of Nicky making her relax. “Please send me videos, alright?”

“Of course. I might have to just pull out a tripod at this point. I don’t wanna miss a thing, but I also want to record it all for memory’s sake.” Agatha was a live-in-the-moment type of person, but when it came to savouring her child’s memories, she loved capturing it all.

“Maybe check with the school if they’ll set up a recording? I bet other parents wanna have it, too.”

“Yeah, that’s a good idea, thanks.” Once again Rio had been incredibly considerate, thinking of solutions to problems that hadn’t even been her own.

“If I don’t get to see him before Wednesday, can you wish him luck from me?” Rio asked shyly. She wanted Nicky to know that he had her full support, even if she was not there. Agatha nodded in confirmation, making a mental note so that she wouldn’t forget. Nicky would be over the moon if he knew Rio remembered, anyway.

“Alright, I should get going. Got flowers to arrange and all that,” she finished the last bit of her drink and placed the empty cup on the counter. “How much do I owe you?” She held up her card, walking over to the register.

“It’s on the house,” Agatha replied with a gentle smile.

“What? No! Do you know how much money I’d pay for that cinnamon roll alone?!”

“Well, technically we weren’t open yet when you ordered,” Agatha gave her a pointed look, smirk curling on her lips, “so I’m sorry, you can’t pay for it.”

Rio rolled her eyes playfully, knowing she wouldn’t win this fight. “I’ll stick to the official business hours from now on, then.” That was a lie, actually. She realised she quite liked being there before anyone else, having Agatha’s full attention. And the freshly baked goods were a very welcome bonus.

—————

Two days later, Nicky stood in the bathroom enthusiastically rehearsing his roles for the play. He had barely slept, unable to contain his excitement. It was way too early to be this energetic, Agatha thought. She was used to waking up early, but their mornings generally passed much more quietly than this. She couldn’t blame him, though, so she let him ramble on about the recital while she helped him get ready.

When she dropped him off at school, she promised she’d make it back well before his play would start, just to give him a few more words of encouragement before he had to go on. As excited as he was, she knew he would be nervous, too. When they had set up all the chairs for the audience, he had been impressed with how many seats there were. This was the first time he let his nerves simmer through. Agatha placed a kiss against his temple before bidding him goodbye, and she went to work.

Once she was at the cafe, the time passed rather quickly. She made limited small talk with the customers, prepared their drinks, tried one of Lilia’s pastries, and before she knew it, it was almost time to leave again.

When she stepped outside, she found herself glancing at the flower shop across the street, hoping to catch a glimpse of Rio. She didn’t see her though, and she assumed she was working further in the back. The florist had told her that she had to provide fresh flowers for a wedding cake, so Agatha knew she was busy. A part of her was disappointed she would miss their daily interaction in the cafe today. Well, there was always tomorrow.

The drive to school was short and traffic was on her side for once, so she made true to her promise of arriving early. Right as she reached the entrance, where she noticed the “The Very Hungry Caterpillar” poster on the door, her phone buzzed. She fished it out of her purse and skimmed over the notifications.

Rio: Tell Nicky good luck from me

Rio: And please take a picture of that monstera leaf he made!

A smile made its way to Agatha’s lips. She remembered. Not wanting to make Rio wait for a response till after the show, she texted back right away.

Will do! Check your phone in about 5 min.

Her smile faltered quickly when she read the next notification.

Ralph: Something came up

Ralph: Stuck in meeting, can’t make it anymore

Fuck, she thought. As happy as she was to not have to see him today, Nicky was going to be devastated. This was all he had been talking about for weeks. He had been so excited when his father had agreed to come to his show, thrilled to finally get to share one of his passions with Ralph. This better not be a made-up excuse, or else Ralph wouldn’t hear the end of it. This should’ve been blocked in his calendar a long time ago. Not wanting to argue right now, she quickly typed a response.

Ok. I’ll let Nicky know.

Agatha ran a hand through her long, wavy hair and sighed. How was she going to explain this to him? She tried to collect her thoughts as she stepped into the school, making her way to Nicky’s room. Her stomach churned as she pictured the disappointment on his face when she would break the news. Welp.

Her worries washed away for a second when she saw him through the classroom window; he was sitting at his designated desk, listening carefully to Mrs. Davis’ instructions. When he spotted her, he flashed a grin so wide it could result in world peace. Agatha was the furthest from feeling at peace right now, but seeing her little boy helped. He shot up out of his seat and ran over to the door. “Mama! You’re here!”

Agatha crouched down to meet him at his level. “Of course, honey. I promised I’d be here, didn’t I?” Admittedly, that was a silent jab at his dad. Luckily, Nicky wouldn’t know that. She made sure to stay neutral about Ralph whenever she spoke to her son about him.

He nodded eagerly and stepped in for a hug, wrapping his arms around him tightly. She kissed the top of his head and rubbed his back before looking up and smiling at his teacher. Mrs. Davis encouraged all the other kids to get up too, so they could make their way to the makeshift theatre.

A few more parents gathered around the children while they scrambled backstage in a hurry, getting all the props and costumes ready. Agatha stood next to Nicky, who was excitedly telling her about the different decorations, pointing out the one he made. When they reached the monstera leaf, Agatha pulled out her phone once again to snap a picture of it. When she explained to Nicky that she would send the picture to Rio, he quickly jumped into the frame, posing with the leaf.

Here you go, Nicky and his famous leaf :)

As soon as Agatha clicked on send, the three texting bubbles popped up in the chat. The picture received a heart reaction almost immediately, quickly followed by a message.

Rio: It looks even better than I imagined!

Had Rio been waiting for her text this whole time? Agatha’s heart fluttered. The next message was a picture of the real version of the plant, taken by Rio on the spot.

Rio: See, it’s super realistic. The kid’s a genius.

He learned from the best

How’s the wedding cake going?

She showed the picture to Nicky, who then proudly compared his piece of work to the real thing. In his eyes, they were identical. In Rio’s eyes, apparently they were too. In Agatha’s eyes, they were just leaves.

Agatha helped Nicky into his first outfit, wanting to take some of the weight off of the teachers by helping out. She felt her phone vibrate again, but she was too focused on the task at hand. She wondered to herself how she was going to let him down easy, telling him that Ralph wasn’t going to be there after all. As much as she tried to shield him from the hard truths by going the extra mile for him, this only proved that she couldn’t protect him from everything. As if reading her mind, Nicky spoke up.

“Mama, when’s Dad gonna be here?” His eyes roamed the room, trying to catch a glimpse of his father. His mom crouched down again, adjusting something on his costume that didn’t really need adjusting.

She pursed her lips, deep in thought. “About that…” she started. Nicky’s face fell immediately, sensing where this was going.

“I’m so sorry, sweetheart. He just texted that he can’t make it.” She had to rip off the bandaid, he would find out soon enough.

Nicky pouted and fidgeted with his hands, unsure what to say. He was trying his best to maintain his composure, not wanting to cry right before the show. Agatha’s heart shattered into a million tiny little pieces. She opened her arms, signalling for him to step in for a hug. He defeatedly rested his head on her shoulder, arms tightly hung by his sides, not reciprocating the embrace. Nevertheless, Agatha rubbed his back in an effort to console him. “I’m sorry, baby, I know you wanted him here,” she lovingly whispered in his ear.

Nicky took a couple of deep breaths, trying to calm himself down. A single tear slipped from his eye, which he wiped away quickly. After a final deep breath, he looked at his mother with a smile that didn’t fully reach his eyes. “It’s okay, Mama. I’m glad you’re here. Thank you for coming.”

“I wouldn’t miss it for the world.” And she meant it. She’d do anything to be there for him. Anything. He shouldn’t have to thank her for merely being there. That was the bare minimum. Her own mother didn’t even have the decency to do the bare minimum for her, but Agatha was determined to go above and beyond for Nicky.

One of his classmates walked up to Nicky, showing off his costume. Luckily, this managed to distract him, his excitement for the recital quickly resurfacing.

Agatha let out a deep sigh and took her phone out again, looking at the missed notifications.

Rio: you flatter me, Agatha.

Rio: Cake’s going ok, trying not to get hungry from staring at the picture lmao. The arrangement is kicking my ass, though. They’re so specific about what they want.

Rio: how’s the superstar?

She could picture the florist, deep in concentration as she tried to come up with the perfect arrangement for the cake. She guessed it was best that she was working off of a picture, rather than the actual cake. By the sound of it, there would be no more cake left if the couple had dropped it off at her shop.

I’m sure it’ll turn out wonderfully.

When she read the last question, Agatha hesitated for a moment before replying. She should probably be honest, but she felt like it highlighted her own shortcomings as a parent. If she couldn’t even get her child’s father to show up, what could she do? Maybe if she had informed him even earlier, he would’ve been able to clear his schedule. Maybe if she reminded him, or arrived with him, this could’ve been avoided. Maybe then, he would’ve been able to show up for Nicky. The misplaced guilt started gnawing at her. She glanced at Nicky, who was back to smiling and bouncing around from excitement.

He’s excited to go on.

Rio: Good, he’s gonna be great. Ralph there yet? Treating you ok?

Agatha was surprised at the rapid responses she was receiving from the florist, despite her being busy with work. She could feel Rio’s protectiveness through the screen, most likely stemming from the way he had treated Agatha on the weekend. Welp. Here we go. No point in hiding it now.

Nope. He cancelled last minute. Nicky was upset but I think he’s ok now.

Her eyes scanned the room for Nicky once more as she locked her phone, and she saw the group of children gathering for some final words of encouragement from Mrs. Davis. The parents were urged to find their seats in the room, where each kid was assigned two seats. All Agatha had to do was find the ones with Nicky’s name on them. Should be easy enough.

She greeted the other parents politely, not in the mood for small talk. Everyone already perceived her as distant, and it worked out in her favour this time. Trying not to worry too much about her son, she quickly sat down in her assigned seat, ignoring the pang she felt in her chest when she looked at the empty seat next to her. This was going to stick out like a sore thumb to Nicky when he was on stage.

Looking for a distraction, she pulled out her phone again. She saw a notification from Wanda, wishing Nicky good luck for his performance and demanding videos. She quickly sent her the picture she had taken of Nicky and his props, promising a video would follow later. There was no reply from Rio, though. Weird, Agatha thought. She had been so responsive not too long ago. Agatha shrugged it off. She was probably sucked back into her work, trying to decorate that wedding cake. She couldn’t blame her; it was 4 pm, and she was most likely rushing to finish her to-do list before closing. Agatha dropped her phone back into her purse and lifted her gaze to the small stage, waiting for the kids to come on. She fiddled nervously with the zipper on her bag, anxious for Nicky’s well-being. She hoped he’d be okay.

All the parents around her slowly but surely found their seats, and a few minutes later, the teachers in the room hushed them and dimmed the lights, indicating that it was time to start.

In the meantime, Rio had been hurriedly wrapping a pink Amaryllis in cellophane, tying a neat ribbon to secure it. She didn’t have much time to overthink her decisions, she went with her gut feeling and merely focused on moving fast. The pictures of the wedding cake long forgotten, she grabbed her belongings and made her way outside before locking up her shop. She had barely finished putting on her coat by the time she stepped into the cold air, but she didn’t care. The florist luckily didn’t park too far away, and made it to her car in no time.

Rio wasn’t always convinced that small towns were her thing, but right now it really worked in her favour. Westview didn’t have many schools, so there was no need to knock down all the doors in hopes of figuring out which one was Nicky’s. With a newfound determination, she drove over to the building, manifesting that she was able to avoid the traffic. Once she pulled up to the parking lot, she grabbed her flower and made her way inside, frantically looking around to find out where she needed to go. She had not set foot in an elementary school since her own childhood, so she wasn’t exactly a natural. Thankfully, one of the teachers was in the hallway and sensed her distress. Right when he lifted his hand to show her the way, she bolted to the door he pointed at. She entered from the back of the room, facing the small stage. She heard hushing in the audience and the lights were already dimmed, but it seemed like she hadn’t missed much yet.

She had slipped through the door as quietly as possible, careful not to disturb the children and the audience. Rio winced when the door creaked slightly, but apparently it wasn’t loud enough for all eyes to turn to her. She swiftly closed the door and let her eyes roam the room. It was dark and all she could see was just random heads and vague faces of people she didn’t really recognise. There were a few scattered empty seats here and there, but she was determined to find a very specific one.

There she was.

She spotted Agatha on the third row, right at the centre of the stage. She’d recognise those long, dark waves from anywhere. The older woman heard the commotion from behind her and turned around, staring straight at Rio. Her eyes widened in surprise, and confusion was quickly written all over her face. The florist gave her a shy wave, suddenly feeling very self-conscious about randomly showing up there. Once again, she felt like she was intruding. When Agatha flashed her a small yet genuine smile and gestured for her to come over, Rio tried to rid herself of any negative thoughts. She straightened her back and walked over to the empty seat that was assigned to Nicky. She apologised as she squeezed her way past the other parents, before plopping down on the seat and letting out a deep breath.

She turned toward the woman next to her, finding Agatha already looking at her, blue eyes radiating warmth. “What— How—” Agatha was searching for the right words, unable to form a coherent sentence.

“I hope it’s okay that I came,” Rio whispered, too nervous to hold eye contact, shifting her focus to the restlessly fiddling hands in her lap, flower now placed right atop her thighs.

Agatha’s gaze softened, and she reached out to wrap her hand around Rio’s wrist, stilling her anxious movements. She brushed her thumb over the skin reassuringly. “Of course, I’m just surprised you were able to make it. I know you were busy.”

“Anything for him,” Rio said, as if it was the most obvious thing in the world. And anything for you, she almost added.

She glanced up at Agatha, searching her eyes for any potential hidden message; she needed to know if she hadn’t overstepped, if she hadn’t once again weaselled her way into their day, completely uninvited. The blue eyes looked at her with so much admiration, even in the dim lighting, that her worries washed away. Rio looked back down in her lap, seeing where the other woman was still touching her. Awfully aware of the gesture, her cheeks started flushing.

Agatha noticed that the brown eyes were watching her movements intently, suddenly feeling embarrassed. She quickly retracted her hand and placed it in her own lap, fingers twitching from the lack of contact with Rio’s skin. She flashed her a quick smile with a brief nod, before turning her attention back to the stage.

As if on cue, Mrs. Davis walked on and started with a short introduction, explaining that the entire performance would be recorded, and there wouldn’t be a break in between. After all, they were just children, and The Very Hungry Caterpillar didn’t exactly have a three-hour script.

Mrs. Davis posed as a narrator of the story, while the students acted out the scenes and occasionally said a few words. For the opening scene, the children shuffled on the stage, pushing a big moon-shaped prop in one corner, and a big leaf in the centre. A little boy dressed as an egg walks on, and lays down on the leaf.

Rio and Agatha paid close attention to the children moving on and off the stage with the props whenever the scenery changed, hopeful of spotting Nicky. The sun came up, and the boy previously dressed as the egg took off his costume to reveal a caterpillar underneath. Remembering the storyline well from Nicky’s explanations, Agatha felt her body tense in anticipation. As the caterpillar started looking for food, he stumbled upon an apple.

And it was the best apple they had ever seen. Although both Rio and Agatha were extremely biased.

Nicky proudly walked onto the stage, dressed in a red apple costume. His expression was serious, as if he was performing in a sold-out Broadway show. When he reached his spot, he finally turned to the audience, not so subtly looking for his mother. It wasn’t hard to find her, and the shift in his expression was obvious and incredibly endearing. He let his eyes drop to the seat next to Agatha, fully expecting it to be empty because his dad couldn’t make it. But no, to his genuine surprise, Rio was sitting there, beaming at him with a big grin. He returned the smile, looking from Rio to his mother and back to Rio. He couldn’t believe his eyes.

It was in that moment, that the younger woman felt all of her worries completely disappear; she belonged there, in that seat. She no longer had to doubt whether she had made the right decision to show up; merely Nicky’s reaction was enough to justify it, no matter how Agatha would feel about the situation.

The florist glanced at Agatha, who couldn’t keep her eyes off Nicky, watching him proudly. She took the moment to fully look at the woman for the first time that night. Agatha had been wearing blue jeans and a white pinstripe blouse, her hair tucked behind her ears but still falling over her shoulders in that messy and nonchalant way that Rio loved. She reached over to grab the older woman’s hand and squeezed it tightly, to which she gave a gentle squeeze in return. Rio wished she could’ve let her hand linger in hers, but felt too shy to do so. Thus, her hand found its way back into her lap after running it through her own hair.

As much as they wished the scene could last forever, it was quickly time for Nicky to move backstage again, before reappearing in another costume later.

Mrs. Davis continued to tell the story of The Very Hungry Caterpillar, and several kids came on stage, dressed as different foods: two pears walked in hand in hand, three plums shyly shuffled to the stage, four strawberries almost forgot where to stand for their part, five oranges giggled and waved at their parents in the audience when it was their time to come on.

“And on Saturday, the caterpillar decided to stop eating fruits, because it still left him hungry. He discovered…. desserts!” the teacher narrated when a little girl dressed as a chocolate cake appeared.

Right after the caterpillar had pretended to eat the cake, Nicky—quite literally—hopped onto the stage again. His enthusiasm was evident to everyone, the child obviously enjoying being dressed as a life-sized strawberry ice cream cone. He dramatically exclaimed when the caterpillar started eating him, his passion for theatrics shining through.

“God, he is so dramatic,” Rio leaned in to whisper.

Agatha laughed softly, eyes still trained on the stage, but leaning her body towards Rio to whisper back. “I don’t know where he gets it from.” 

“I have a feeling I know…” Rio teased, still watching Nicky.

The older woman smacked Rio’s knee in mock offence, but the smile tugging at her lips gave away her true feelings. Agatha knew she was right, he definitely got it from his mother.

“See, you just proved my point,” Rio smirked, rubbing her knee pretending to be hurt.

“Don’t be such a baby,” Agatha responded. Missing the woman’s touch, however, she reached over to the knee in question and smoothed it over, as if to soothe the pain. In reality, she just wanted an excuse to touch Rio. Little did she know, she didn’t need an excuse; Rio was happy to receive any kind of attention and affection from Agatha.

Nicky exited the stage, and a few more foods came and left. It was now time for the famous nice green leaf to come onto the stage, and Nicky had the honours to carry it. Unable to contain his excitement this time, he walked on with the big prop in his hand, waving it around proudly. This was the one that he had been telling Rio about, and the woman noticed right away. Not that she needed any confirmation, but Nicky waved at the florist excitedly and pointed at the leaf, trying to convey the message. Rio responded with a big smile and gave him two thumbs up, while Agatha’s gaze was fixed on her.

Rio couldn’t believe she was witnessing this with her own eyes, and she was extremely grateful that Ralph had not been able to make it. She felt her heart swell at the thought that she was the reason that Nicky was so passionate about the creation of the plant. That was her influence, no matter how small. Agatha watched her fondly, having the same realisation as Rio. She admired the florist for her impact on her child, and seeing him so happy was the best thing Agatha had experienced in a long time. It felt as if her own walls were being lowered each time she looked at Rio and Nicky’s interactions, all filled with laughter, smiles and big shiny brown eyes. Oh, she was in trouble.

Her mind felt foggy and her attention slipped from the play as the last scenes played out, deep in thought about the woman sitting next to her.

Eventually, the caterpillar turned into a butterfly and all the students came back onto the stage, indicating that the show was over. A loud applause erupted from the audience, every parent proud of their child. Agatha glanced over at Rio, right in time to catch her putting her fingers to her lips and whistling loudly. She watched as Rio’s lips wrapped around the fingers and had to stop herself from entertaining thoughts that were wildly inappropriate for the setting they were currently in. She felt her cheeks flush but tried to shake it off, feeling embarrassed by the way it had affected her, whereas the other woman had returned to applauding enthusiastically.

Rio, however, was very aware of the eyes on her and might have been doing it on purpose. Who could blame her, though?

She made eye contact with Agatha, who was still slightly flustered. She raised an eyebrow in question, carrying a cocky smirk on her face. Feeling caught, Agatha tried to physically shrug it off with a small raise of her shoulders. “I didn’t know you could whistle like that,” she leaned into Rio, whispering in the same way she had done before.

Rio, proud of the reaction she had elicited from the older woman, leaned in even closer than necessary to whisper in her ear. “There are a lot of things I can do that you just don’t know yet.”

When she moved back, she shot Agatha a small wink, before turning her attention back to the students, who now made their way behind the stage again. Agatha was grateful not to have her eyes on her anymore, because she felt a warmth spread over her entire body and was sure her hot flush could be seen even in the dark.

After taking a second to calm down, Agatha felt confident that she had regained her composure and faced the younger woman again. She was immediately greeted with a smile. “So, what’s next?” Rio asked.

“I’m gonna go help him get changed, don’t want Mrs. Davis to deal with all that alone.”

Rio nodded before looking around the room, unsure what she should do to keep herself occupied while Agatha helped her son. Maybe she’d go chat with some of the other parents, or grab herself a drink from the table in the back. She waited for Agatha to leave, but the moment didn’t come. Instead, Agatha’s eyes softened before she spoke up. “Do you want to come with me? He’s definitely gonna ask for you anyway.”

Her eyes snapped towards Agatha, not expecting the question. That felt like something reserved for a parent, not a random friend who showed up technically uninvited. However, she could read in Agatha’s eyes that the offer was genuine, and failed to hold back her smile. “Yeah, I’d love that.”

“Alright, come on,” Agatha said, pulling her by her arm. Again, any excuse to touch Rio was being used tonight. Rio happily followed.

They squeezed past the other parents and made their way backstage. It was chaotic, seeing the students run around still in their costumes, play fighting with the props. It took quite some effort to find Nicky amidst the chaos, but they spotted him receiving compliments from his friend’s parents. When he noticed them, he immediately sprinted toward them.

“Mama! Rio!” he exclaimed, running directly into Agatha’s arms, who stumbled a little from the impact but lifted him up regardless. She peppered kisses on his cheeks as she swayed gently, and Rio looked at them with a big smile. She kept her arms at her sides and decided to let them have their moment.

Nicky, still in his mother’s arms, leaned back slightly and turned to Rio. He reached out with one arm, keeping the other wrapped around Agatha’s neck. Understanding the silent request, Rio stepped forward so that he could reach her, too. Suddenly, they found themselves in a shared hug, and Rio ran her fingers through Nicky’s hair, gently scratching his scalp. She tentatively wrapped her other arm around Agatha’s waist, keeping the touch light as she was scared to commit to the action fully. This felt good, natural. Extremely terrifying, but good.

“Hey there, superstar,” Rio greeted.

“I’m so happy you came!” Nicky told the florist after Agatha lowered him back onto the ground. He knew that his mother would be there, but after the disappointment of his father not coming, he really hadn’t expected this. The sadness about Ralph not being there was overshadowed by the excitement from the florist being there instead. He had seen the two women sitting next to each other during the play, and they looked so much more comfortable than Agatha was around his dad. Nicky was too young to understand all the deeper layers behind that, but he was smart enough to understand his mother liked Rio much better than Ralph. That was all he needed to know.

“I’m so happy, too. You did amazing buddy, I’m glad I didn’t have to miss it.” Rio’s words were genuine, feeling grateful to have experienced this moment with the two people who were slowly becoming an integral part of her life.

“I got this for you,” Rio crouched down in front of Nicky to be at his eye level, and held out the pink flower. “Have you seen this one before?”

Nicky furrowed his brows in concentration, trying to remember the flower. Agatha had been wondering all night what the flower was for, and it all made sense now. She watched silently, curious to hear where this was going. Eventually, Nicky shook his head, feeling disappointed with himself for not knowing.

“It’s an Amaryllis,” Rio explained as the boy held the flower, examining it closely. “You can use it to express that you’re proud of someone. And I am, of you,” she added shyly. The compliment touched Nicky, and the interaction touched Agatha even more deeply. This woman was going to be the death of her.

“It also means “to sparkle” in Greek, kinda like the way your eyes are sparkling right now!”

“Wow…” he said, deep in thought. “Is it always pink?”

“No, there are also other colours. But the pink is for friendship and affection, which you can use to show friends and family how much they mean to you.”

Nicky listened intently, trying to memorise everything that the florist was telling him. “What about the other colours?”

She should’ve expected his curiosity by now, but was more than prepared to answer. Happy to turn this into a full-blown lesson about the flower, she patiently explained more about its various meanings. “Well, there’s the orange one, which carries positive energy. Yellow is for good luck, so I could’ve given that one before the performance. Then there’s red…” she paused, glancing up at Agatha, who was already looking at her, her gaze soft and kind. “which symbolises passion and attraction,” she purposely held eye contact with the older woman when she explained that specific colour, before turning back to Nicky.

“That’s so cool! Thank you so much, Rio!” he hugged her tightly, arms wrapped around her neck. She rubbed his back affectionately and kissed his temple. He turned to show the flower to his mother, who looked at it full of admiration, understanding what the florist had been trying to do. Even though she had shown up last minute, she had taken the extra step to show how thoughtful she was. It was very impressive, and Agatha felt herself grow more fond of Rio.

Agatha put her hand on Nicky’s shoulder, gently guiding him to his bag of clothes while she and Rio continued showering him with praise about the play. His mother helped him out of his costume, and Rio handed her the clothing from the bag to make it easier. They made a good team.

“Did you see the leaf, Rio?” he asked hopefully, almost feeling more proud of his prop than of his actual roles.

“Yeah! It was my favourite prop of the whole play!” Rio nodded enthusiastically.

“Maybe we can ask Mrs. Davis if we can keep it?” He turned to his mother, who took a deep breath. She didn’t want to turn down his request, but she also didn’t exactly want a big fake leaf from his school play sitting in their house. At this rate, their house would quickly be overfilled with all of his crafts.

“I’m not sure, honey, where would we even keep it…” It wasn’t an immediate ‘no’, but she couldn’t always give in to him. He slumped his shoulders a little, not protesting but visibly disappointed.

“I mean… If we can keep it, I’d love to display it in the shop,” Rio interjected hesitantly, unable to withstand seeing Nicky upset, but also not wanting to cross any boundaries. His eyes shot up at her immediately, and a newfound determination washed over him. He immediately ran over to his teacher and seemingly asked permission. Before the women could go after him, he was already skipping back to them, prop in hand.

“She said it’s okay!” He looked like a child in a candy store, eyes filled to the brim with excitement. Agatha glanced at Rio, trying to convey the message that she didn’t need to do this just for Nicky’s sake. The look she gave back said that this was for herself as much as it was for him.

“Perfect!”

“What are we gonna do now, Mama?” He didn’t want their evening to end there, still feeling a rush from the recital.

“Are you hungry? We can go grab a bite,” she looked at Rio, wondering if it was appropriate to ask her to join too. After all, she had proven that she would show up whenever necessary. It made sense to invite her, right?

“Yes, please! Can Rio come too?” Well, that answered Agatha’s question. Leave it to Nicky to solve her issues for her. The two women made eye contact, Rio carried a timid smile.

“Of course. Unless she’s busy?” she aimed the question at Rio, who quickly shook her head.

“Nope, not busy. Never too busy for you, Nicky.” She looked at the boy, but also quickly glanced at Agatha, whose lips curled up into a smile. She was never too busy to spend time with either of them. Rationally, she knew it wasn’t a competition, but she’d never be like Ralph.

“There’s a place close by that has stands from all different kinds of cuisines. They also have the best ice cream, so we can finally make it up to you, kiddo.” Nicky was pleased with the offer, still remembering how the women had had ice cream without him on the weekend. She didn’t even turn to Agatha to see if she was against the idea, she figured that he deserved it today. Luckily, Agatha agreed, and they headed outside.

They drove to the location separately, as they had arrived in their own cars, and ended up ordering food from various stalls. They shared all the bites together, curious to try new things. It felt very domestic, but no one questioned it, simply enjoying the moment.

For dessert, Agatha allowed her son to have three scoops of ice cream, after Nicky and Rio teamed up against her, convincing her that it was a special day and he deserved it. She already never stood a chance against her son’s pleading brown eyes, but it was completely hopeless the moment Rio’s eyes inserted themselves into the equation too. The two pairs of warm, brown eyes managed to make Agatha completely disregard her speech as to why it was way too much sugar for him to have at this hour. Instead, she pretended to be annoyed while she ordered dessert for the three of them, and almost fought with Rio at the register when the florist tried to pay for them.

Chapter End Notes

I know I was the one who decided this was gonna be a slow burn but the anticipation is killing meeeee

Chapter 9: Bloody and bruised

Chapter Summary

warning: homophobia

Ralph finally gets what he deserves.

Chapter Notes

some of you asked for this, so I figured it was time to deliver. enjoy!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

On the Friday that week, just two days after Nicky’s play, Rio found herself mounting two small hooks on the wall behind her register. She bought the hardware to hold up the monstera leaf that Nicky had carefully crafted, wanting to give it a special place. This would certainly catch people’s eye, as it was now becoming a centrepiece on the wall.

She took a step back to admire her work and changed the angle of the leaf slightly until she was satisfied. Now, she’d have a constant reminder of the little boy and their meaningful time spent together. She couldn’t wait for him to visit again so he could see it for himself. She considered sending a picture to Agatha, but decided against it, not wanting to take away that element of surprise.

After having left so abruptly on Wednesday, she only made her situation at work worse for herself. She had spent all of yesterday working out various floral designs for the wedding cake, wanting to share it with the clients before she went ahead. When they finally settled on a final design, Rio started gathering the flowers. She couldn’t cut them too far in advance, they had to look fresh on the cake. She was also in charge of the floral arrangements in the venue, so she didn’t want to waste any time. She had arrived extra early that day, determined to finish everything.

She was fully focused on one of the bouquets when she heard the little bell above the door chime.  Her head lifted to see who it was, hoping that it wouldn’t be another customer with a big demand, she wasn’t sure she could handle it today.

However, upon seeing the figure that walked in, she might’ve preferred a difficult client anyway.

The man who walked inside was the one who she had spent more time thinking about than she would’ve liked. None of those thoughts had been positive. Rio felt her shoulders tense, and she let go of the garden shears, afraid of what she might do with them if she held on.

Ralph made his way into the shop, looking smug as usual, but there was something else there. He seemed… tense. Angry, perhaps. It was obvious that he was trying to cover it, but Rio could read people well enough to sense that she should keep her guard up, ready for anything. She gritted her teeth, not even wanting to spend energy on staying polite.

“If you’re coming here for roses again, please don’t bother, you saw how it worked out last time,” she called out with a raised eyebrow, before mindlessly rearranging the flowers in front of her, desperate to keep her hands busy.

“I’m not here for that. I need to talk to you.”

She simply looked at him and raised her eyebrow once again, urging him to go on. She wasn’t sure what to expect, but she didn’t want to let her nerves show.

“I need you to stay away from my family,” he said, approaching her like he was an animal circling its prey.

Rio couldn’t hold back the scoff that escaped her lips. “What the hell are you talking about?”

“You know damn well what I’m talking about. You’re everywhere these days, even at my son’s fucking school. You had no business being there.”

“You mean the play you cancelled on last minute? Because you couldn’t get out of a work meeting?” Rio stepped around the counter and into the shop, far away from the garden shears. She couldn’t believe the situation unfolding in front of her. Was he being serious? How was any of this her fault?

“Some of us have real jobs, you know. We can’t just all leave whenever we want,” he spat at her, visibly bothered that the woman had called him out. “Or play with flowers all day.”

Rio crossed her arms and rolled her eyes, smirking. “Right, because when you screw up, where else would you go but to a florist for an apology gift? Maybe I should start stocking ‘sorry for being a shitty husband and father’ cards. Judging by the state of you, they’d sell like hotcakes.”

It was clear that Ralph was trying to intimidate her, and she wouldn’t allow that to happen. She stood her ground, jaw tensed, shoulders squared, glare as hard as steel.

Ralph’s fists clenched at his sides and he stepped closer. Rio had been eyeing him intently, watching his every move, not wanting to be surprised by anything he might pull. “You think you’re so clever, don’t you? You’re the reason they don’t spend more time with me. You’re distracting them.”

“Oh, please,” Rio scoffed and did her signature tongue-in-cheek thing. “Ask yourself why they don’t want to spend more time with you. That’s on you, Ralph. Not me.”

His expression twisted with anger. “It’s entirely your fault. You’ve been corrupting them—filling their heads with your nonsense, and nestling into their life like a parasite that’s impossible to get rid of.”

That last sentence hit Rio harder than she would’ve cared to admit, for her anxiety about her sudden presence in their life had never really faded away. She had been worried that she was doing too much, being too much, caring too much. She had been down this road before and she knew how it would end.

Not wanting to give him the satisfaction, she focused on a different part of his accusations.

“Corrupting them?” Rio raised an eyebrow, her voice sharp. “And how am I doing that, exactly?”

“You know damn well what I mean,” Ralph spat, his voice dropping venomously. “You and Agatha, playing house. It’s disgusting. Nicky doesn’t need to see that kind of influence.”

Rio’s smirk faded, her jaw tightening, lips forming a thin line. It suddenly made so much sense why he got along so well with Evanora. Both brainwashed, minds rotten. “Funny. You suddenly care so much now, but you didn’t seem to care when you cheated on her.”

His face flushed red with fury. He certainly didn’t believe that was enough reason for their relationship to end. This man wholeheartedly believed that he could never do anything wrong. “Agatha should feel lucky I even wanted her in the first place,” he sneered. “She’s always been a selfish, twisted—”

“Careful,” Rio warned, her voice dangerously low.

“—abomination,” Ralph finished, his voice dripping with venom. If he had bothered to pay attention to the look on Rio’s face, he would know that it was in his best interest to shut up. “Evanora was right. Agatha’s evil, not normal. I tried to save her, figured it couldn’t hurt because she’s hot, but it seems she doesn’t want to be saved. I was her only way to redemption, but she’s too stubborn to see it. Too distracted by people like you.”

Something inside Rio snapped. The rage that had been simmering boiled over in an instant. She didn’t care about the things he said about her, the accusations he made, the homophobic comments and how blind he was to his own mistakes. She could handle it all. But when he started talking about Agatha, mentioning all the things her mother had used against her for years, diminishing her worth to just being hot like some fucking plaything, she couldn’t take it anymore. She felt grateful that Agatha wasn’t there to hear it all, unable to withstand seeing the knife get twisted inside the older woman’s heart over and over again.

She stepped forward and before she could regain control over her limbs, her fist connected with Ralph’s nose. She felt the cartilage wobble under her clenched fist, and her knuckles started to ache almost immediately upon impact.

A sickening crack was sent through the air, and Ralph stumbled back, clutching his face as blood poured from his nose. “You bitch!” he snarled, his words muffled.

Rio stood her ground, her chest heaving, her hand throbbing from the impact. “Get the fuck out of my shop, Ralph,” she growled.

“Fuck you,” he spat back.

“No, thanks. I’ll pass.” At this point, the whole interaction felt like an out-of-body experience, and Rio wasn’t sure where all this newfound cockiness came from.

The man was visibly seething, with anger and humiliation, as he staggered toward the door, glaring at her through the crimson streaming down his face. Well, didn’t he look pretty. He was lucky those gardening shears were out of reach, or else he would’ve ended up a lot worse.

“This isn’t over,” he hissed out the empty threat before slamming the door behind him.

Once the door flew shut, Rio exhaled sharply, her adrenaline slowly ebbing and the pain seeping through. She stared at her throbbing fist, bruises quickly forming as a result of the hit. A mix of guilt and satisfaction washed over her. She knew she had crossed a line, but she couldn’t bring herself to regret it—not after the things he had said about Agatha.

Agatha.

How was she going to explain this to her? She was bound to find out eventually, so should she just rip off the bandaid and tell her straight up? Rio wondered if she would get mad at her, it was still Nicky’s father after all. Would Nicky find out? What would the poor child think of her after hearing what she did to his father? What if he started to resent her, or didn’t want to hang out with her anymore?

Her thoughts raced in her head at a thousand miles a minute, realisation now settling in. She felt so strongly about the cafe owner and her son that she let her emotions take over, she allowed herself to be pushed over the edge and would now need to deal with the consequences. She looked over at her new wall decoration, hoping that the leaf would provide some sort of comfort. Unfortunately, it only made her more aware of the consequences if this turned out badly.

Fuck.

She shook her head, her jaw tightening as she went to the back room to wash her hands. She’d deal with the fallout later. For now, she just needed to breathe.

The cold water made the tiny cuts on her knuckles burn, but she let the water run over it regardless, cursing under her breath. She took a few deep breaths, trying to steady herself. Of all the days that he could’ve picked to show up, he had to do it when she was drowning in work, pressured by a tight deadline. Great.

As much as she wished she could explain her side of the story to Agatha right away, she knew she’d lose too much valuable time. Time that needed to be put into her very real job.

She ran her hands through her hair frantically and walked back to her workstation, continuing where she left off as if nothing had happened. She could tell her anxiety still lingered though, her teeth pressed tightly together, shoulders tense and hands slightly trembling.

Each time she applied pressure on the shears to cut through the stubborn stems, her hand protested. She had used her dominant hand for a guaranteed successful punch, but it was biting her in the ass now. She couldn’t afford to give up, though. Determined to still deliver her best work, she continued to carefully create bouquet after bouquet.

Once she felt like the workload had lessened significantly, she allowed herself to take a step back. Rio needed a break, mentally and physically. Her anxiety was still stirring through her body, feeling a lot of unrest. She caught herself longing for Agatha’s presence, knowing that it would be able to calm her down. However, this time she wasn’t too sure if she would be welcomed with open arms.

Unable to procrastinate any longer, she decided it was time for her daily dose of coffee along with a healthy dose of apologising.

The florist grabbed her coat, putting it on as an extra layer of protection. The more layers, the more out of reach her heart would be—ready to brace herself for whatever reaction would follow. If it all went south, she’d be fine. Right?

She took one last steadying breath as she stepped outside, hands buried deep into her pockets when she crossed the street. Rio wasn’t sure if the winter was becoming less harsh, or if she was just feeling hot from the nerves. Whichever one it was, she felt a small layer of sweat form at the nape of her neck.

As the door pushed open, her eyes immediately roamed the room in search of Agatha. She stood behind the counter with Billy, working together to fulfil the orders. With her eyes on the goal, she stepped inside, directly approaching the counter.

“Hi, Rio!” Billy greeted with a big smile. He knew how much her presence affected Agatha, so he was always happy when the florist visited.

Upon hearing the name, Agatha’s head whipped into her direction, eyes softening and a small smile curling on her lips. “Hey there, Vidal.”

“Hey.” It came out more breathy than she had hoped, the word escaping her lips more like a sigh. She managed to muster a small smile, but it didn’t fully reach her eyes. Agatha watched her closely enough to notice, and the older woman hated how concerned that revelation made her.

“The usual?”

“Actually, can we, uhm, talk? Or is it not a good time? I can come back later if—” She cursed at herself, wishing she didn’t sound so goddamn insecure right now. Her bruised hand was still shoved deep into her pocket, fist clenched tightly, hiding the evidence of her tumultuous morning.

“No, no. I have time,” Agatha interjected hurriedly, immediately dropping what she was doing, concern washing over her. She could tell something was bothering Rio, and she was anxious to find out what it was. “Let’s go sit over there.”

Rio nodded and walked toward the table, silently gathering the words that would explain her story. She didn’t pay much attention to her surroundings, but she vaguely registered Agatha telling Billy to take over the order she was working on, and to bring the both of them their preferred drink.

When Agatha reached the table, she found Rio sitting there, still wrapped up in her coat, anxiously gnawing at her lip while she stared out the window. “Rio?” she asked, attempting to get her attention.

The younger woman turned to face her with a silent hum of acknowledgement. She blinked once, twice, trying to ground herself into the situation.

“What is it? Are you okay? You’re scaring me…” If she was this thrown off balance, something bad must be going on. Agatha feared it had to do with her, or with Nicky. Perhaps it’d been a bit too much for her? Was she here to tell her that they should stop seeing each other so much? Or maybe she had received bad news, and didn’t know how to process it. Either way, Agatha felt anxiety pooling in her lower abdomen, worried about the woman in front of her.

“I don’t really know how to start…” Even though she had had plenty of time to think about it, now sitting in front of Agatha, the words seemed to fail her. She ran her non-dominant hand through her hair, and felt the sweat sticking to her forehead.

“Maybe start with taking off that damn jacket? You look like you’re burning up! Are you getting sick?” Agatha’s motherly instincts kicked in, and she reached forward to press the back of her hand against Rio’s forehead. To her relief, it felt warm, but not feverish.

Rio sighed and slowly shrugged off her coat, desperately trying to shield her hand from Agatha’s vision as long as possible. She revealed her outfit underneath, a simple black oversized crewneck along with oversized black jeans. She felt happy with her outfit choice, feeling as though she could hide herself in the loose-fitted clothing. “I’m gonna tell you something, but you might get mad. However you may feel, just be honest with me, alright?”

She wasn’t doing the best job at easing Agatha’s nerves, because her mind was now running wild with possibilities. Nevertheless, she still nodded, a silent promise that she would be truthful.

“Okay, well,” Rio started hesitantly, clasping her hands together tightly and moving them over the table. “I might’ve, uhm, punched Ralph this morning…”

It came out like a whisper, as if speaking the words out loud made it more real. Rio still stood by her choice to do it, though she couldn’t help but worry that it was unjustified.

Agatha’s gaze shifted from Rio’s face, down to her hands on the table, and back to her face. Her eyes widened and her mouth was ajar, a small gasp leaving her lips.

“Shit, Rio… Are you okay?” She reached for Rio’s hands, urging her to unclasp them so that she could inspect her bruises.

“I’m sorry, it was stupid. I mean, he deserved it, but I know I probably shouldn’t have.” She rambled on, not registering Agatha’s question, merely focused on defending herself and justifying her actions.

Agatha’s thumb brushed over her knuckles with so much goddamn care. It was hard to regret it when this was the outcome.

“I asked, are you okay?” Agatha repeated, her voice dripping with concern. She couldn’t care less about Rio’s apology right now. She was probably right, Ralph most likely had deserved it. Agatha also knew that Rio wasn’t pushed over the edge easily, so she worried for her. What had he done to provoke her? Why did he even go see her in the first place?

“What, me? Yeah, I’m fine, don’t worry.” Everyone knew that if a woman said she was fine, she was definitely not fine. “I just… I wanted you to hear it from me, not from him.”

“Are you sure? This bruise looks nasty.” Agatha lifted Rio’s hand to inspect it more closely. “I’m pretty sure I can feel your pulse hammering through your knuckles right now, that’s not normal.”

Rio suddenly felt uneasy, hating that she worried the older woman so much. She quickly retracted her hand, lowering it into her lap as she fiddled with her hands. “It’s okay, not that bad. It’ll heal.”

Shaking her head, Agatha stood up from her seat abruptly, turning around to walk away. Fuck. Maybe she was mad, then. Rio’s anxiety came rushing back tenfold, hitting her harder than before. The idea of upsetting Agatha led to a tight feeling in her throat, every gulp feeling like sandpaper.

Relief kicked in when she saw Agatha return, holding a kitchen towel in one hand and an icepack in the other. Without saying a word, she sat back down and held out her open palm, waiting for Rio to place her hand in hers. Rio quickly obliged and winced when she felt the pressure of the icepack on her bruises and cuts, even through the thick layer of the towel. Agatha used the ends of the towel to wrap around her hand more tightly, securing the ice in place. Her brows were furrowed, worry evident on her face. Rio felt guilty and didn’t know what to do or say to make it better.

Once Agatha was satisfied, she wrapped her hands over the makeshift bandage, applying gentle pressure as she held Rio’s hand. She finally lifted her gaze to make eye contact again, and her eyes softened. “There you go.”

“Please. I don’t want you behind the wheel half asleep.” For once, her reasons for inviting Rio over were completely selfless, no ulterior motives as she would sleep in the spare room anyway. Thus, she added ever-so-gently, “I’d worry.”

“Mmkay,” Rio gave in, her fatigue weighing too heavily on her to protest. She stretched her limbs, attempting to rid her body of at least some of the sleepiness.

“Alright, come on. Or do I need to carry you up, too?” Agatha asked teasingly. As much as Rio would’ve loved to see her try, she decided not to impose any more than she already had. Reluctantly, she lifted herself off the couch and followed closely behind Agatha.

When they reached the top of the stairs, Rio only recognised Nicky’s door, which was right across from the stairs. There was another door on the right side of the hallway, and two more on the left side. Apparently, the one on the right was the guest room, the one on the left to Nicky’s was the bathroom, and the one in the far left corner was Agatha’s room.

Good to know.

Just in case she ever needed it.

Agatha urged the younger woman to follow her as she showed her to the bathroom, where she grabbed a spare toothbrush from the cabinet. She told her she could use whatever she needed, including the makeup remover on the sink. In the meantime, Agatha went to her room to retrieve a pair of pyjamas for Rio to sleep in. As it was still quite cold and she wasn’t sure what Rio preferred to wear to bed, she settled on a simple black cotton long sleeve, and long checkered flannel pants. She quickly changed into her own purple set of pyjamas, nothing too special or revealing, though she doubted for a second whether she should pretend to wear enticing pyjamas to bed. She ended up choosing comfort over impressing a half-sleeping Rio.

When she returned in the bathroom, she found Rio barefaced, now rid of any makeup she’d been wearing. She looked so effortlessly beautiful, so soft. The grin she flashed Agatha only completed the look, causing the older woman’s chest to flutter.

“Here, you can change into these. I’ll meet you in the other room, okay?” She left Rio alone again, giving her privacy to change. Agatha opened the door to the guest room, feeling happy with her decision to keep it nice and tidy, even if they never really had unexpected guests. She expertly made the bed with fresh sheets, preparing the bed for Rio. What she was not prepared for, though, was Rio walking into the room wearing Agatha’s clothes. The pants hung low on her hips, baggy in just the right way. Her shirt had ridden up slightly when she stretched her arms, exposing a small sliver of her stomach. She noticed vague lines of ink on the visible skin, which she hadn’t noticed before. She knew that Rio had tattoos, because she had seen bits and pieces whenever Rio rolled up her sleeves, but she didn’t know the extent of it. She found herself wondering just how many tattoos she had, where the ink began and where it ended.

How could one look so cozy yet attractive?

Agatha pulled back the covers, making a gesture with her hand to motivate Rio to get on the bed. She didn’t need to be told twice, though and eagerly climbed in, clearly exhausted.

“Thanks,” she muttered softly, propping the pillow against the headboard and resting her back on it. The bed was king-size, so she looked quite small with all the space around her. Agatha had expected her to go back to sleep right away, but Rio had hands clasped together in her lap, looking at her with an unreadable expression.

“Did you have a good time tonight?” Agatha found herself asking as she sat down at the edge of the bed.

“I did. Thank you for having me, I appreciate it.”

“I should be thanking you. You saw how happy Nicky was to have you over again.”

“Just Nicky?” Rio asked with a teasing smirk and quirked eyebrow.

Agatha chuckled. “No, Rio, not just Nicky. I’m glad you came.”

Rio hadn’t expected Agatha to give in so easily, to admit that she liked spending time with her. Perhaps it was the result of everything they’d had to endure that week, and it being late. Maybe it had opened the floodgates for a split second, allowing Agatha’s true feelings to flow through.

Agatha hesitated for a second, but made a move to lie down on her stomach, facing Rio. She leaned on her elbows, propping her chin on her palm so she could look at the other woman.

“I’m impressed by how you handled him tonight, by the way. You were so good at reassuring him.” She had been replaying the scene in her head multiple times and felt a strong urge to compliment the younger woman, not wanting to leave it unspoken.

A shy blush crept up Rio’s cheeks, as if she wasn’t expecting such an honest comment from Agatha. A small frown formed on her face as she spoke her next sentence. “I felt so bad for him, I can’t believe he thought I wouldn’t want to come over again…”

“Yeah, I… I had no idea. I would’ve reassured him if I’d known…” Agatha sighed, looking down and fidgeting with her hands. She anxiously pulled on the loose skin around her cuticle. “Maybe I should’ve paid closer attention—”

“Hey, stop that. It’s not your fault.” Rio reached for Agatha’s hand, stilling her anxious movements. “We did what we could. And he seems to be okay, right?”

The hand that rested on Agatha’s was the one covered in the dinosaur bandaid, and an involuntary smile tugged on the older woman’s lips. She brushed over it gently with her thumb. “Yeah.”

“Nothing a bit of purple can’t fix,” Rio winked. Agatha let out a soft laugh, feeling slightly more light-hearted at the mention of their silly tradition.

“Yeah, you’re right. Thank you.” The look Agatha gave her was one of genuine appreciation, her eyes softer than usual. She realised she didn’t mind having a second pair of soft brown eyes to bid goodnight to.

“Alright, time for bed now. Otherwise my healing potion won’t get to do its job,” she teased. “That’s what I always tell Nicky.”

Agatha lifted herself off the mattress and rounded to Rio’s side as the younger woman scooted down, settling into the bed comfortably. Agatha grabbed the edge of the duvet and tucked it around Rio’s body, making sure she was warm and comfortable.

“If you’re gonna treat me like Nicky, then I at least deserve the goodnight kiss, too.”

Rio wasn’t sure where she found the confidence, which she would later blame on being so sleep drunk that she was unaware of her actions. This was probably partly true though, because she never would’ve spoken those words aloud if she had been a hundred percent conscious.

The result, however, was a small chuckle and roll of the eyes from Agatha, before she bent down to place a soft kiss against Rio’s temple. Her hair fell over her shoulder, tickling Rio’s face for a short second before Agatha pulled back. The scent from her perfume and hair products lingered, though, which only wrapped Rio in an extra layer of comfort. Agatha looked down just in time to see a soft smile playing on Rio’s lips, much to her own delight.

“Goodnight, Rio.”

“Goodnight, Agatha.”

When Agatha returned to her own bedroom, she laid in bed thinking about Rio. She caught herself wishing that she hadn’t owned a stupid spare room, or a stupid pull-out couch. That way, Rio would’ve had to share a bed with her.

Her thoughts were much different compared to that morning. Sure, she was still incredibly attracted to Rio, but that wasn’t her main thought. She merely longed to be close to her, to feel her body heat radiate from her even if they weren’t touching. She wondered what it would be like to feel Rio play with her hair again, like she had on the couch. Would she be able to fall asleep to the feeling?

If Agatha closed her eyes tightly enough, concentrated hard enough, she could still remember what it felt like. Like a ghostly touch hovering over her shoulder, twirling her hair and massaging her scalp. It frustrated her that the woman consuming her every thought was just down the hallway, but there was no excuse to convince her to spend the night in Agatha’s room.

Though Agatha was obliviously unaware that she had not needed an excuse — all she had to do was ask.

Chapter End Notes
