Chapter 11: French toast and flower crowns

Chapter Summary

The next morning, Nicky and Rio make breakfast together.

Chapter Notes

shorter chapter than the last, but at least the update came faster! enjoy the fluff :)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

When Rio woke up the next morning, she was only partially aware that she was not in her own bed. The feeling of the sheets against her skin was a different kind of comfortable; the way the pillow supported her neck was just a little softer than her own at home; the sounds of the rain tapping softly against the glass window above the bed indicated that it was located elsewhere compared to her own bedroom.

Memories of the previous night came back to her, and she couldn’t resist the smile tugging at the corners of her lips. It’d been a nice way to end her day. She could get used to it but preferably end up in the room on the other side of the hallway. Maybe one day, somehow.

Rio checked the time, and it was only 7.30 AM, but the sleepiness had left her body. She perked her ears, trying to listen for any other sounds in the house, indicating that the mother and son were awake. Aside from the calming drizzle, she didn’t hear anything. The woman still decided to get up due to her bladder feeling like it was about to burst. When she lifted the covers, she remembered that she was wearing Agatha’s clothes. She wondered when the older woman had worn them for the last time, wanting to feel closer to her somehow. She could get used to this, too.

She opened the door as quietly as possible, not wanting to wake the others, but when she poked her head through the doorway, she saw another door creak open. Nicky stepped into the hallway, trusted Señor Scratchy in his hand, most likely about to make his way to his mother’s room, when he noticed the movement on the other side.

“Rio?” he whisper-yelled, also aware that his mother had not yet woken up. In response, Rio shut the door behind her and walked over to him.

“Good morning,” she whispered back, ruffling his hair affectionately. He smiled up at her, thrilled to learn that the woman hadn’t left yet.

Rather than letting him go to his mother’s room, Rio guided him to the bathroom, where they both washed their faces and brushed their teeth. Rio only made a half-attempt at taming her hair, raking her fingers through it to undo some of the knots. It didn’t look great, but it could be worse.

Still donned in their pyjamas, they quietly made their way down the stairs. “Are you hungry, kiddo?”

When he nodded excitedly, she lifted him to set him down on the kitchen counter. He sat with Señor Scratchy clutched tightly to his chest, looking at Rio full of admiration. His eyes were still small from sleep, but their brown colour had a very gentle sparkle in them. He was happy.

Rio figured it would be okay for her to open the fridge and cabinets in search of ingredients for breakfast. She felt slightly invasive but knew that Agatha would most likely not mind, especially since Rio was taking care of the little boy.

She opened the fridge, looking for inspiration as to what to make. Breakfast wasn’t often her favourite meal of the day, but she wanted to make sure Nicky started his day in a positive way. She spotted butter, milk and eggs, and remembered how to make French toast. She grabbed the items along with the pack of sandwich bread, collecting it on the counter next to Nicky. He eyed the ingredients curiously, not yet sure what she was going to make.

“Wanna help me make French toast?”

“Yes!”

Rio dove into the pantry, feeling relieved when she found cinnamon and vanilla extract, the last main ingredients that she needed. She pulled one of the dining chairs into the kitchen, using it as a makeshift step stool for Nicky. He carefully slid down the counter and onto the stool, Rio standing right behind him.

A part of her had wanted to pour herself a cup of coffee, but of course Agatha had one of those complicated espresso machines at home, too. Although it seemed partly intuitive, she wasn’t entirely sure how to operate the machine, and it looked expensive, so she didn’t want to break it accidentally. She had secretly hoped to find cheap instant coffee in the pantry, but she should’ve known that it was futile. She’d simply have to wait for her caffeine fix.

Holding the plastic mixing bowl with one hand, Rio instructed Nicky how to crack the eggs. She preferred to do this as a first step, just in case any bits of the shell fell in. With her hand holding Nicky’s, they cracked the egg together, albeit a bit clumsily. When the first one successfully dropped into the bowl without any stray egg shells pooling in, Nicky whipped his head to Rio, proud grin on his face. She complimented him, telling him he did a good job.

As next step, they needed to add milk into the bowl.

“Hey, Rio?”

“Yes?”

“What did that word mean? You called me something yesterday, and I don’t think I've heard it before.”

Rio knew immediately what he was referring to, as it had taken her aback that she had so naturally let the nickname roll off her tongue when soothing the boy. She hadn’t called anyone that in a long time, not having seen her little cousins for a while.

“Cariño?” she asked, just to double-check. When Nicky nodded, recognising the word, she continued to explain. “You know how your Mama calls you sweetheart?” Another nod, while they unscrewed the lid from the milk carton. “It’s kinda like that, but in Spanish.” Another understanding nod followed, and they poured the milk into the mixing bowl together. Rio didn’t want to explain why she hadn’t used the nickname before, and what made her switch to it now. She knew deep down what it implied, as she was more than aware of the little section in her heart now closed off from anyone else, having Nicky’s name written all over it.

Rio knew she probably should have used a measuring cup but ended up pouring it based on intuition instead. It would turn out fine, even if Nicky tipped in slightly more than necessary. As the final step, they added the smallest amount of vanilla extract and a generous amount of cinnamon.

They had been so focused on the tasks at hand, that they didn’t notice Agatha coming down the stairs, after she’d been surprised that Nicky hadn’t dragged her out of bed yet. She pulled on her floral robe to wrap herself in it more tightly, leaning against the doorframe as she watched the scene in front of her.

Rio had found a whisk and given it to Nicky, allowing him to mix the ingredients together. She turned to grab a skillet, wanting to heat it on the stove already, when she noticed Agatha watching them.

“Oh, hey, morning,” Rio said, slightly taken aback. She didn’t know how long Agatha had been standing there, and suddenly felt shy. She wondered what Agatha had heard, and what she thought of the fact that they’d infiltrated her kitchen while she had been in her room. It was clear she wasn’t annoyed, though, judging by the affectionate smile that was planted on the older woman’s lips. The affectionate look in her blue eyes made Rio shiver, goosebumps appearing on her skin. Rio tried to ignore how domestic it felt, being greeted by Agatha, whose hair was messier than usual from sleep, wrapped in a purple floral robe. Rio also tried to ignore how the robe consisted of both of their favourite things: flowers and the colour purple. The realisation made the florist’s cheeks heat up.

“Good morning, you two.” She pushed herself off the doorframe, walking into the kitchen to make her presence known to Nicky, too. He whipped his head around, almost spilling the mixture from the bowl in the process, and smiled widely at his mother.

“We’re making French toast, Mama!”

“I can see that, honey. Sounds lovely.” Agatha turned her attention to Rio, who had stepped back behind Nicky to give the ingredients a final, more vigorous stir. “Is there anything I can do?”

“No, no, we’ve got it.” Rio added a small amount of butter to the pan before showing Nicky how to dip the bread into the mixture without letting it get soggy. She pointed at the bar stool at the small kitchen island, and pushed her tongue against the inside of her cheek. “You can just sit there and be pretty.”

It was way too early for Rio to flirt with Agatha so openly, so straightforwardly. And right in front of Nicky, too? She didn’t know what had possessed her, but if she ever wanted to graduate from the guest room, she’d need to grow a bit bolder. If Agatha questioned it, she’d merely pretend it was just a friendly compliment. In her defence, whenever she was in a room with Jen and Alice, she’d say things that could be considered a lot worse.

Agatha shook her head, but carried a betraying smile on her lips. She sat on the stool, leaning on her elbows to watch them work. She wasn’t a morning person, but she could get used to this, if she allowed herself to indulge.

Nicky happily prepared slice after slice, enjoying working together with Rio. When the final slices turned golden brown in the skillet, Rio turned off the stove. She let out a small yawn, but tried to hide it by closing her mouth, gulping down the yawn instead. Agatha had been watching her closely, though, and it didn’t go unnoticed.

“Haven’t had your morning coffee yet?” Agatha searched the counter for any hints of a used cup, but couldn’t find any.

Rio closed the fridge, pack of strawberries in hand, and looked at her with a sheepish smile. Her eyes drifted to the machine, and Agatha quickly realised that not everyone had experience with the intimidating equipment. “I, uh, didn’t know how to…”

The older woman nodded in understanding and was happy to finally help out, even in a small, mostly insignificant way. When she walked by Nicky, she pressed an affectionate kiss against his temple. She turned the coffee maker on and reached for two mugs, gentle hands on Rio’s waist in warning as she passed behind her.

Agatha caught Rio watching her curiously while she washed the fruit, wanting to learn how to operate the machine. “I’ll teach you one day, if you want.”

It was reassuring to Rio, hearing that the older woman wanted her to be able to operate the coffee maker, indicating that there was a possibility she’d get to spend more time at Agatha’s place. She smiled appreciatively and turned back to her own task.

“Are we cutting the fruit?” Nicky asked curiously, still standing on the chair. Rio hesitantly looked at Agatha, not sure at what age a child was allowed to use sharp kitchen tools. She knew how to entertain kids, sure, but it was small things like this that reminded her that she was inexperienced. Agatha appreciated her checking in, respecting how Rio was able to show that she wasn’t a hundred percent certain how to proceed, rather than getting in over her head and possibly putting Nicky in danger.

The older woman reached for a drawer, soon returning with a small chopping board and Montessori knives, specially made for young children to work autonomously and safely. She placed the tools in front of Nicky, and gave Rio a reassuring smile. “He’s allowed to use those. I usually still need to help him a little, though.” 

Rio patiently helped Nicky chop the strawberries, not paying much attention to the fact that the pieces were coming out rather unevenly. It only added to the charm of their breakfast. Agatha finished frothing the milk for their drinks, and reached for a small unopened bottle of vanilla syrup. Rio pretended not to notice that she had yet to remove the plastic film from the cap, indicating that it was new. Clearly, it had been part of their shopping spree when Nicky and Agatha had been on a mission to buy the things that Rio liked. Agatha obviously knew how she liked her coffee, but it was nice to see that she made the same effort at home, not just at the cafe.

Agatha carried the cups of coffee to the table, which she had set with plates and cutlery for them while waiting for the coffee to brew. She walked back to grab maple syrup, powdered sugar and Nutella, while Nicky and Rio brought over the French toast, blueberries and chopped strawberries. Agatha poured a glass of apple juice for Nicky, and they all sat down to enjoy breakfast together. The conversation flowed easily, topics light. It felt comfortable and natural, as if this was their regular routine. The realisation that this was not their normal routine, though, led to a small pang in Rio’s chest. Eventually, she’d need to go back to her own house, her own life.

It would be for the better, Rio thought. She’d need to create some distance again, already feeling like she was spending too much time with them again. She knew Nicky was happy, but what about Agatha? She wondered if Agatha felt suffocated. She was counting down the days, if not hours, till Agatha would feel too trapped and leave her alone without warning.

Nicky, always having impeccable timing, pulled her from her thoughts. “Do you want to paint with me later? We bought new stuff yesterday.”

Rio wasn’t sure if the question was directed at her, but she looked up from her plate regardless. And sure enough, his brown eyes were looking at her, full of hope. She tapped her phone to check the time, seemingly silently calculating. Agatha didn’t know what for, but didn’t want to ask either.

“Yeah, sure. I’ve got a bit of time before I need to get back.”

This answer only motivated Nicky to finish his food faster, eager to spend more quality time with the florist. Cleaning up was a team effort, even though Agatha tried to insist that she didn’t need any help. They’d collectively gotten changed out of their pyjamas, now ready for the day. Nicky excitedly grabbed the large plastic bag that held his new art supplies. There were a few A3 sized canvasses, various brushes and tubs of paint. Agatha spread a large plastic cloth over the table, already expecting things to get messy.

Rio spread the supplies on the table, but rather than sitting down, she hovered awkwardly around the table, unsure what to do. After a moment of silence, she spoke up. “So, what are you gonna paint? Inspire me.”

Nicky seemed to consider the question rather seriously, trying to decide what he was going to make. “Hmm.. A dinosaur!”

That answer was completely unsurprising to the women, and Rio wondered why she even bothered to ask. She chuckled softly, before pulling a chair out for him.

“Will you help me?”

Rio hadn’t expected him to ask that, assuming that he’d want to do his own thing for a little while. Nonetheless, she obliged, taking a seat and pulling him into her lap. She rolled his little sleeves back, in order to avoid getting paint smudged over them.

Realising that she had no clue where to start or how to paint a dinosaur, she pulled her phone out of her pocket. She decided to show Nicky several options, all cartoon versions to make it easier for herself, letting him choose which one he wanted to put on the canvas. He settled on a classic-looking dinosaur, one with a large neck. According to Google, it was a Diplodocus. Hmm. The more you know.

The woman left her phone unlocked on the table, allowing herself to study the image whenever necessary. Rio wasn’t an artist by any means, but she enjoyed being creative and had picked up various hobbies in the past. She sketched the main shapes onto the canvas with a pencil, before letting Nicky cover the entire background in a colour of his choice. Agatha sat across from them, forced by Nicky to make an artwork of her own. She settled on a more abstract approach, mixing various colours together and creating random shapes. Admittedly, it was a way for her to let loose a little, not needing to think too much. Which proved to be harder than expected, when the main subject of her thoughts was sitting across from her, interacting with her son with a tenderness she hadn’t seen in a long time, if ever.

While they were working on the background, Nicky scooped up a large amount of paint on his brush to work faster. When he leaned on the table to reach for the far end corner on the canvas, he lost control over the brush he was holding, accidentally bumping it against Rio’s dark green sweater and smudging it on her sleeve. His initial reaction was to freeze completely, before apologising profusely.

“Sorry, Rio, I-I’m so sorry!” His small voice came out panicked and frantic. He was panicking a little, memories of his father scolding him over little things like this replaying in his head. He made himself physically smaller, shrinking into his own body to brace for the impact of any harsh words. Agatha saw it all happen, and unfortunately knew very well why he was reacting like that, clearly remembering one of the incidents they had had when he was younger. They’d been having dinner together, sometime after the divorce — upon Ralph’s insistence, obviously — and he had spilled his drink over his father’s clothing. Even though they’d been in the comfort of their home and changing clothes was an easy solution, it didn’t stop Ralph from lashing out. He’d been too vain to realise that these accidents happen, especially with small children, and he had raised his voice at Nicky. Agatha had jumped in immediately, but she hadn’t been able to stop the harsh words from reaching his little ears.

It also took Agatha back to when she was younger, and how the smallest accidents and mistakes would lead to verbal and physical abuse from her mother. She had been successful at protecting Nicky from any physical abuse, as far as her knowledge went, but the memories of his father’s anger still caused him to flinch in moments like these.

Agatha wasn’t sure what to do, how to intervene, or how to soothe Nicky if Rio was going to be upset with him. A part of her knew that Rio would most likely not have a reaction as extreme as Ralph, but life taught her to always expect the unexpected; you never knew when someone was going to snap. There was a chance that Rio had reached her limit, having spent a lot of time in close proximity with the Harkness family. Her mind was preoccupied with her spiralling thoughts, and she very closely analysed Rio’s body language.

The younger woman didn’t seem to mind at all, though, and was only worried about Nicky’s reaction. She tried her best to convey that it was not a big deal and purposely kept her voice kind, calm and composed when she spoke up. “It’s okay, Nicky, it’s just paint. It’ll wash out. And even if it doesn’t, that’s fine, too.”

Agatha let out the breath that she’d been holding, realising that Rio was nothing like Nicky’s father. And definitely nothing like Evanora. She shouldn’t be surprised, and deep down, she wasn’t, but the irrational voice in her had told her that there was a slight possibility. She felt relieved that was proven to be wrong each time she watched Rio interact with her little boy.

Nicky slowly turned his head to face Rio when he realised the coast was mostly clear, no scolding following his little accident. “Y-You’re not mad?”

“This is nothing to get upset about, silly!” As if to prove her point, she picked up her paintbrush and swiped a generous amount on her own hand, smearing it out messily. She wasn’t entirely sure that the paint was body-safe, but she figured Agatha wouldn’t have bought it if it had had twenty warning signs on the package. Nicky let out a loud gasp, surprised by the action. He quickly copied her, brushing the paint against his own skin with an infectious giggle.

Agatha laughed softly, shaking her head at the chaos unfolding in front of her. Though she much preferred this scenario over the memory that had been replaying in her head.

They moved on to painting the dinosaur, using several shades of green. The room was rather silent, apart from Agatha’s music playing in the background, but Rio wanted to ensure that it was a comfortable silence, especially after Nicky’s small breakdown earlier. She mixed the green with a tiny dollop of black paint to darken the colour. “Did you know green’s my favourite?”

Nicky perked up at the information, happy to learn more about Rio. Agatha, too, added this piece of information to her mental note of Rio’s favourites. Within only a day, her knowledge was expanding rapidly. “Really?”

“Yes! Specifically this shade,” she explained as she pointed at the emerald green on their paint palette. Agatha craned her neck to see what she was pointing at, just in case she ever needed the information.

“That’s why you wear green so much!”

Rio chuckled. “Yeah, buddy, you’re right. What’s your favourite?”

“I like that one.” When he pointed at the bright blue colour, it made sense why he had painted the majority of the background blue. The shade of light blue reminded her of the colour of Agatha’s eyes, which, admittedly, was becoming one of her favourites, too.

Almost automatically, Rio looked up at the woman sitting across from them, only to find her already staring back at her. “That’s a beautiful colour, indeed.”

A faint blush tinged Rio’s cheeks, which was the only reason Agatha caught onto what she had implied. She hadn’t thought much of it, not really associating the blue with her own eyes, but the way Rio had looked deeply into her eyes had given her true intentions away. Agatha bit the inside of her cheek to refrain her smile from growing too much.

When Nicky finished colouring in the outlines that Rio had marked clearly for him, the dinosaur was as good as finished. Rio had one final touch to add, though. When Nicky granted her permission to go ahead, she added a small flower crown on the dinosaur’s head, using bright colours for the flower petals. She mostly used shades of purple but tried to cover it up by adding a little bit of orange, too. This way, all of their favourite colours were present in the painting.

The two admired the end result, clearly proud of their work. Rio carefully lifted the canvas, turning it to Agatha. “What do you think? Isn’t that the best Diplo-whatever you’ve ever seen?”

“Diplodocus!” Nicky jumped in with a soft giggle.

Agatha squinted, looking at the painting across from her and to Rio’s phone, where the reference image was, but was slightly too far for her to see clearly. “Lemme see what it’s supposed to look like again.”

Rio held up her phone with her free hand, putting the phone and painting side by side for easier comparison. Unbeknownst to Rio, a Hinge notification had popped up at the top of the screen.

Jess liked you! Tap to see the comment on your photo.

Agatha allowed her eyes to skim over the notification, curiosity taking over, but tried very hard to keep her face neutral, not wanting to give anything away. “I’m impressed. Very good job, guys.”

She tried her best to convey real pride in her compliment, but her monotonous voice betrayed her.

Was Rio actively dating? Had she been chatting with people on those apps since her friends had edited her profile? Was she just spending time with her and Nicky to pass time?

Agatha found herself wondering how many people Rio was talking to, for how long, and if she’d been on any dates. She wondered if Rio had a favourite person on those apps, too. However, this was the only one of Rio’s favourites that Agatha was not eager to find out. The idea of the younger woman being involved with other women caused overwhelming jealousy to wash over Agatha, even if it was unjustified. It wasn’t her place to be jealous; she couldn’t be possessive over Rio, but part of her wished that these soft and vulnerable sides of the florist were reserved just for her and Nicky.

Rio, completely unaware of Agatha’s spiral, put her phone down and beamed at Nicky, who was equally proud to receive his mother’s approval. They left the painting to dry and went over to the sink to wash their hands, scrubbing off the paint that had made its way on their skin, whether on purpose or by accident.

The older woman shook her head, pulling herself back from her spiralling thoughts. There was no need to jump to conclusions. Besides, Rio deserved to date a nice woman, she deserved to feel loved. It shouldn’t matter to Agatha. It shouldn’t. But it did.

Remembering the stain that her son had caused on Rio’s sleeve, she got up from her seat to walk over to them.

“Would you like me to wash that for you?” She pointed at Nicky playfully when she spoke next. “This one over here has made me an expert at getting those stubborn stains out.”

Nicky smiled sheepishly, knowing very well that he had oftentimes spilled things on his clothing that took several attempts and different methods to get clean again. Rio considered the offer, and although she felt guilty for leaving Agatha to deal with it, she also knew her own chances of treating the stain properly were slim to none.

“If you don’t mind…”

“It’s the least I can do.” She turned to Nicky, who had finished drying off his hands. “Nicky, honey, can you grab a sweater from Mama’s closet for Rio to wear?”

Happy to be sent on a special mission, Nicky sprinted off right away. When he returned, he had a long-sleeve clutched in his hands and held it out for Rio. She did mention that Lucy wanted more quality time just the other week, so it could be the solution to her case. Dwelling on the possibility, Agatha felt a small weight hook into the bottom of her heart, slowly pulling it deeper into the pits of her stomach.

She continued her day as usual, but Lilia noticed a shift in Agatha’s mood compared to before. The older woman brushed the grey curls out of her face when she addressed the cafe owner. “Have you spoken to Rio? She told me yesterday that she was sick, just in case any customer came in to ask.”

Relief washed over her, before she knitted her brows together in concern. She shook her head in response, because truthfully, she hadn’t spoken to Rio at all, wanting to give her some space. That was out of the window now, though, because Agatha had become worried about her. She quickly excused herself and took out her phone, scrolling to Rio’s contact. Without hesitation, she pressed on the number and lifted her phone to her ear, listening impatiently while the phone rang.

It took a couple of rings before she heard a groggy Rio on the other end, the usually smooth voice turned hoarse from extensively coughing. “Hello?”

She sounded tired, exhausted even, and a little confused.

“Hey, Rio.”

“Oh, Agatha,” Rio sounded surprised as she cleared her throat, and Agatha could hear a soft shuffle through the phone, probably repositioning herself to make her voice come out clearer. “Hey.”

“Are you alright? Lilia told me you’re sick…” Agatha’s teeth sunk into her bottom lip, worrying at the flesh while she waited for a response.

“Yeah, I must’ve caught something when I was out with the girls on Sunday. I’m not gonna lie, it’s pretty bad.” Rio sniffled on the phone, her nose runny from the sickness she was battling.

Agatha shifted her weight from one leg to the other, tapping against the surface of the table she was standing by. “Can I… do anything to help?”

There was a beat of silence, as if Rio was weighing her options here. Agatha decided she was willing to fight her if she said no.

“I’ll be fine, but thank you.” Agatha was about to interject when Rio continued. “I’ve got Lucy here, who I instructed to tell the world if I happen to die.”

Alright, no fighting then. Agatha wasn’t going to meddle in whatever that was, she had at least some dignity left. Though she hated that Lucy was there first, jumping at the chance to take care of Rio. Fucking hell.

“Oh, okay… Well, let me know if you need anything.” Agatha’s voice came out more defeated than she had hoped, but she prayed it just sounded like concern to the florist’s ears.

When they ended the call, Agatha tried her best to distract herself with work, even going as far as chatting with the customers to keep her busy. That was certainly new.

Thankfully, it worked, and it was already time for Agatha to get Nicky from school. She quickly went to the market beforehand, buying fresh vegetables for yet another soup that she’d force Nicky to have to make sure he made a full recovery.

Exhausted from spending the whole day with his school friends again, Nicky crashed on the couch, mindlessly watching television while Agatha washed the greens. She kept an eye on her son, watching his chest rise and fall with each heavy breath that he took. Her gaze drifted to the windowsill, catching the purple orchid in the green flower pot, blooming beautifully. To her surprise, she had actually remembered to water it, in contrast to all the other plants she had previously owned. Something in her made sure to nurture Rio’s gift, and in the back of her mind, Agatha knew damn well why. Looking at it now, though, felt bittersweet, because Agatha felt like her bond with Rio wasn’t flourishing as much as this plant was thriving. It seemed like their relationship needed more than water and sunshine to survive, external Lucy-shaped factors affecting everything in a way that Agatha wasn’t certain they’d be able to withstand.

She was pulled from her thoughts by a vibration in her pocket. Curiosity ever getting the better of her, Agatha fished it from her back pocket and looked at the notification, only to be surprised to see Rio’s name light up on the screen. Her heart quickened, and she opened the message right away, worried that something might’ve happened.

Rio: sent a picture

Rio: btw i’m coughing all over your hoodie rn so i don’t think you’ll want this back…

There was a photo in their chat, the majority of the screen taken up by the cracked pink outlines of the palmistry print on the black fabric that Agatha recognised immediately. In the upper right corner, she saw Rio’s jaw, chin and lips before the picture got cut off. There was a hint of a smile pulling at the corner of the plump lips that Agatha stared more intently at than she would care to admit.

Wanting to keep things lighthearted and friendly teasing, especially with Lucy roaming around her place, Agatha typed an easy response. After pressing ‘send', she went back to chopping the vegetables.

Nice try. I’ll wash it.

She didn’t expect Rio to fight her on it, though she was prepared for another joke to be directed at her, because Rio never gave up. Not even when she was accompanied by another woman; had she no shame claiming Agatha’s clothes when she probably had easy access to Lucy’s? Perhaps it was the challenge that excited her, which made Agatha reconsider the entire banter they’d had going on.

Rio: boooo

Rio: can’t even let me win when i’m sick :(

Agatha sighed, well aware that she was becoming unable to deny Rio anything she asked for. Even if she couldn’t see her pleading brown eyes right now, Agatha knew she’d crumble the moment she did, and she wasn’t ready to deal with those consequences, if Rio ever voiced her request outside of the comfort of her phone screen.

Keep it. It looks better on you anyway.

The heart reaction to that message was immediate, a tell-tale sign that Rio had won this battle, and Agatha willingly let her. The next notification arrived with the confetti effects, yet another display of the childishness that Rio possessed and Agatha loved, even if she pretended to get annoyed.

Rio: thank you thank you thank you

Rio: Luci thinks so, too :’) he likes how soft it is

The last notification confused Agatha yet simultaneously made her grow more concerned about Rio’s wellbeing. Luci with an ‘i’? He? Two typos in one message — that was unlike her. Had the woman gone delirious from the fever reaching her brain?

Unless, of course, that was how Lucy/Luci preferred to be addressed. Agatha could imagine that it wouldn’t matter much to Rio, most likely able to love someone regardless of their pronouns. She just realised that she had never actually heard Rio use pronouns whenever she had mentioned the mysterious person.

Somehow, Agatha couldn’t shake the feeling in her gut, and still typed her next message before she could overthink it.

He?

Her heart was beating rapidly in anticipation of the next reply, anxiously staring at the text bubble at the bottom of the screen.

Rio: yeah?

“Oh,” Agatha whispered to herself. There was a small beat of silence in the chat, consisting of Agatha typing and removing words in the text box, not knowing how to respond. From Rio’s point of view, there was merely a text bubble popping up and disappearing, over and over again. Thus, Rio had seemingly taken matters into her own hands, growing impatient.

Rio: wait, you’ve never seen him?

No?

Agatha placed her phone back on the counter as she finished cutting the ingredients for their meal, though her eyes kept darting to the unlocked screen, her entire body filled to the brim with anxious anticipation.

Rio: Agatha, meet Lucifer, the absolute menace that terrorises my home

There was a logical explanation as to why the message took so long to send because there was yet another picture on display in their chat. Not dissimilar to the previous one, this, too, had the majority of the screen filled with the black fabric of Agatha’s former hoodie. However, this time, the line work was partly covered in black, and Agatha had to squint to see it clearly. There was a small black cat lying on top of Rio’s chest, its soft fur barely visible due to lack of contrast. The angle was the same as before, but Rio’s lips were no longer forming a smile, opting for a kiss pressed to the top of her pet’s head instead.

Oh. Luci. Not Lucy.

Luci. As in Lucifer.

Cool, cool, cool. Luckily, Agatha hadn’t overreacted, whatsoever. There goes another name, Agatha thought to herself, moved from the hit list to the ‘I tolerate’ list.

Agatha stared at her phone screen, and for the first time since hearing the name ‘Lucy’, she felt the defences that were specifically lowered, and ultimately reinstalled, for Rio, break down again, albeit ever so slightly. The weight pulling on her heart unhooked itself and evaporated, allowing the organ to travel from the pits of her stomach back to its original spot, beating in a healthy manner.

With a deep sigh, she walked over to Nicky and showed him the picture, knowing that her son wouldn’t want this information about his proclaimed best friend to be kept from him. He looked at her with a massive grin opening up his face, brown eyes sparkling warmly at the newfound information.

He’s very cute. Nicky wants to meet him.

Rio: you guys should come over sometime

The invitation led to a small smile on Agatha’s face, allowing herself to imagine what Rio’s house looked like on the inside, and picturing Nicky in the space.

Maybe when you’re not spreading your bacteria all over the place…

Rio: aww i thought you’d like me coughing all up in your personal space :/

The reminder of Rio being sick pulled Agatha back to reality. She shook her head in disbelief, not understanding why on earth Rio would decline her offer earlier, claiming that Luci would look out for her. Agatha resisted the urge to scold her for it and jumped into action instead, but not before texting a quick response.

I’m good, thanks. Nicky’s done enough of that already.

She informed Nicky of her plan, unsure whether he was in good enough spirits to agree. Unsurprisingly, Nicky was magically recharged with newfound energy, eagerly nodding along.

The soup was boiling on the stove, and Agatha dipped a small spoon into the pot, blowing on it before tasting it. When she was satisfied, she turned the stove off and placed the lid back on, letting it sit while she quickly took care of the dishes. She hesitated for a moment before texting Alice to ask for Rio’s address. Weirdly, there were no questions asked, and she received it immediately. She didn’t have the time to wonder why that was.

After a quick pitstop in front of the mirror in the hallway to straighten herself out, Agatha walked back into the kitchen to grab the necessities. Nicky appeared on her side, holding a large empty glass bottle, trying to unscrew the lid. He held it up in the air, waving it in front of his mother as if she’d catch on immediately. “Mama, you’re forgetting something!”

“Oh, right. Of course. Thank you, honey.”

Now they had everything, and Agatha secured Nicky into his car seat in the back and put the soup pot on the passenger seat, checking once more if it was sealed properly so it wouldn’t spill everywhere. She typed the address into her navigation and pulled out of her driveway, glancing at Nicky through the rearview mirror and melting at the beaming child, Señor Scratchy clutched in one hand, glass bottle in the other, both safely placed into his lap.

When they reached Rio’s front door, Agatha suddenly felt self-conscious, wondering whether she should have done this. What if Rio had wanted to just be alone, clearly exhausted from being sick?

There was no time for Agatha to reconsider and turn around, though, because Nicky knocked on the door loudly, unaware of his mother’s internal battle. Well, here we go.

Unsure whether Rio had heard them, Nicky pointed at the doorbell, which was just out of reach for him, gesturing for his mother to take action instead.

After the bell buzzed, they could faintly hear the sound of shuffling and approaching footsteps, growing louder as Rio neared the door. The door opened to reveal Rio, wrapped in a big, fuzzy blanket that she clutched closed tightly with one hand at the chest, needing the other to pull on the door handle. There was movement behind Rio’s legs, and she quickly used her body to block the door opening.

“Agatha? Nicky? What are you doing here?” Both eyebrows were raised, and her voice was laced with surprise, but it never turned unkind, no indication that they hadn’t been welcome.

“Soup delivery. Doctor’s orders.” Agatha shrugged innocently and held up the pot with both hands in emphasis of her answer.

“And I’m here to meet Luci!”

Rio couldn’t suppress the smile that forced its way to her lips, and stepped back so they could enter her apartment. Agatha quickly kicked off her boots without using her hands and ordered Nicky to take his shoes off too, before making her way into the apartment, which was a lot more spacious than Agatha had expected. The hallway had a large shoe rack and several hooks for coats and bags, and she walked through the open door on the left to reveal an open floor plan for the living space; a large kitchen with dark wood cabinets and black granite counter top situated on her left, sizeable dining table across from it. On her right, there was a large Persian-style patchwork rug, mixing reds, greens, blues and beige beautifully, complementing the large dark green corduroy couch. On the couch was another thick blanket, messily spread out, clear evidence that Rio had been resting there. Unsurprisingly, there were many, many, plants and flowers in the house, most of which Agatha couldn’t name. There was a black multi-layered scratching pole for Luci, covered in Halloween-style spiderwebs in white stitching, situated next to a black coffin-shaped cat bed. There were big windows, allowing the natural light to seep in and sparkle against every surface. Agatha figured the other doors in the hallway led to the remainder of the house, like the bathroom and bedroom.

Agatha didn’t know what she expected, but this suited the florist so well. The combination of dark, earthy tones mixed with the bright colours of her flowers, plants, vases, and various ornaments really seemed to describe her entire being perfectly.

She placed the soup on the counter and turned around to apologise for ambushing her, only to find Nicky and Luci getting familiar with each other, Señor Scratchy and the glass bottle now sitting on the floor. There he was, the culprit that had plagued Agatha’s mind without even knowing she existed. Nicky carefully reached to pet the small animal and in turn, Luci leaned into the touch. It was adorable to watch, and Agatha was even happier with her decision to do this. Rio shuffled into her direction and looked at her with tired eyes, though they seemed to be layered with something else, something Agatha couldn’t quite pinpoint.

“Do you mind?” Agatha asked, pointing at the induction stove on Rio’s counter, already reaching for the button to turn it on. Rio quickly shook her head, careful not to get dizzy from the sudden movement. The florist advanced to the cupboard, most likely wanting to help out, but Agatha quickly stopped her with a warning look. “Please, Rio, sit down.”

Reluctantly, Rio took a seat at the dining table, watching as Agatha worked in the kitchen while looking out for Luci and Nicky, making sure they were treating each other nicely. She pulled her knees up to her chest and leaned her chin on top of them, wrapping herself up in the blanket that was covering her shoulders. It only took a couple of minutes for Agatha to reheat the soup, and she grabbed three bowls that she found upon opening random cupboards, feeling her way through the unfamiliar kitchen. She filled all of them with her homemade soup and carried them over to the table, placing the steaming bowl in front of Rio along with a spoon. She happily accepted the meal, trying to smell the food even through her blocked nose. Agatha called Nicky to the table and blew gently to lower the temperature of the soup before feeding him a spoonful.

“Thanks for this, Agatha. Really.” Rio looked up from her meal and met Agatha’s blue eyes, smiling in appreciation. The exhaustion was evident on her face, but she seemed to be in good spirits.

“My pleasure,” Agatha responded earnestly.

“But… I am worried I’ll get you guys sick…” Rio trailed off, worried that the short distance between them wasn’t enough to keep her from transmitting her flu.

“Oh, don’t worry. After the flu he came down with last weekend, I’m pretty sure he has enough antibodies to last him a lifetime.” A reassuring smile accompanied her statement, wanting to ease Rio’s mind rather than adding onto her worries.

“Then what about you?” Rio grunted groggily.

“I have mother privilege, I don’t get sick.” It was true: ever since having Nicky, Agatha had rarely been sick. Or better yet, she had rarely allowed herself to be sick, even when her body protested; someone had to keep the household afloat, and that wasn’t going to be Nicky.

Rio eyed her with a look that told Agatha she wasn’t fully convinced, but she didn’t have the energy to fight her. They ate in relative silence, and it was abundantly clear to her that both Nicky and Rio had been in need of some peace and quiet, so she happily obliged. After their meal, Agatha ordered Rio to lie down on the couch while she cleared the table. Nicky was completely enthralled by the black cat, his eyes following the small creature everywhere he went. He sat down on the rug in the living room, patting the floor in hopes of Luci walking over. Rio turned to her side, fighting her heavy eyelids so she could keep an eye on them.

When Agatha made her way to the couch with an empty glass in her hand, Nicky remembered the bottle he had brought. He walked back into the hallway to retrieve it, along with his favourite stuffed rabbit.

“Rio!”

“Shhh, inside voice, please, Nicky,” Agatha reminded him gently, already feeling bad for invading Rio’s safe space.

“Sorry,” he whispered before continuing in his regular voice. “Here, we brought you some purple so you can feel better. It helped me a lot!”

Rio lifted herself off the couch with a strained push from her hand, using all of her energy to sit up. The blanket dropped, revealing the famous black hoodie underneath, and Agatha’s heart swelled at the sight of a vulnerable-looking Rio in her clothing. Agatha might've lost the battle, but she felt like she won the war, as this view was more than worth it. The idea that Rio wanted to basically live in the item pleased Agatha a concerning amount. The older woman helped Nicky pour the purple ‘magic’ drink into the glass, careful not to spill it everywhere. The boy used both hands to lift the cup within Rio’s reach, handing it over cautiously. Rio felt an urge to cry, incredibly overwhelmed by the two people in front of her, showering her with the utmost care. She gulped down the lump that had formed in her throat, blinking away the shiny layer that washed over her irises.

“Thank you, cariño.” She gently raked her hand through his hair, before cupping his cheek and caressing it.

Nicky recognised the nickname, and his entire face lit up. Rio quickly took a sip to mask her emotions. Agatha watched the interaction fondly but decided to stay quiet.

The florist grasped the edges of the blanket again, lifting it over her shoulder as she shivered slightly. Agatha reached out to press the back of her hand against her forehead, checking if she had a fever. “You’re burning up a little. Do you have a washcloth I can borrow?”

After short yet clear instructions of where the bathroom was located, Agatha disappeared to grab what she needed. She returned with a wet washcloth, drenched in cold water. She took a seat on the couch next to Rio and patted her lap, to which Rio just responded with a puzzled look. Agatha wrapped an arm around her shoulder, pulling Rio down gently so her head landed in Agatha’s lap. She was lying on her side, using Agatha’s lap as a supporting pillow. The older woman adjusted the blanket over her shoulder, tucking it around her nicely, and placed the cool cloth against her forehead. Rio sighed in response and finally allowed her eyes to flutter shut, albeit momentarily. She only opened them when she felt an unknown object press against her, only to find Nicky tucking Señor Scratchy under the blanket and into her arms. That was the final straw, and Rio closed her eyes tightly, allowing a single tear to escape from the corner of her eye and seep into the fabric of Agatha’s pants, who was completely unaware. Rio couldn’t remember the last time someone had willingly taken care of her like this, and she was silently warning herself not to get used to it.

That thought was incredibly hard to hold on to, with Agatha’s fingers gently raking through her hair and rubbing comforting circles on her back. She took a few deep breaths, savouring the moment. In all honesty, she had started to feel a little bit better. That purple potion placebo effect was welcomed with open arms.

“Mama!” Nicky whisper-yelled, hovering over one of the plants on the floor. “I’ve never seen a plant like this before. Why is this dirt so weird?” He lifted it in his arms, and it was surprisingly light, and he carried it over to where his mother was sitting. Agatha wasn’t a botanist, but she did know what was going on here.

“My, my… Rio… Is this plant fake?!” Agatha teased, hands still playing with Rio’s soft hair. Rio opened one eye, squinting against the light to see what Nicky was holding.

“Mhmm,” she hummed absentmindedly, eyes disappearing behind her lids again. “That one is. Some plants are toxic to Luci, but I still like how they look.”

Agatha and Nicky both nodded at the information, neither of them knowing that there were plants that were dangerous to cats. Agatha added it to her mental list of facts about Rio, and she let her eyes wander the rest of the space. There was a small wooden shelf next to the television, and various plants scattered across it. One item in particular caught her eye, though. It was a black picture frame with hand-painted green and purple flowers. Behind the glass wasn’t a picture, but Nicky’s drawing from a few weeks ago, displayed proudly in the centre of the shelf.

Rio hadn’t been lying when she said she’d find a good spot for it.

She’d point it out to Nicky later, not wanting to disturb the peaceful quiet right now. Agatha tentatively lifted the cloth from Rio’s forehead, using her hand to gauge Rio’s temperature once again.

“What’s the prognosis, doctor?” Rio said with a hint of teasing laced in her voice.

“Hmm… I think you’ll live.” The florist couldn’t see Agatha’s face, but she could hear the smirk in her voice.

“Great, I don’t know what I’d do with the shop otherwise.”

Nicky perked up at this comment, excited to share his two cents. “Don’t worry, Rio! I could look after the store for you!”

“I’m sure you could, buddy. I don’t doubt that for a second.”
