Chapter 14: Am I no good?

Chapter Summary

some angst, some fluff. enjoy the ride!

warning: mentions of nausea/vomiting, not in major detail tho

Chapter Notes

im a relatively silent lurker on agathario twt and i saw a tweet asking for updates on a few fics, including MINE?! that was wild. here you go, this is 14,5k chapter is for u, along w a platonic smooch if u want :*

I also saw someone complaining that it’s been 13 chapters and no kiss, asking when the burning is gonna start… IJBOL im so sorry i feel like i’ve taken the term 'slow burn' too seriously. gotta stick to the bit now, unforch. but significant things are happening to get them moving!! one day it’ll happen, trust.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

It was the end of the week, and everyone was luckily nursed back to good health, resuming their usual routines. Nicky was able to sit through the entire school day again, whereas Agatha could once again enjoy Rio’s presence during her coffee breaks. With the Luci mystery now solved, Agatha felt a lot more at ease around the florist again, less worried that she was competing against someone else.

However, because Nicky had been sick the other weekend, he had missed his time with Ralph. Not that Ralph had even offered to take care of Nicky, but Agatha would never have let him anyway. But that did mean that on Friday night, the mother had a text message waiting in her app, asking if Nicky would make up for lost time.

With a deep sigh, Agatha ran her hand through her hair, holding the phone in the other. She shifted her weight from one leg to the other, contemplating what to do. She was a firm believer that Nicky should be given autonomy over his own choices — within certain boundaries — and decided to ask him what he wanted to do.

When he responded that he, in fact, did not want to see his father, Agatha simply accepted that as an answer and relayed the message to Ralph, not bothering to wait for his reaction.

“But maybe we can see Rio tomorrow?”

Although Agatha wasn’t completely against the idea, she also knew it wasn’t really an option. Rio had missed a lot of work due to falling sick before, and given the history between Rio and Ralph, Agatha didn’t want to know what would happen if Nicky’s father found out he spent time with the florist instead of him.

“Tomorrow might not be the best idea, honey.” She saw the pout growing on his face, so she quickly made an offer he would very likely not refuse. “But we can ask Aunt Wanda if you can hang out with the boys?”

As expected, he was quick to agree with the new plan. He even went to bed without any trouble that night, excited for the next day to come even sooner. When they woke up in the morning, he grabbed Señor Scratchy along with a few of his other favourite toys, and waited patiently for Agatha to take him to their house.

“Thanks for this, Wanda,” Agatha said to her best friend, pulling her in for a quick hug. “He’s super excited.”

“Of course, we love having him over. Will you swing by later?”

“Yeah. Let me know if he causes any trouble though, okay?”

They both knew their children well enough to understand that there would very likely be some mischief and trouble, though it was usually nothing major. The women agreed to meet up again at the end of the day, allowing Agatha to take some time to herself for once. She wasn’t planning anything special. If anything, she enjoyed checking off tasks from the list that was ever-growing and rewarding herself with some downtime on the couch.

She was in the middle of wiping down the coffee table when her phone rang. Without looking at the screen, she picked up and pulled her phone to her ear, pressing it between her ear and shoulder while she continued working. “Hello?”

“Hello, Agatha.”

Agatha stopped in her tracks, frozen in her spot, awkwardly bent at the waist to reach the table. She knew that voice all too well. She also knew that this person hardly ever called, and when she did, it was never good news. She slowly straightened while clearing her throat.

“Hello, mother. To what do I owe the pleasure?” The sarcasm dripped from her voice, her fingers itching to press the big red button on the screen.

“I heard Nicholas is not with his father.”

Of course, the only reason she’d call was to stand up for Ralph. Typical.

“Correct.”

“Why on Earth is that?”

“He didn’t want to, and I’m not going to force him.” Agatha kept her answers short and to the point, not wanting to give Evanora any opportunity to use her words against her.

“Force him? Agatha, please stop this nonsense. Ralph told me what happened with your friend.”

Agatha’s breathing caught in the back of her throat, wondering just how much Ralph had shared, and how much he had blown it out of proportion merely out of spite.

The cold voice on the other end of the line continued. “You should stop holding grudges against him. See, he’s still making an effort to see his son even after he got assaulted.”

A scoff escaped Agatha’s lips and she rolled her eyes so hard that Evanora could feel it through the phone connection. Before Agatha could interject, her mother’s voice sounded through the phone once again.

“We all know you’re brainwashing this poor child. Who wouldn’t want to see his own father?”

“Excuse me?”

“You heard me.” The voice came out stern, a version that Agatha remembered all too well from her earlier days. Then, ever the actress that Evanora was, the voice turned softer, almost heavy with emotion. “It hurts me, you know… My grandchild being unable to connect with his father, all because of my own daughter. It breaks my heart.”

At this, Agatha couldn’t bite back the laugh that escaped her lips. Boo-fucking-hoo.

“That’s funny! I didn’t know it existed,” she spat out. “That thing in your chest. I always thought it was missing. One big, gaping, black hole.”

Clearly not expecting this reaction, Evanora quickly scrambled for words to throw back her way. “Don’t be ridiculous. If anyone’s missing it, it’s you. It left your body right when you decided to sell your soul to the Devil.”

Not this again, Agatha thought. After all the years of hearing her mother repeat the same things to her over and over, she was hardly affected by it. She had learned to tune out and simply hum along in response. Evanora’s next words, however, hit her in a spot that had only barely started to scar over.

“All the bad things happening to you are a result of your entire being, your entire lifestyle. I can only pray it doesn’t spill over to Nicky, you remember how sick he got…” All the memories came rushing back to her and she felt a tightness in her chest, sitting her body down on the floor by the coffee table in search of some stability. Even through the phone, Agatha could feel the knife Evanora was wedging through her heart, but not before holding it above a blazing fire just to get the metal burning hot. As the cherry on top, she twisted the knife, reopening the old wounds far enough to flick a fuck-load of salt into them. “I wouldn’t be surprised if different illnesses would keep plaguing him until they eventually fully wring the life out of him, all because of your own bad karma. If you care about him, you won’t keep him all to yourself, just so he has a chance in this world.”

There was a loud ringing noise echoing through Agatha’s head, accompanied by the words her evil mother was spitting at her. Evanora’s voice infiltrated her brain and befriended the voice that carried Agatha’s anxiety, her deepest darkest fears, and invited it to dance. Together, the voices waltzed from left to right, wrapped up tightly in each other, one amplifying the other and vice versa.

The one main goal Agatha had, the one purpose she felt strongly about, was taking care of Nicky in ways that nobody had done for her. She strived to give him everything he required and desired. The one time she felt like she had failed miserably at that, was when he had been stuck to a hospital bed, begging his mother to go back home so he could play with his toys again. Agatha had felt helpless, leaving him in the hands of doctors and medicine, worried that there might never be a day he would get to do so. For Evanora to use that against her, that should’ve been too far, even for her. She couldn’t believe Evanora called her evil, when this was the example she grew up with.

Agatha felt her throat constricting, her breathing shuddered, and her chest rose and fell rapidly. There were tears welling in her eyes, blurring her vision. She hadn’t had a moment like this in years, but she recognised it like no other; she was about to have a panic attack.

Unable and unwilling to register anything else Evanora was spewing, she miraculously found the button on the phone to end the call, before dropping the phone on the floor in front of her, pulling her legs up tightly to her chest, willing her breathing to even out. Fuck.

She vaguely registered the phone vibrating at some point, but she didn’t have the energy to pick it up and look at the screen to see what else her mother had left to say.

—————

In the back of Westview’s famous flower shop was Rio, relieved to be back at work and catch up on everything she had missed. It was a regular Saturday for her, no big event to cater to for once, and she finally had time to enjoy being surrounded by all the greenery as she watered the plants, snipped the flower stems and rearranged their positions based on the sunlight that was casting through the windows more and more now that winter was coming to a slow end.

Her peaceful quiet was disrupted by the buzzing of her phone. It was her personal one, not from the store, which caught her off guard. Not many people called her out of the blue. She read the name on the caller ID and frowned, confused.

“Wanda?”

“Hey, Rio,” the greeting came out slightly breathy, not as light and breezy as the redhead usually was.

Well aware that she would not be calling without a reason, she asked, “What’s up?”

“Is Agatha with you?”

At the mention of her name, Rio’s heart rate picked up speed. “N-No, why?”

“Oh, shit, okay, um,” Wanda shuffled on the other end of the line, her voice muffled as she covered the microphone with her hand, talking to someone else.

“Wanda? Why?” Rio probed again, growing restless. She had a nagging feeling in her gut, telling her something was wrong.

“Oh, nothing. I just— I can’t get a hold of her and I hoping she was just preoccupied or something.” Although Wanda tried her best to cover the concern in her voice, Rio picked up on it loud and clear.

“Is everything okay?” Nice job, Rio. Of course it wasn’t, otherwise she wouldn’t be calling you. Fucking dumbass, Rio mentally scolded herself.

“Yeah, it’s probably nothing…” the best friend started in an attempt to reassure Rio, before considering her options, settling for the truth. “Nicky’s here and she said she’d come over later. She hasn’t responded to my texts or calls. Usually she’s much quicker, especially when Nicky’s over.”

“What can I do?”

“You don’t need to do anything, Rio. I just needed to know if she was with you, that’s all.” The redhead did not sound relaxed at all. If anything, she sounded more worried, despite her effort not to show it.

“Okay, but she’s not with me. So, what’s next?” Not willing to back down, the uneasy feeling nestled in her chest. “Do you need me to go check on her? Or would you like to do it? I could watch Nicky?”

“No, that’s okay. I don’t want to worry him. But… if you want to check on her, that’d help ease my mind…”

Before Wanda even finished that sentence, Rio was in the back room, gathering her belongings. She quickly ditched her trusty apron and pulled her arms through the sleeves of her jacket, bag in one hand and her bundle of keys in the other. She clipped the carabiner to one of the loops in her jeans after locking the door of her shop. “Of course. Do you think I’ll need a key?”

“There’s a spare one at the cafe. Ask Lilia, she’ll give it to you.”

“Alright. I’ll text you once I’m there?”

“Yes, please.” The voice on the other end of the line seemed to smoothen out, relieved to have shared some of the weight of her worries. “And Rio?”

“Yeah?” she asked, looking both ways while she crossed the street, the phone still pressed to her ear.

“Thank you.” It was genuine, Rio could feel it even through the phone. She muttered a quick ‘you’re welcome’ back before ending the call, stepping into the cafe with only one goal: getting Agatha’s spare key. Luckily, Lilia did not question it in the slightest, even though Rio didn’t explain the whole situation to her, not wanting to worry the older woman if there was no reason to. She quickly thanked her, then made her way to Agatha’s house, the route slowly but surely becoming muscle memory.

Rio tried not to let her doubts and worries get the upper hand, and she hoped Agatha would simply be busy cleaning the house with music that was way too loud, or taking a relaxing bath. Anything. As long as she was okay.

That hope was hard to hold on to when she reached the front door and rang the doorbell once, then twice, and impatiently tapped her foot. She knew she had the key, but if Agatha was fine, she didn’t want to barge into her house unannounced. She could imagine that wouldn’t go over well.

However, given that there was no response, she figured it would be okay to let herself in. If not, she’d deal with that later. With trembling hands, she inserted the key, twisting it to unlock the front door.

“Agatha?” she called out hesitantly. Once again, she was met with silence, so she quickly kicked off her boots, not bothering with her jacket, and stepped into the house. She rounded the corner, and entered the kitchen. She frantically scanned the space with her eyes, her heart beating louder and louder when she didn’t spot her favourite brunette. She didn’t see her standing in the kitchen, wiping the cupboard with noise-cancelling headphones. Rio also didn’t hear the sound of a shower running, so that was out of the equation. A bath, perhaps?

She peered over to the living room just to make sure, and stopped dead in her tracks when she saw who she was looking for. Rio had almost missed her, from the way Agatha had made herself incredibly small, perched on the floor. Not wanting to startle her, she resisted the urge to run over, but her pace was quick and deliberate when she walked over. Rio shrugged off her jacket and ditched it on the dining table, taking in Agatha’s appearance as she got closer.

The woman was sitting on the floor, knees pulled to her chest and her elbows propped on them, using her hands to cover her eyes as she rocked back and forth slightly. The purple orchid was moved from its designated spot on the windowsill, now placed on the ground next to Agatha, though she wasn’t doing anything with it; it was just… there.

Relieved that she had found her, yet worried about the state she found her in, Rio dropped herself to the floor in front of her. She recognised the signs of the panic attack Agatha was in, watching as her breathing was so uneven she wasn’t sure the older woman was even breathing at all, and the muttered words that she could not understand properly. Rio didn’t know whether touching Agatha would make things worse, and opted to use her voice instead.

“Agatha? It’s me, Rio.” There was no response, no change in movement, no change in breathing. So, she tried again, desperation and worry evident in her voice. “Agatha, honey? It’s Rio. I’m here. Look at me, please?” she cooed softly, fingers itching to reach out, but clutched tightly to herself.

The words seemed to click in Agatha’s head, wheels turning at the disruption of her solitude. The pressure of her hands against her eye sockets lessened, hands shaking visibly as she tried to move them away from her face. Rio hoped that it wouldn’t backfire, and reached forward to gently grip Agatha’s wrists, helping her settle her hands onto her knees, caressing the wrists with care.

The blue eyes that Rio loved staring at so much were surrounded by red, blood vessels more visible than usual due to the crying she had clearly been doing. The tears were still flowing, but she wasn’t sobbing; it was a silent cry, which was even more heartbreaking. Her eyes were puffy, and she looked incredibly broken. The sight of her tugged at Rio’s heartstrings, wishing she could absorb Agatha’s pain and make it her own.

“It’s okay, you’re okay. I’m here.” Rio wasn’t sure what had caused her to spiral, but one thing she knew for sure: she was going to help her out of it.

There was no verbal response from Agatha again, but there was a slight movement in her eyebrows as her eyes blinked slowly, a silent acknowledgement that she registered Rio’s words and presence, albeit barely.

“Can I—“ Rio started hesitantly, biting the inside of her cheek. “Is it okay if I touch you?”

The nod that Agatha gave her was barely there, but it was all she could manage, and it was all Rio needed to jump into action. She shifted her body so that she could lean her back against the couch, then gently wrapped her arms around Agatha to pull her into her, letting her sit between her legs. Agatha’s body complied easily, allowing the florist to embrace her from behind, the older woman shifting so that her knees were no longer pulled up to her chest, but her legs stretched over the carpet instead. Her foot rested against the dark green flower pot housing the gifted orchid, not knocking it over, but merely pressed against it.

“It’s okay, you’re okay,” Rio repeated. “I’ve got you. Just breathe.” Rio’s breaths were slow, deep and full of purpose, her chest pressed against Agatha’s back in hopes that she would be able to feel and therefore follow her breathing pattern. She moved one hand from across Agatha’s waist to the middle of the woman’s chest, hand splayed over the material of her soft sweater. With each inhale, the pressure of her palm against Agatha’s chest lessened without breaking contact, allowing her chest to expand freely. Then, with every exhale, Rio pressed down gently, encouraging Agatha to follow. The florist’s free hand was rested in her own lap, unmoving, until she felt Agatha’s hand slip into hers. Rio squeezed her hand gently, a silent encouragement and reassurance that she was there for her, silently praying that Agatha missed the way Rio’s heart had skipped a beat at the gesture.

They breathed together for a few minutes, Rio whispering little words of reassurance into Agatha’s ear to soothe her, until eventually, Agatha lifted her hand to brush at the tears streaking her face. Rio took that as her cue to move her hand down from Agatha’s chest, settling on her thigh instead, gently brushing her thumb back and forth. The younger woman didn’t say anything, unsure whether Agatha had found her voice again.

Suddenly, Agatha’s body shifted forward, stretching out. Rio thought that was her sign to move away from the woman and give her space, so she bent one knee to push herself up on her foot, but stopped when she felt Agatha’s grip on her hand tighten. Confused, she peered over Agatha’s shoulder. In between her legs sat the purple orchid in its pot, no longer neglected, and to Rio’s surprise, Agatha was pinching one of the green leaves between her thumb and index finger, running it along the plant’s veins gently, careful not to pull too hard.

Perhaps this was her attempt at grounding herself? That would explain why it had been on the floor. Rio tried to ignore what that information did to the big, red, pounding muscle in her chest. She leaned forward, resting her head on Agatha’s shoulder, and trailed her own fingers along the little ridges on the dark green pot. She pulled the pot slightly closer. “You’re doing so well, Agatha. Just keep breathing. Let yourself see, feel and smell your surroundings. You’re okay.”

The older woman nodded in response and sighed deeply, shoulders relaxing when she exhaled. “Thank you,” she croaked, barely managing to push the words out.

Rio didn’t have anything to say to that, and simply squeezed her hand again. She leaned back against the couch, giving Agatha some space to settle into reality again, without leaving her side.

“Sorry you had to deal with all that,” Agatha’s voice came out laced with insecurity and self-deprecation, feeling embarrassed once the realisation of their situation kicked in.

“Don’t apologise, please.” Rio couldn’t believe the older woman was apologising for a panic attack, as if she had any choice over those. She was quick to shut it down. Her voice was gentle when she carefully spoke her next words, not wanting to unsettle her again. “Would you like to tell me what happened?”

“I— Um,” Agatha fidgeted with the hands in her lap, considering whether to open up or not. She didn’t know how to explain that even past forty years old, her mother still held this type of power over her, no matter how hard she had tried to shut her out.

“You don’t have to,” Rio quickly added, not wanting to pressure her.

Agatha took a deep breath, steadying herself before she spoke. “Evanora called. She wasn’t happy about Nicky not spending time with Ralph.” Rio didn’t need to see Agatha’s face to know she was rolling her eyes.

“She pulled the homophobe card, as usual,” she chuckled bitterly. “Which I’m used to, so that doesn’t really get to me anymore, but…” She hesitated, taking another steadying breath.

“… but?” Rio gently encouraged her to continue.

Agatha lifted her head, staring off into the distance, out the window. “She brought up Nicky’s illness, and how it was my fault. That if I wasn’t.. me… it wouldn’t have happened. And it wouldn’t happen again. And I know it doesn’t really work that way, but fuck, I can’t help but feel useless, like it is somehow all my fault.”

Rio had been sitting quietly, listening to Agatha, trying to follow along. Agatha could hear the wheels turning in the florist’s head, and realised she was missing some crucial information to understand the gravity of Evanora’s words. Agatha considered turning around, looking Rio in the face as she told her story, but she couldn’t find the strength to do so. She spoke into the distance again, hoping that the words were clear enough to reach Rio’s ears.

“Nicky… He had a really bad case of Bronchitis when he was three, the inflammation spreading in his chest. It just barely bordered on Pneumonia, and I was worried sick. Like, literally, sick. I threw up from anxiety so often.” Rio didn’t say anything to interrupt, but showed that she was listening by caressing Agatha’s back softly, rubbing circles. “He was constantly coughing and he even had bruised ribs from the severity of it. I felt helpless, I couldn’t do anything to fix it no matter how badly I wanted to… I felt like I failed my job as a mother.”

Rio’s heart felt heavy at the unravelled information, she couldn’t imagine what that had been like for Agatha. She wished she could have been there for her.

“You did the best you could do; you left him in the hands of experts. You showed up for him. That’s all you had to do, Agatha. And he’s okay now, right? That’s what matters.”

“I guess… But I can’t help but wonder if it’s my fault somehow. What if I really am no good?” The words come out choked, as if the more rational part of Agatha had wanted to bite them back, but her anxiety had won the battle and pushed them out regardless.

“Oh, shit.” Agatha gulped loudly and stood abruptly, legs wobbling as she moved, fully ignoring her dizziness. Rio scrambled to her feet as fast as she could, but Agatha had already bolted to the hallway.

“Agatha?”

Rio rushed after her, concern coursing through her veins from head to toe. She was just in time to see Agatha yank open the door to the toilet before dropping to her knees in front of it, spilling the contents of her stomach into the bowl with a loud hurl. As quick as she could, Rio stood behind her, gathering Agatha’s hair into her fist to keep it out of the way, rubbing soothing circles on her back with her other hand. Agatha waited a moment to see if there was anything else travelling up her throat, taking deep breaths as she dug her nails into the palms of her hands. She felt like a fucking mess.

Slowly rising up again, Agatha wiped her mouth with the back of her hand before washing them, Rio stepping backward to allow her to move more freely, still eyeing her cautiously. “Are you... okay?”

“Yeah. Just… Anxiety.” All Rio could do was nod, ready to catch Agatha in case she dropped down again. Luckily, she didn’t. Instead, she seemed to have regained her posture quite a bit, surprisingly. Rio went to the kitchen to retrieve a glass of water, handing it to Agatha who had trailed behind her. There was no protest from her side, taking a large sip from the glass to wash down the disgusting feeling in her throat. Agatha’s eyes lifted to Rio’s face, and the brown eyes were looking at her as if she was fragile, breakable, or already broken. Agatha hated the feeling, yet simultaneously couldn’t believe that Rio could feel that much goddamn concern for her, as not many ever had.

“Can you do something for me?” Rio asked after a moment of silence. Agatha raised an eyebrow in question, encouraging her to voice her request. Rio held out her hand for Agatha to take. “Follow me.”

Although Agatha was confused, she allowed Rio to guide her, which was apparently up the stairs. Her muscles felt sore from the exertion, but she managed. Rio pulled her to the left, stopping in front of the bathroom door. She turned to face the older woman, opening the door with an exaggerated bow of the head. “You look like you could use a nice, relaxing shower. Please enter, m’lady.”

“Well, well, well. And they say chivalry is dead.”

The air felt less tense, less charged with the weight of heavy, depressing emotions. For just a second, Agatha allowed a small smile to tug at the corner of her lips. Rio followed Agatha inside the bathroom, but only to turn on the knobs of the shower, adjusting the temperature and letting the water run so it could warm up already. The shower was tucked around the corner, shielded by a high brick wall-type divider for privacy, with only a small opening to step into it. Rio grabbed a towel and washcloth from the cabinet where she had previously seen Agatha take one out for her, and set it on the rack closest to the shower. Rio turned to the door, ready to walk out, when she saw contemplation on Agatha’s face.

“Do you need anything else while you’re in here?”

There was a pause, accompanied by hesitation. “Um, if you could, um… Grab me some clothes?”

“Yeah, absolutely. Is that all?”

“Well, um,” Agatha started hesitantly, as if she didn’t want to speak the words out loud, for the sake of not seeming weak. Rio lowered her hand from the door handle, waiting patiently while Agatha found the courage to speak up. “Would you mind coming back in once I’m in the shower? Just like… sitting in the room, I guess. I don’t want to be alone.”

“Of course. Just let me know once I’m allowed to go in.” Rio didn’t say it out loud, but she felt proud of Agatha for voicing her needs, she could see how hard it had been on her. She returned to the bathroom with a small stack of comfy-looking clothes, including new underwear and socks. She had made a point to stay focused on the task when rummaging through the underwear drawer, ignoring the amount of lace and sheer material she came across before finding a cotton pair that would probably be most comfortable.

Rio briefly knocked on the door, not wanting to walk in while Agatha was getting undressed, given the circumstances. “Come in!”

Once inside, Rio settled on the bathroom tiles, back resting against the wall while she listened to the sounds of the water dropping. She took out her phone, finally having the time to update Wanda, and sent her a quick message, then opened up her TikTok app, scrolling mindlessly while Agatha enjoyed her shower. At some point, she stumbled across one of her favourite songs, humming along to it quietly, much to Agatha’s delight. This certainly was helping her relax.

Agatha turned the shower off and reached for the towel, keeping her body tucked away around the corner as she did so. She dried herself off before wrapping the towel around her body, long hair still dripping wet. Rio was so deep into her phone that she didn’t hear the shower stop, but was quickly pulled from her little digital world when she caught movement in the corner of her eye. Her eyes landed on Agatha, wrapped in a towel that was barely long enough to cover all the important parts, skin still covered in little water droplets. She felt bad for looking, even just for a mere second, and quickly averted her gaze. As her eyes dropped down, she caught sight of something unexpected; was that a tattoo on her ankle? She quickly hurried to her feet, flustered when she spoke. “Oh, um, sorry. I didn’t know— I can leave.”

“Sorry, should’ve announced I was stepping out,” Agatha said sheepishly, not sure why she hadn’t announced it. She reached for the pile of clothes that was waiting for her, but didn’t move before Rio had the chance to reach for the doorknob. “I’ll wait.”

Rio exited through the door as soon as she could, mostly to hide the blush on her cheeks. She breathed deeply as she walked back to Agatha’s room, waiting until she was finished. When the older woman made her way to her bedroom, fully clothed this time, she saw Rio sitting cross-legged on her bed, scrolling on her phone. The florist noticed her right away and dropped her phone into her lap with a small smile, shuffled backward just a tiny bit to accommodate Agatha in front of her, patting the empty space on the bed.

Unsure, yet willing to do what she was told, Agatha sat down on her mattress, facing Rio. Rio gently shook her head and urged her to turn around, which again, she listened to. “Be right back.”

And surely, Rio returned rather quickly, holding a hair dryer and some hair products in her hands. She plugged the device into an outlet and moved to sit behind Agatha again. The older woman’s wavy hair had been put up in a bun, not having the energy to deal with it properly. Rio was happy to take care of it, though. She loosened the hair tie with her hands and let the hair drop down, cascading down the entire length of Agatha’s back. Rio picked up the hairbrush and started at the ends, gently combing through the hair before slowly making her way up, careful not to pull on any knots. At the first brush of the hair, Agatha lets out the softest involuntary moan, causing both of their cheeks to flush, all the way to the tips of their ears. Rio pretended not to notice, and simply continued her movements. When she reached about mid-way through the lengths of Agatha’s hair and brushed down in one, longer stroke, a slightly louder whimper was choked back. “Oh shit, sorry. Am I hurting you?”

“N-No, not at all. Sorry.”

Oh. Okay. Good to know. That’s perfectly fine, I’m fine. Great, even, Rio told herself.

Somehow they made it through the rest of her long hair without any more sounds and noises, which they were both relieved yet disappointed about. Shaking the thoughts from her head, Rio pulled herself back to her task at hand. She squeezed some of the curl cream into her hands, raking it gently through the dark hair, spreading it evenly. Next, she applied a little bit of gel, careful not to use too much to avoid weighing down her waves. Lastly, she applied a light mousse from root to end, before scrunching the waves into place. Rio used her fingers to manually coil a few strands that bothered her, before attaching the diffuser head to the dryer.

To be honest, Rio had never done any of this before, but she had somehow ended up on Curly Girl TikTok a few weeks ago, and she had been enthralled by the intricate routines they seemed to have. Based merely on her memory, she tried to mimic the steps. It was turning out rather good, if she could say so herself. Agatha’s thick, long hair took a long time to dry, but Rio’s patience never wavered. She handled the hair with so much care, nurturing it until it was fully dry. She was so wrapped up in what she was doing that she didn’t hear Agatha’s soft sniffles, only audible once the blowdryer was turned off. Worried, she leaned forward, trying to see what was going on. “Are you okay?”

Agatha sniffled with a small chuckle, feeling embarrassed at her display of emotion, once again. “Yeah. I just… Nobody’s ever done this for me before. It’s a bit overwhelming.” It was true, the act was so innocent yet intimate to her, that it felt strangely disarming. Agatha had never shared a moment like this with anyone before, and wasn’t sure how to act, especially since she already had an overwhelming amount of feelings for the woman sitting behind her.

“Sorry, I didn’t mean for it to be. I just want to show you that I’m here for you.”

“Thank you,” was the first thing Agatha replied to that. Immediately after, she realised that Rio had apologised for something that was not at all her fault. Agatha didn’t want her to think that she couldn’t repeat her actions in the future if the opportunity ever presented itself. If anything, Agatha was secretly wishing she’d get to relive this feeling, no matter how strange and unfamiliar it had been. She was awfully aware as to why she wasn’t used to it. “You don’t need to apologise. It’s just unusual for me, receiving such care. I always wondered how anyone could care about me when my own mother, the person who gave birth to me, couldn’t. She resents me simply for being alive.”

Rio’s brown eyes softened, the entire day leading to an increased understanding of what Agatha’s relationship with her mother had been like. If it already tore Rio apart on the inside, she couldn’t imagine how the older woman must’ve felt.

“She has convinced me that everything in my life has a purpose. Most of it feels like a form of torture. Even when people are good to me, they always have to leave eventually. That is my punishment.” Rio started playing with the ends of Agatha’s freshly dried hair, fingertips grazing over her back. As much as Agatha hadn’t wanted to move, she felt like she had to look at Rio before speaking her next words. The door to her soul had cracked open ever so slightly, and she needed Rio to see it before it was hammered shut again. “That’s why I always have my fist clenched around the handle of an invisible suitcase, ready to leave. At least then it’s on my own terms.”

The florist nodded in understanding, an empathetic smile forming. She considered her next words carefully, taking Agatha’s hand into her own. “I’m sorry she’s made you believe those horrible things, Agatha. But you must know, not everyone is going to do that. It’s okay to let people in, even if it’s a little. She’s trying to keep you from living the life you deserve, and you’re letting her win by keeping your distance from everyone. ”

“I’ve always been an arm’s length, small-talk type of woman. The blazing hellfire of fuck-ups tends to get in the way of forming true connections, anyway,” Agatha chuckled bitterly, in that self-deprecating manner that Rio was starting to recognise for what it was; Agatha’s defence mechanism. 

Unwavering, Rio caressed the hand in her palm with her thumb. “I’d still encourage you to try, though. There’s plenty of people that have stuck around, no matter how insignificant it might seem to you…” She spoke gently, eyebrows furrowed momentarily at the idea that her actions might’ve been going unnoticed. “You just need to open your eyes to see it.”

“Yeah, maybe you’re right.” Agatha stared at Rio’s moving thumb, tracking the motions with her eyes as if it was the most interesting thing she’d ever seen. Her head finally started clearing, and a new question plagued her mind. “How’d you know where to find me?”

Rio bit the inside of her cheek, not sure if she should reveal the truth. She didn’t know how Agatha would respond to her best friend’s interference. The words came out tentative, as if testing the waters. “Wanda called me. She, uh, was worried that you hadn’t replied.”

“Oh shit, I completely forgot about Nicky!” Agatha moved to jump up from the bed, scolding herself silently for her carelessness. How could she neglect him like that?

Before her thoughts could spiral any further, falling back into her negative thoughts of not being good enough, Rio pulled her back by her arm. Her words came out rushed, almost stumbling over her own tongue just to reassure Agatha as quickly as possible. “Don’t worry. I updated her, and Nicky’s still playing with the twins. It’s okay. He’s taken care of. She’ll drop him off later.”

Now it finally felt like the raging storm inside her body was settling down, and Agatha forced the tension out of her shoulders. She thanked Rio, who was quick to dismiss it, and suggested they should have a bite to eat.

Once downstairs, Rio asked Agatha what her favourite comfort meal was. When Agatha shyly revealed that it was a Greek salad, Rio ridiculed her for having such a healthy comfort meal, before asking what the ingredients were.

“You don’t know what’s in a Greek salad?” Agatha pulled a hand to her chest in mock disbelief, eyes widening and eyebrows raising, “Honey!”

Per Agatha’s instructions, Rio pulled out the feta cheese, tomatoes, olives and some mint, before seasoning it with olive oil and pepper. The younger woman had to keep her opinions to herself, unable to understand how one’s favourite meal could not include three different kinds of carbs. Perhaps that was how Agatha kept her figure like, well, that. Rio told herself she’d give it a try.

While Agatha was slowly turning back to her old self, though never fully, Rio figured it was a good idea to update Wanda once again. She texted her to ask when she’d bring Nicky over when she saw Agatha walk back to the flower pot that had been abandoned on the floor. The older woman placed it back in its designated spot and turned around, only to find Rio eyeing her curiously. Agatha felt her cheeks heat up, and even though Rio couldn’t know for sure if Agatha had sought out the gift in search of comfort during her panic attack, her face revealed everything Rio needed to know. She simply smiled at Agatha, before averting her gaze so that the older woman wouldn’t feel too exposed.

Moments later, Wanda arrived at the front door, Nicky happily beaming next to her. At least he hadn’t noticed anything. Good.

Rio had opened the door to let them in, Agatha still preoccupied in the house. There was a silent conversation passing between Wanda and Rio, eyes communicating more than they had wanted to say out loud in front of Nicky. When Nicky pushed past Rio to greet his mother, Wanda finally used her voice. “Is she okay?”

Nodding once, Rio forced a small smile onto her lips. “Yeah. I mean, she will be. She’s had better days.”

“Thank you, Rio.”

“No, thank you for calling me.” Rio paused for a moment, opening the door just a little further. “Would you… like to come in?”

“Oh, I don’t know, I don’t want to crowd her too much.” Wanda hesitated, her concern for her best friend weighing against the understanding that Agatha might prefer to be left alone.

Rio noticed Wanda’s inner battle and realised she might need a small push of encouragement. She hoped Agatha wouldn’t get upset with her, but she’d deal with the aftermath if needed. “I think she’d appreciate a friendly face right now. Even if it’s just for a second. I don’t think she’ll admit it, but I can bet she could use a hug.”

The redhead didn’t expect that response from Rio, and quickly realised that the florist truly did have her friend’s best interest at heart. She knew right then and there that she had called the right person. With an appreciative smile, she entered the house and headed straight to her goal. Agatha seemed surprised to watch her walk in, but didn’t say it aloud. When Wanda pulled her in for a tight hug, Agatha leaned her chin on the redhead’s shoulder, making questioning eye contact with Rio, who simply shrugged innocently. Nevertheless, she returned the embrace, allowing herself to relax in her best friend’s arms.

Just as quickly as she had entered, Wanda had turned back to the door, her worries finally washed away upon seeing her friend up close. Rio and Wanda shared a quick hug, too, and Rio promised to let Wanda know if anything else occurred.

With Nicky back at home, Agatha easily slipped on the mask of being a strong mother, forcing away all of the negative emotions she had experienced earlier that day. Her full focus was on her son, who was excitedly telling her about his day. She knew she was projecting her fatigue on him when she told him it was time to go to bed, but he listened regardless. Rio had offered to take over for her, but Agatha would never turn down the opportunity to tuck her little boy in, no matter how drained she was.

However, Nicky was a traitor, asking Rio to read him a bedtime story and shooing his mother away. Thus, Agatha placed a loving kiss on his forehead before bidding him goodnight, and made her way to her own bedroom. She changed out of her lounge set and into her pyjamas before sitting down at the edge of the bed. She could hear the laughter from Nicky’s room as he shared his favourite adventures of the day with the florist, the sound soothing her aching insides little by little. She dropped her back onto the mattress, legs and feet still dangling off the edge, and crossed her hands over her stomach, staring at the ceiling. Agatha didn’t know what she would’ve done without Rio, well aware that she would not have recovered as easily without her presence.

Agatha felt like it had been a figment of her imagination; the way Rio’s brown eyes radiated warmth and care couldn’t have been real. It couldn’t have been for her. Nobody treated her like that. Ever.

With a small but genuine smile, the florist looked at the couple across from her. “Thanks, girls. I’ll keep you updated.”

“You better,” Alice started, before her face shifted into a mischievous smirk. “Now, back to more pressing matters: you finally broke your dry spell, and we need more details!”

Jen laughed at her girlfriend’s attempt to lighten the mood, but quickly jumped in, too. “Yeah, you can’t possibly think ‘really good’ is a good enough answer for us. What happened to the Rio that would use us as props to show each and every position of the night?”

The florist felt her cheeks flare up, the skin turning a bright shade of crimson. However, she’d rather feel the embarrassment right now than stay stuck in her misplaced anxiety, and gulped down the remainder of her wine in one go before turning back to her friends.

—————

That same Sunday, Agatha was still busy, hands kneading the navy blue clay before she rolled it into several smaller balls, flattened them and pressed one of the dinosaur-shaped cutters into it. Carefully, she removed the remaining clay from the sides, leaving her with the shape of a species she forgot the name of. Somehow, she managed to peel it off the table without it breaking, and handed it off to Nicky so he could decorate it further.

She sat cross-legged on the dining chair, with her sleeves pushed up, surrounded by a small rainbow of Play-Doh containers, while Nicky sat next to her, tongue sticking out in concentration. He was fully absorbed in his newest project: an ambitious herd of brightly coloured dinosaurs.

“Okay, this one’s the Stegosaurus.” Nicky proudly recalled. “Did you know that they were really, really big, but their brain was small like a ping pong ball!”

Agatha chuckled and shook her head. “I did not know that, honey.”

She was fairly certain that she knew close to nothing about dinosaurs, only a few weeks ago. Now, she knew too much. Way too much.

“And this one,” Nicky continued excitedly, though Agatha braced herself when she saw which one he pointed out, “is the Pterodactyl. He flies!” He dramatically swooped his hands through the air, making loud screeching noises. “KKEEEAAHHH!”

His mother laughed, covering her ears. “Oh no. Not the Pterodactyl again. My poor ears, Nicky. You’ve been practising those sounds all day.”

“That’s because they’re my new favourite,” Nicky said, giggling. Unfortunately for Agatha, ever since they had watched a documentary that included the species, he had been interested in learning everything about them, including the sounds they made.

They continued building their tiny prehistoric world as best as they could, while the house was filled with the lively beat of Agatha’s playlist, Chappell Roan’s voice echoing between the four walls of the room. Nicky started humming along to Pink Pony Club, putting a gentle smile on his mother’s face while she observed him.

For most of the day, Agatha had kept her phone out of reach, wanting to give Nicky her full attention. She took pride in being the type of parent who could truly be present with their children, rather than the ones who would visit her cafe, giving their children too much screen time and too little attention. It was one of those rare weekends for them where everything felt calm. Just the two of them, no distractions.

Except for the occasional slipping of her mind, drifting to a certain beautifully brown-eyed florist. But Agatha had given up on letting that distraction go completely, as it had proven to be futile.

As Nicky got focused on perfecting his tiny Stegosaurus, as best as a six-year-old could, Agatha finally picked up her phone. The screen lit up, showing a few missed texts from Rio earlier that morning — light chatter, nothing urgent. She smiled softly to herself, feeling the familiar warmth that always washed over her when she saw the younger woman’s name pop up.

Seeing Rio’s request for a picture of their creations, Agatha couldn’t help but comply. Without spending too much time thinking about why she wished that Rio could have been there with them, she snapped a photo of their growing dinosaur collection and typed out a message.

His new favourite thing is to run around making Pterodactyl sounds. I wish I could go back to not knowing what those sounded like. Please send help.

She hit send and stared at the screen for a moment longer than necessary, still smiling, albeit unknowingly.

“Who are you texting?” Nicky’s voice piped up from beside her.

Agatha glanced at him, still a little dreamier than her usual look. She pushed a strand of hair out of her face, feigning nonchalance. “Oh, just Rio.”

“Cool,” he said, his eyes focusing on the clay that he was modelling in his small hands. Agatha thought that was all, that the conversation ended there. But then, he spoke up again, his eyes shining with curiosity. “Mama?”

Agatha hummed in response and made eye contact with him to reassure him that he had her attention, waiting for him to ask his question.

“Did you tell Rio that you like her?”

His mother’s breath caught for a moment, not expecting the question to be so blunt. She chuckled nervously, remembering the conversation she had had with Nicky a while ago, when he asked if he had wanted Rio to be her girlfriend. “Sort of.”

Which technically wasn’t a lie. They had spoken about their interest in each other, and decided to explore. But Agatha hadn’t straight-up said she liked Rio. As the realisation dawned upon her, another one quickly followed—Agatha was quite convinced that the word ‘like’ was no longer strong enough to describe her feelings for Rio.

Nicky’s brows formed an adorable frown, and he lowered his hands, setting down the dinosaur figure carefully. “What do you mean ‘sort of’?”

Agatha sighed, looking for the right words to explain this to her son without having to do a deep dive into her issues with trust and communication, amongst many other things. “I didn’t use those exact words. But we talked.” There was a small pause from her side, hoping to see Nicky nod in understanding. When the look on his face remained a state of confusion, she continued. “Sometimes, when there are big feelings involved, it’s a bit more complicated to talk about them.” Then, followed in an almost-whisper, “Scary, even.”

This, the child seemed to understand more easily, giving a single nod. Agatha busied her hands with the  Play-Doh, avoiding his scrutinising eyes.

If she thought she was off the hook, she was definitely mistaken.

“Not fair, Mama.” Why did it feel like she was being scolded by her own son? Was it not supposed to be the other way around?

“What do you mean?” she asked, keeping her voice neutral, unsure where he was going with this.

“You always say that I have to tell you when I have big feelings. That it’s not good to keep them in, and I should share it with you. Because people…” he trailed off, his head slightly tilting to the side, challenging his mother.

Well, Agatha be damned. She started questioning why she was so adamant on being the type of mother who made communication such a vital part of her child’s upbringing, insisting that everything should be talked about openly. She knew it was because she never had that with her own parents. Now, though, Agatha decided she didn’t mind if her child was a little less emotionally intelligent. Gentle parenting was biting her in the ass.

“… aren’t mind readers.” She tried her best not to roll her eyes as she completed his sentence, knowing very well that they had had this argument various times in the past, just with the roles reversed.

Nodding proudly, as if he were the teacher and his mother the student, he concluded his lesson. “So, you should share your big feelings with Rio. Even if it is scary,” he continued, his words carrying more wisdom than a child should have.

Finally, she let go of the clay that had doubled as a stress ball in the past minute, and moved closer to her son. She reached out her hand, running it through his curls to brush them out of his face. After placing a soft kiss to his forehead, she lingered there, whispering against his skin. “You’re right, Nicky. You’re absolutely right.”

When she pulled back, her hands remained, cupping his face. Agatha swallowed as she caressed his cheeks with her thumbs, taking a moment to really look at him, at his surprisingly insightful face. Every day, she was grateful for him, but it was moments like these where she realised how lucky she was with such a smart, considerate child. Staring into his brown eyes always made her feel at ease. She could have the worst day of her life, and a glance at Nicky could instantly lift some weight off of her.

“Sometimes grown-ups forget to follow their own advice. But luckily, I have you.”

Nicky visibly swelled with pride, delighted that he had helped his mother in a way, no matter how big or small. Agatha smiled, both fond and exasperated at the simplicity of it all.

They both returned to the completion of their dinosaur collection, each working on a different species. When Agatha’s phone buzzed again, she picked it up right away, a betraying smile on her face yet again.

Rio: consider me officially terrified of tiny pterodactyl boy

Rio: the dinos are adorable. which one’s yours?

She quickly circled the two she had made for the collection, and re-sent the picture. Rio’s reply was instant.

Rio: should have known

Agatha wondered what she meant by that, quickly typing a response before removing it, unsure how to ask for an explanation. As if on cue, the younger woman added to her initial message, and Agatha could practically hear the teasing tone in her voice through the screen.

Rio: those are the least historically accurate, and nicky would never let me down like that

The older woman scoffed and rolled her eyes, though the smile never left her face, the corners of her mouth seemingly permanently curled up any time she spoke to Rio. Instead of coming up with a defence, she simply responded with the middle finger emoji.

“Do you hold hands?” He asked innocently. “With Rio?”

Before Agatha could respond, she noticed the exact moment he came up with another question in his mind, a mischievous smile making its way to his lips. She raised an eyebrow in warning, hoping he would leave the topic be.

However, at his following question, she nearly squished the whatever-something-saurus she was working on.

“Did you kiss?”

Agatha felt a rush of heat crawl up her neck, the images of their night in New York flashing in her mind in startling clarity.

“Oooh, you did!” Nicky's voice sounded in a singsong, then he giggled mercilessly, satisfied with the response that came from his mother’s lack of actual response.

“Nicky!” Agatha scolded playfully, unable to contain the soft laughter that escaped her lips. Desperate to hide her flustered state, she stood up and walked over to the kitchen sink, suddenly very eager to place their cups into the dishwasher. Her smile stayed plastered on her face, while her ears were filled with the sound of Nicky’s unstoppable giggles.

It seemed like she had her work cut out for her, both withstanding Nicky’s newfound teasing, and with telling Rio about her feelings. Though she hadn’t told Nicky when she would talk to the florist, so she was safe for now.

No need to rush into things, she thought to herself. One day, maybe somewhat soon.

Chapter End Notes
