or, the one in which their friends make a return because i remembered they exist.

Chapter Notes

to the person who said to be cringe is to be free: i finally reread my story from start to finish. We are sooooo back babes.

chapter title inspired by stuck around from abby powledge bc "baby keep doing what you're doing cause it feels right, i'm just new to this"

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Rio grunted as she lifted a heavy bag with plant soil, pouring some into a large empty pot. It had been yet another early morning for her, but lately those felt a lot lighter than they’d had before.

Granted, it was hard to evenly distribute her time between her job, friends, alone-time and relationship. Especially because when it came to her relationship with Agatha, there was still a difference between quality time with the two of them, or quality time including Nicky. The florist greatly appreciated both, but all in all, it left her with fewer hours of sleep than she was used to. Still, she welcomed every new day with open arms.

She pocketed her phone after responding to Agatha’s latest text, returning to the task at hand. Once the soil was packed into the pot, she carefully removed the plant she wanted to repot, ensuring that the roots were still intact. When she walked to the backroom to refill her watering can, she heard the bell above the door chime.

“Helloooooo,” she heard a very familiar voice call out.

Struggling to hold the full can with one hand, she quickly shut off the tap, before walking back into the shop with a big smile. “Coming!”

As expected, she was met with the faces of her best friends. They’d texted their group chat, mentioning that they missed spending time together. Rio had felt guilty, but there were no accusations, simply determination to see each other again.

“Big smile for someone who barely remembers who we are,” Jen teased as Rio approached her for a hug.

Rio rolled her eyes for a second, before giving Alice a teasing wink. “What? I greet all my customers like this.”

To be fair, it wasn’t like the women usually saw each other constantly, but they would stay in touch through their very active group chat. The past few days, Rio had been a bit less responsive, getting caught up in the whirlwind of her packed days.

Since effort should come from both sides, and they were all adults who understand that one’s availability fluctuated over time, Jen and Alice figured they could just swing by Rio’s work to see her. Nothing would get between their friendship, especially after all this time.

The women noticed the big monstera leaf mounted to the wall, smiling softly at each other when they remembered the florist’s story about it. Rio had come a long way since then.

Rio continued watering repotting the plants she was working on, sitting on the floor while making small talk with her friends. It was nice to have them at her store again. She enjoyed watching Alice and Jen argue about which flowers to get, as their tastes tended to differ quite a bit. Alice always relented, Jen always got her way. And then Jen would make Rio add in a few of the flowers Alice had wanted, because she couldn’t help herself.

Their relationship had always been one that Rio admired. The way they fit together, how they understood each other without needing any words. Their relationship was something she always held in the back of her mind as an example when she was looking for a partner of her own.

Speaking of which, Rio noticed her phone buzzing once. She had put it on the floor beside her while she worked, and saw Agatha’s message light up her screen. She couldn’t help the smile that made its way onto her face.

Jen stopped mid-sentence. “Oh, that’s disgusting.”

Rio looked up, slightly startled. “What?”

“That face,” her best friend teased, pointing at her, then at her phone. Rio caught on and straightened her face, tugging the corners of her mouth down, despite the smile still fighting its way up. She asked, “What face?”

Alice sat down across from her, packing some soil into a different pot. She didn’t mind getting her hands dirty if it meant helping her friend. Quality time was quality time, no matter how they were spending their time. “The Agatha face.”

“I just checked my phone! I wasn’t making a face,” Rio defended lamely, hiding the faint blush on her cheeks. She missed her friends teasing her. Things finally felt good for once. Comfortable.

“Yeah you did,” Jen chuckled. “Seeing you lovesick is going to make me sick.”

Rio rolled her eyes again, but then her phone buzzed, and she couldn’t help looking at it from the corner of her eye. She bit the inside of her cheeks to stop herself from smiling. Perhaps she was a lovesick fool after all.

“It’s nice to see you this happy, Rio,” Alice commented, her eyes warm and gentle.

The florist looked back at her, smiling this time. She had spent so long trying not to expect too much from Agatha that it was difficult to believe she could finally have all of this without losing it. “I am happy. It’s a bit scary, but it feels good.”

Jen, finally done with her relentless teasing, placed a hand on her shoulder. Unexpectedly gently, she told Rio that being cautious was fine. As long as she didn’t let her fear make decisions for her. She’d been doing well so far, all she had to do was keep it up.

When Rio’s eyes turned to her phone once again, without a notification to trigger it, Alice got up and dusted off her pants. She offered a hand to Rio, urging her to get up as well. “Why don’t we go see your wife in the flesh? C’mon babe, let’s go.”

An offer Rio simply couldn’t refuse, especially because it was not a super packed day. She also knew there was no use in correcting Alice, telling her that Agatha was actually not (yet?) her wife. 

Hold on, Rio told herself mentally. Wife? They had only just become girlfriends, and here Rio was, thinking about whether Agatha could one day be her wife. Rio hadn't even allowed herself to consider if she still wanted to get married in the first place. One thing at a time. 

She wrapped the flowers her friends had picked, and grabbed another small bouquet before heading out the door together.

With spring around the corner, the weather had turned a lot softer, the sun hitting their faces as soon as they stepped outside. As much as Rio would have enjoyed sitting in the sun, she was even more looking forward to seeing her girlfriend, and all but sprinted to the cafe. Very nonchalantly, of course.

Alice and Jen walked in first, Rio trailing closely after them. The scent of freshly baked cake still hung in the air, a warm welcome to anyone entering the cafe. Lilia was sitting at a table by the counter with her glasses perched on her nose, reading a magazine. It was quiet, still. Only a handful of customers were inside, merely two tables taken. There was barely any noise, the music from the sound system clearly audible.

Agatha rounded the corner with two cups of coffee in her hand, planning to take a seat next to Lilia. She set the cups down and turned around, pleasantly surprised to see Rio and her friends.

“Hi, good morning,” she said warmly, reciprocating the hug that Alice had initiated. Agatha was still getting used to allowing other people in her inner circle, but she found herself adjusting to the two women rather easily. Jen stepped in for a hug, too, before taking a seat next to Lilia.

Rio had been looking at Agatha the whole time, a part of her struggling to keep herself from running in for a hug first, the other part of her simply enjoying watching while her friends were talking to her girlfriend.

Quite aware of the fact that Jen, Alice, and Lilia were watching them closely, Rio felt rather shy as she stepped toward Agatha to greet her. She went in for a hug, which Agatha gladly accepted, whispering, “Hey, babe.”

Agatha pulled back from the hug, giving Rio a gentle smile before pecking her lips quickly. “Hi, honey.”

The older woman pretended to ignore that she could practically feel the three other women melting behind her back. So what? She kissed her girlfriend. Big deal.

“You look good,” she added, trailing her hand down Rio’s arm and lacing their fingers together. Rio was wearing loose barrel jeans with a fitted tank top, a small silver chain sitting right on her sternum. It wasn’t anything special, but Agatha always thought her girlfriend looked good. Now that the weather was getting nicer, she was getting to see more of Rio’s style. It worked in Agatha’s favour that that meant seeing more of her toned figure, with less oversized clothing.

Rio smiled appreciatively, and held out the small bouquet she had brought over. She had taken it upon herself to make sure that Agatha had fresh flowers sitting on the counter from now on. She’d bring a new bouquet for as long as Agatha would let her, she vowed. Agatha’s eyebrows rose in surprise, but she happily took the flowers, smelling them in the process. “Go on and join them, I’ll bring out your drinks.”

A few moments later, all of them were sitting together, enjoying their coffees and the banana toffee cake that Lilia had made earlier that morning.

It was nice, spending time together like this; their lives slowly merging together more and more.

The conversation flowed easily among them. Rio had told her friends about the small changes at the cafe, and Alice set her tote bag on the table, taking out the comics that she’d brought over to add to the bookshelves. Agatha felt touched, not expecting Rio’s friends to do something for her cafe. It was a way for them to welcome the older woman into their circle with open arms.

“This is nice,” the florist said after a while. “We haven’t spent time together like this in so long.”

Apart from that night at the bar, they hadn’t really merged their social circles often. They were too busy trying to figure out what was going on between them. Now that that was finally sorted, it felt right to get together again.

As if reading her mind, Agatha spoke up. “Why don’t you all come over for dinner sometime this week? I’ll ask Wanda, too.”

Agatha placed her hand on Rio’s knee, squeezing it affectionately. In return, her girlfriend smiled at her, nodding eagerly.

They settled on a date surprisingly easily, and Jen insisted on them bringing food as well, feeling bad if Agatha had to cook all of it by herself. Lilia promised to take care of the wine.

With all of it settled and arranged, the women went on with their day, leaving Rio to feel like she was on cloud nine. She hadn’t felt this lucky in a long time.

—————

Rio showed up to Agatha’s house with a few overly full grocery bags. It had taken quite some convincing, but she wanted to help her girlfriend as much as she could. Reluctantly, Agatha shared the grocery list, but not before telling the florist multiple times that she really could take care of it on her own. It wasn’t that Rio didn’t believe her; she simply wanted to make herself useful. They were a team now, and the younger woman took that very seriously.

They put away most of the groceries, only keeping what they needed for the main course. Nicky joined them in the kitchen, because he went wherever Rio went, which meant that he, too, wanted to help with the cooking.

Rio helped Nicky climb onto the counter, making him sit and watch while she prepared the food. Despite seeing this increasingly more often, it never failed to melt Agatha’s heart, seeing them together like this. Rio always handled Nicky with so much care, never telling him to stop rambling so much, never excluding him from whatever she was doing.

The three of them had just started setting the table, when the doorbell rang. Nicky ran to the door, revealing Alice and Jen. He hadn’t really seen them often, but he decided that he liked them because they were Rio’s friends. That, and the fact that Alice had red streaks in her hair; he told her that they were so cool.

Jen quickly set the large bowl with her signature pasta salad on the counter, before properly greeting Agatha and Rio. Alice trailed in behind her, still talking to a very energetic Nicky, who eagerly stuck to Alice’s side when he found out that she was carrying dessert. Smart kid, she’d give him that.

Soon after, Wanda and Lilia arrived, making the group complete. Chatter quickly filled the room, and Agatha put on some background music. Rio continued in the kitchen, which was still an unfamiliar sight to their guests. They could’t help but notice how effortlessly the women moved around each other.

Lilia helped herself to the fridge so she could make sure the wine stayed chilled, and watched as Agatha reached behind Rio for a plate without asking her to move, giving a gentle squeeze at the waist. When Jen asked for some serving spoons for her salad, Rio was the one to locate them and hand them over. Agatha was putting the last of Wanda’s ingredients onto the charcuterie board, arranging them carefully. She spotted movement in the corner of her eye, and without looking, playfully smacked Rio’s hand away before she could steal something off of the board. Of course, when Rio pouted, Agatha handed her the dried apricot anyway.

It felt a little surreal to Wanda, who was watching them fondly. She still remembered clearly when Agatha would pretend not to care whether Rio had called or come by the cafe. She remembered the way Agatha would go stiff whenever Rio’s name came up, and deflected whenever she tried to ask her best friend about her feelings for the florist. Now, Rio was standing in Agatha’s kitchen, stealing food and getting kissed on the cheek for her trouble.

The redhead smiled into her glass, feeling her heart swell with pride. Agatha deserved to be happy. She was glad to see her best friend finally go after it.

When it was time to take a seat, Nicky loudly announced that he wanted to sit next to Rio. Throughout the dinner, he included her in everything; telling her something that happened at school, asking her more questions about plants, mentioning that he wanted to show her something on his tablet once they finished.

The other adults at the table teased him for monopolising Rio’s attention.

“I’m sorry,” Rio said, completely unapologetic. “I’m very popular with the six-year-old demographic.”

“Don’t let it get to your head,” Lilia replied.

“I fear it’s too late for that,” Agatha teased, using her hand to smooth over Nicky’s hair while smiling at Rio in that small, unguarded way that she reserved just for her. They look like a family, Alice thought to herself. A very cute family.

After dinner, Nicky pushed his luck and tried to negotiate his bedtime. He argued that having guests should count as a special occasion, and special occasions meant staying up later. The kid made a strong point, but Agatha still said no. She knew that if he went up now, it’d still take quite some time until he actually settled down to sleep, so she figured it was best to get him moving already.

Rio attempted to help him make his case, which earned an immediate, betrayed look from Agatha. “You’re not helping,” Agatha said sternly, but not unkind. Never unkind to Rio.

“I’m merely advocating for the rights of the people.” Rio held up her hands in defence.

Agatha narrowed her eyes slightly. “The people have school tomorrow, honey.”

The florist pulled Nicky into her lap, wrapping an arm around him protectively. “The people are outnumbered and being oppressed!”

Laughter erupted at the table and Agatha compromised, telling Nicky that perhaps if he asked very nicely, Alice, Jen and Rio would read his bedtime story. With his big, hopeful eyes, he looked at the women, who didn’t have the heart to tell him no.

By the time they came back downstairs, the dinner plates were stacked, wine glasses filled, courtesy of Lilia, and the charcuterie board was taken out of the fridge. As the women settled back in, Agatha quietly excused herself, unable to let Nicky go to bed without tucking him in properly, personally.

They moved to the living room, settling on the couch together. Wanda sat on the sofa beside Agatha and Rio, while Jen and Alice claimed the seats on the opposite end. Lilia sat in the large recliner, pulling up her feet and tucking them beneath herself, twirling the wine in her glass.

Despite not really knowing what to expect when they all gathered, not knowing what to talk about, what their common interests were, it felt awfully easy and comfortable. There were no awkward silences between the women. Agatha assumed that it helped that there were a lot of chatty people in their group, always finding something to talk about.

The topic of conversation jumped from one to the next, flowing as easily as the glasses of wine. When Rio remembered that they had yet to take out Alice’s dessert, Agatha told her to stay seated, that she’d handle it.

Jen turned to Rio, sitting forward and leaning her elbows on her knees. “You know, Rio,” she started.

“Yes, Jen?” Rio replied, suspiciously eyeing her friend, suddenly feeling all eyes on her.

“I never took you for one to enjoy princess treatment,” she finished, tilting her head in the kitchen’s direction, where Agatha had disappeared. In all honesty, it had been nice to see her best friend be treated like this; for her to have someone that’d go out of their way to make nice gestures for Rio, instead of it only being the other way around. However, Jen would, of course, take the opportunity to tease her for it whenever she got the chance.

Rio shrugged as Agatha returned with dessert and plates. “What can I say. When a pretty lady tells me to stay still, I stay still.”

Agatha ran her fingers through Rio’s hair, scratching her scalp lightly. “And you do that so well, darling.”

Feeling herself burning up at that, Rio quickly spoke up to defend herself. “And by the way, I do things for Agatha, too!”

“Really?” Alice joined in on the teasing, teaming up with her girlfriend, “because so far I’ve only seen her service you, making your coffee before you even enter through the door.”

“Tell me, Agatha, does she even know how you like your coffee?” Jen smirked into her wine glass.

Agatha thought it over for a second, shrugging noncommittally. Rio, however, grew a smirk of her own, poking her tongue into the inside of her cheek, tilting her head slightly. “Yeah, I do. Like her head; first thing in the morning.”

Jen’s mouth fell open in exasperation, slapping her hand up to cover it, and Alice nearly choked on her wine, Wanda and Agatha looked at each other in disbelief, and Lilia just laughed, muttering a soft atta girl into her glass.

“Rio!” Agatha gently slapped her girlfriends knee, her cheeks flushing a light shade of red. Alice leaned over to Jen, whispering ooh you got her in trouble.

The florist looked at her girlfriend as if to say “What, am I wrong?” but also knew it was better not to say that out loud. She had scandalised the room enough, satisfied to have gotten a reaction out of everyone.

“Watch it, or you’ll find yourself sleeping in the other room tonight.”

Lilia couldn’t hide her amusement very well, and turned to Rio, “You can always stay with me if that happens.”

Rio looked at her girlfriend with a smile, placing a hand on her knee. “She wouldn’t do that, she’s very fond of me.”

The older woman took a long sip of wine. “I’m reconsidering.”

“No, you’re not.”

The certainty in Rio’s voice caught Agatha off guard slightly. Not because it was arrogant, necessarily, but because it was true. And it felt surreal to feel just how true it was. For once, there were no doubts lingering between them, no uncertainty about whether they felt the same way. For once, they were secure. Thus Agatha, despite herself, couldn’t quite hide her smile.

Jen saw that, too. She leaned back against the sofa, looking satisfied. It was nice to see that Rio was comfortable enough to flirt with Agatha in front of their friends now. The teasing was part of the happiness that she was slowly allowing herself to enjoy, not just held at arm’s length.

After they had all finished their desserts, Wanda helped carry the plates to the kitchen, offering Agatha a hand with the dishes. Everyone recognised it for what it was; an excuse for some alone time with her best friend.

Wanda took the plates to the sink, giving them a quick rinse before loading them into the dishwasher. She turned to Agatha.

“You seem happy,” she said, rather gently, quietly. “Are you? Happy, I mean.”

Agatha looked at her. It was a simple question, really. But coming from her best friend, it meant something different. She wasn’t asking whether Rio was a good girlfriend or whether Agatha was enjoying the romance. She wanted to know whether Agatha was letting herself have it. Enjoy it.

“Well, she’s apparently a bit of a menace,” Agatha joked. She tended to deflect while she reflected internally, Wanda was aware of this. There was the Agatha she knew, using humour instead answering.

Wanda chuckled. “She is.” She looked over to the living room, where Rio had moved to sit on the floor, showing Alice something on her phone. “Suits you, though,” she added pointedly.

Agatha followed her best friend’s gaze, thinking it over for a second. “Yeah, she does.”

It seemed like the Agatha Harkness had found her match. And instead of setting it aflame, she cherished it, keeping the fire rather controlled. After a moment, Agatha added, quieter, “I think I forgot how nice it is to have someone to come home to. Someone other than Nicky.”

Wanda’s expression softened and she reached for her hand, giving it a gentle squeeze. “You deserve this, Agatha. I hope you’ll remember that.”

The honesty in Wanda’s voice landed differently in Agatha’s chest. She tried to keep her emotions at bay, suddenly feeling overwhelmed. Sometimes she needed to hear that she deserved good things just to believe it. When it came to Wanda, Agatha had learned to take her word for it.

Not wanting to stay gone too long, they returned to the rest of the group, and Agatha picked up her wine glass, sitting in the far corner of her couch. Rio stood up off the floor, opting to sit on the armrest that Agatha was leaning against, her legs dangling over Agatha’s lap. Naturally, Agatha wrapped an arm around her girlfriend’s waist, caressing her gently with her thumb.

When Rio leaned down to press a quick kiss to Agatha’s temple, Agatha didn’t flinch. Didn’t glance around to see who noticed. Didn’t pretend that it hadn’t happened. Instead, she looked up at her girlfriend, smiling softly and pulling her closer into her for a moment.

Naturally, Wanda noticed. She found herself smiling, and was glad to see the same reaction on the faces of the couple across from her. The redhead felt relieved that Rio’s best friends seemed to be just as supportive as she was.

Looking at the newest couple once again, she couldn’t come up with a single reason why anyone wouldn’t want to support them, anyway. The love was practically oozing from the both of them.
